## Deferred from: code review of 4-1-lane-assignment-algorithm-lane-rs (2026-08-22)

- **`expect()` in `LaneAssigner::index` / `lane_number` becomes FFI-reachable in Story 4.6** [`crates/zanshin-graph/src/lane.rs` (`fn index(lane: u32) -> usize` / `fn lane_number(index: usize) -> u32`, called from `push` → `place`/`reserve`/`alloc_lane`)] — project-context § Rust forbids `unwrap`/`expect`/`panic!` on library paths reachable from FFI. Not reachable today (zanshin-graph has no extern surface; 4.1 is pure algorithm), and infallible in practice (`usize::try_from(u32)` cannot fail on 64-bit macOS; `u32::try_from(usize)` fails only past 4·10⁹ concurrently active lanes). Once Story 4.6's `zanshin_graph_*` wrapper calls `assign_lanes`/`push`, the rule applies literally. **Follow-up (4.6):** decide keep-with-`catch_unwind`-backstop (documented as infallible) vs. carry lanes as `usize` internally and convert once at the FFI edge; either way no behavior change on any real input. Surfaced by the Acceptance Auditor; verified low. **RESOLVED (Story 4.6, 2026-08-23): keep.** Both conversions are infallible on the supported 64-bit target (`u32`→`usize` cannot fail; `usize`→`u32` needs > 4·10⁹ concurrently open lanes), so the `usize`-internally alternative would only add a conversion at the FFI edge for no reachable failure; `catch_unwind` in `zanshin-ffi` (`internal.panic`) remains the backstop. Decision recorded as a `// WHY:` at the site (`lane.rs` `index`/`lane_number`, comment-only edit); no behavioural change.

## Deferred from: bmad-build-auto runs of Stories 9.3–9.8 (2026-09-03 / 2026-09-04)

_Six unattended story runs (restore/absorb · file reads · git sync + remote config · show/evolog/interdiff · workspace reads · bookmark create/delete), each with a four-lens review pass. Every run recorded its deferrals in the spec's `deferred:` frontmatter (`spec-9-3-…` through `spec-9-8-…`); this section lifts the ones that matter across stories — the must-haves and the low-hanging fruits — to the central ledger. **Status 2026-09-06:** eleven follow-up runs (`spec-epic-9-followup-a` … `-j`) closed 16 of the 18 bullets below; the two still open are story-owned (9.22, 9.13). The items this intro originally parked as spec-only were re-triaged under **Ralph's rule of 2026-09-06 — Zanshin ALWAYS behaves like the CLI; every on-disk / repo-side / payload-visible effect the CLI produces is required parity, only terminal UX is cosmetic:** `auto-track-bookmarks`, `absolute_git_url`, `file annotate`'s root-commit fallback and `evolog`'s per-start grouping are now tracked in the section `## Lifted 2026-09-06 under Ralph's rule …` at the end of this file. What still stays spec-only is genuinely by-design or story-owned: fileset-DSL parsing (terminal input language, ADR-0003), interactive hunk selection (9.15/9.17), MCP tools (Epic 7), the duplicate-URL / per-ref `ui.warning` texts (terminal UX — the structured facts ride the payload) and the `nameOnly` Dart witness (test-only)._

### Must-haves (cross-cutting, medium)

- ~~**No working-copy snapshot anywhere (D2): every verb reads the LAST-SNAPSHOTTED `@`, the CLI snapshots first**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-09-23 (spec-9-26-implicit-snapshot-colocated-git-import, commit fadbbfc199a40e3b67bac72acc3e4b7dd8160732): every `workspace_helper` verb runs `snapshot::snapshot_impl` first (`Repo::precondition`), `Repo::snapshot()` is `jj util snapshot`, and the on-disk-edit witnesses for restore/absorb/file reads/interdiff/workspace list are in `tests/snapshot_precondition.rs`. **MOVED 2026-09-17 → Story 9.26** (`spec-9-26-implicit-snapshot-colocated-git-import.md`, Ralph's decision in the 2026-09-17 console session: reverse D2 and port the CLI's `workspace_helper` precondition — `import git head` → `snapshot working copy` → `import git refs` in colocated repos, snapshot alone otherwise — in front of every verb the CLI snapshots for, with the CLI's `no_snapshot` exception list and the on-disk-edit witnesses for 9.3/9.4/9.7 as ACs; Story 9.22 supplies the locked-WC primitives and no longer owns this follow-up). **9.22 part LANDED 2026-09-23 (spec-9-22-file-track-untrack-locked-wc, commit c79d61952041d9e37164916472706aa761f57c7a):** `mutate::lock_working_copy` / `commit_with_locked_wc` and `wc_ops::{snapshot_options, base_ignores, auto_tracking_matcher, project_stats, merge_track_stats}` + the `SnapshotStats` payload exist; the per-verb snapshot itself stays open here for 9.26. Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/status.rs:82-89`, `revset.rs:61-62`; hits `restore`/`absorb` (9.3), all four `file` reads (9.4), `interdiff`'s `@` default (9.6), and `WorkspaceEntry.wc_commit_id` (9.7, now a COLUMN of a shipped payload)] — the CLI's `workspace_helper` snapshots on-disk edits before `restore`, `absorb`, `file *`, `workspace list`; Zanshin never does, so an edit made outside Zanshin is invisible to every verb until something else snapshots. `absorb` is hit hardest — its whole point is uncommitted changes — and the gate cannot see it because every behavioural test runs on committed commits. Pre-existing project decision, NOT a Story 9.x shortcut; `LockedWorkingCopy::{snapshot,reset}` + `SnapshotOptions` belong to **Story 9.22**. **Follow-up (9.22):** once the locked-WC lifecycle lands, add a snapshot step in front of the verbs the CLI snapshots for (and NOT for `workspace root --name`, which the CLI runs `no_snapshot`), then add the on-disk-edit witness each of 9.3/9.4/9.7 documented as missing.

- ~~**A workspace with no working-copy commit surfaces as `jj.jj_lib_error` although `jj.no_working_copy` is registered for exactly that**~~ — REJECTED 2026-10-24 (Story 9.13, spec-9-13-file-chmod-workspace-forget-rename-remove, I1): no longer applies. Commit 3ee3f29 (2026-10-23) already made `op_support::resolve_or_wc` return `ResolveError::NoWorkingCopy` with jj-lib's `RevsetResolutionError::WorkspaceMissingWorkingCopy` text — the CLI's own user error for an omitted `-r @` ("Workspace `default` doesn't have a working-copy commit") — mapped to `jj.revset_resolution_error` (mutations, commit views) and `jj.invalid_input` (file reads); `jj.no_working_copy` stays the CLI's separate "requires a working copy" refusal of lock-taking verbs. Story 9.13's `workspace_forget` makes the state reachable through the public API; witness `crates/zanshin-jj/tests/it/workspace_ops.rs::forget_the_current_workspace_then_at_defaults_refuse_with_the_clis_text`. Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/op_support.rs` (`resolve_or_wc`) → `MutateError::Backend` / `FileError::Backend` / `CommitViewError::Backend`; raised by 9.3, 9.4, 9.6 — three stories deferred it identically] — the shared helper reports the missing `@` as a `Backend("workspace has no working-copy commit")` string copied from `sync_working_copy` (Story 6.1), and `revision: null` is the default GUI call shape for a file browser. Could not be fixed inside any single story: a 9.4 AC requires the mutation verbs' observable behaviour to stay byte-identical after the lift, so it is a **crate-wide change** (new `ResolveError`/`MutateError` variant, `api_stability.rs` pin, registry annotation, `mutate.rs` + `file.rs` + `commit_views.rs` together). Unreachable through the public API until `workspace forget` (9.13). **Follow-up:** do it as one focused change before or with Story 9.13, when the state first becomes constructible.

- ~~**Request tables verify interchangeably — no `file_identifier` — and Story 9.8 is the first place the collision is DANGEROUS**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-06 (spec-epic-9-followup-h-request-kind-tag, commit `4e4e710`)** [`schemas/envelope.fbs`: `ShowRequest`/`EvologRequest`/`InterdiffRequest`/`WorkspaceRootRequest` all put one string in slot 0; `BookmarkCreateRequest` vs `BookmarkDeleteRequest`] — house-wide since 9.3 (only `root_type Envelope`, no identifier), recorded by 9.6, 9.7 and 9.8. Until 9.8 a mis-routed buffer was answered plausibly instead of refused with `ffi.invalid_argument`; with the bookmark pair the two `names` vectors have OPPOSITE semantics (create = exact decoded names, delete = glob-by-default patterns), so a create buffer fed to the delete wrapper silently glob-deletes what the caller meant literally — the FFI test `a_zero_handle_is_refused_by_both_wrappers` even does exactly that feed. Only reachable by a caller bypassing the generated Dart adapters. **Resolution:** a wrapper-side TYPE TAG, not a `file_identifier` (Ralph's call, 2026-09-06): a `file_identifier` is a 4-byte prefix `root_with_opts` only checks when the generated type declares one, it would force finish-time discipline on every producer, and it gives no room for two kinds over ONE shared table. `enum RequestKind : ubyte` (17 values) is now field **0** of all 15 request tables, and `envelope.rs::expect_request_kind` — one `pub(crate)` helper, one spelling — refuses a mismatch with `ffi.invalid_argument` and `request kind mismatch: expected <K>, got <K>` in `context`. No new error code, no new `extern "C"` symbol, no signature change: `EXPECTED_EXPORTS` stays 47.
  The tag is per-**WRAPPER**, not per-table — a deliberate strengthening of the intent's wording. Fourteen tables have one wrapper each, so their variant is the table name minus `Request`; `ChangeIdList` gets **two**, `NewChange` and `Abandon`, because a single shared variant would have left the sharpest collision (a buffer meant as `parents` abandoning those commits) unrefused — which is half of what this bullet was about.
  `Unspecified = 0` is load-bearing: FlatBuffers writes no bytes for a default-valued field, so a producer that never sets `kind` emits a buffer BYTE-IDENTICAL to a pre-tag one, which decodes as `Unspecified` and is always refused. "Legacy buffer" and "forgotten tag" are therefore the same, always-refused state.
  **The refusal ORDER changed, deliberately:** all sixteen wrappers now call their reader BEFORE `session_from_handle`, so the request-buffer rungs (null → verify → kind → required fields) precede the handle rung. A mis-routed buffer must be refused on its own merits, not only when the caller happens to hold a live handle. The only callers that see a different answer are those that get BOTH wrong at once; exactly one existing test did (`git_ffi.rs`'s add guard paired handle `0` with a buffer missing `url`) and it now uses a complete buffer for that row, so the handle rung is still witnessed.
  Witnessed by a table-driven wrong-kind test in each of the six FFI suites covering **all sixteen** wrappers — each fed a buffer built for a different verb, each also fed an untagged one, and each file carrying a `handle == 0` row that proves the kind rung runs first. The ledger's own cited evidence is now the witness: `bookmark_ops_ffi.rs::a_zero_handle_is_refused_by_both_wrappers` no longer feeds ONE create buffer to both wrappers (it builds the right kind per wrapper), and that cross-feed became `a_buffer_built_for_the_other_wrapper_is_refused_as_a_kind_mismatch`, in both directions. The `NewChange` ↔ `Abandon` cross-feed is in `repo_write_ffi.rs`. Dart round-trips the tag in `test/ffi/bookmark_ops_test.dart`, and every one of the 16 Dart builders setting it is proved by the pre-existing end-to-end suites, which would fail wholesale on a missed one.
  One `cargo make codegen`; the four committed outputs moved together. The header/bindings diff is `#define`/`const int` vtable-offset constants only (nothing consumes them) — no `extern "C"` declaration line moved. `docs/ffi-abi.md`'s "Known caveat" block is **deleted**, replaced by a `#### The RequestKind tag` section and a five-row refusal ladder; the schema's APPEND-only banner now reads "`kind` is always field 0" plus the regenerate-BOTH-sides rule, since the slot shift was only safe because both codegen sides moved in this one commit.

- ~~**`evolog` + `interdiff` do not compose: `interdiff`'s change-id currency cannot address a hidden predecessor or a commit hex**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-06 (spec-epic-9-followup-i-interdiff-revision-ref, commit `d427eee`)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/commit_views.rs::interdiff` ← `op_support::resolve_commit` (`into_visible()`)] — the CLI's `--from`/`--to` go through `resolve_single_rev`, which takes a commit hex and therefore reaches a hidden predecessor; ours takes a reverse-hex CHANGE-id prefix and only the currently-visible commit resolves. `EvologEntry.predecessor_commit_ids` are hidden COMMIT ids, so the spec's own stand-in for the unported `evolog --patch` ("compose `evolog` + `interdiff`") is inexpressible, and the story's motivating question — what differs between two versions of one change — cannot be answered through the shipped API. **Resolution:** the FIRST of the bullet's two options, scoped to ONE verb (Ralph's call, 2026-09-06): `interdiff`'s two sides became a commit-id-or-change-id UNION. `evolog` gains no `--patch`. `schemas/envelope.fbs` grows `table ChangeIdRef`, `table CommitIdRef` and `union RevisionRef`, and `InterdiffRequest.from`/`.to` change from `string` to `RevisionRef` in place (a WIP edit — one `cargo make codegen`, all four committed outputs in the same commit); `zanshin-jj` grows a `jj_lib`-type-free `pub enum RevisionRef { ChangeId(ChangeId), CommitId(CommitId) }`, threaded through `Repo::interdiff`, the FFI reader, the Dart adapter, `InterdiffCommand`, `ZanshinWorker` and `RepoHandle`. **`show`, `evolog` and every other verb keep their currency untouched**; `diff` may adopt the union when it lands. The change-id arm resolves EXACTLY as before — `op_support::resolve_commit`, `into_visible()`, divergence refused — so a commit hex offered as a `ChangeIdRef` is still `jj.change_not_found`, pinned in all three suites. The commit arm is a new `op_support::resolve_commit_id_prefix` calling `repo.index().resolve_commit_id_prefix`, which is **visibility-blind**, which is the whole mechanism. **The CLI's `IdPrefixContext` was deliberately NOT adopted:** the CLI consults `revsets.short-prefixes` first and falls back to exactly this index lookup, so the context would buy only the narrow case of a prefix ambiguous repo-wide but unique inside `revsets.log` — at the price of a config read plus a revset parse+evaluate per call, i.e. `jj.config_error` and `jj.revset_parse_error` failure modes `interdiff` does not have. It refuses instead of guessing, and the default set holds only VISIBLE commits so it would not have helped a hidden predecessor anyway. **No new error code was minted:** the commit arm's three-stage taxonomy maps onto existing ones — malformed (empty/non-hex) → `jj.invalid_input`, ambiguous prefix → `jj.revset_resolution_error` (the registry's ambiguity code, `context` built from jj-lib's own `RevsetResolutionError::AmbiguousCommitIdPrefix` so the wording cannot drift), no match → `jj.change_not_found`. No new `extern "C"` symbol and no new `RequestKind` variant either: the two ref tables are NESTED union members, not request roots, so the schema still declares 15 request tables and `EXPECTED_EXPORTS` stays 47. **One observable side effect on OTHER buffers:** the union's discriminant byte occupies the slot `from`'s string used to, so the ledger's `Show`/`Evolog`/`Interdiff`/`WorkspaceRoot` "four-way collision" is now a THREE-way one — a populated interdiff buffer and a populated one-string buffer no longer verify as each other, and cross-feeding either is refused by the Verifier at ladder rung 2 instead of by the kind rung. Two rows of `commit_views_ffi.rs`'s kind table moved to minimal tagged buffers and the divergence itself is pinned by `an_interdiff_buffer_no_longer_shares_a_layout_with_the_one_string_tables`. The motivating composition ITSELF is witnessed in the two Rust layers — `tests/commit_views.rs::interdiff_diffs_an_evolog_predecessor_against_the_current_version` and `tests/commit_views_ffi.rs::interdiff_round_trip_diffs_an_evolog_predecessor_through_the_commit_id_arm` — each rewriting a change's CONTENT through a new `test-helpers::rewrite_commit_files` (the `describe_commit` twin: a description-only rewrite leaves both versions sharing one tree, so its interdiff is empty whichever commit the resolver picked), then reading the head evolog entry's `predecessor_commit_ids[0]` back and interdiffing that HIDDEN commit against the visible version. The Dart row witnesses something narrower, because the composition is not expressible from Dart at all: **no verb reachable from Dart writes committed CONTENT**, so there is nothing to rewrite and no hidden commit to resolve. `test/worker/commit_views_test.dart::interdiff_carries_the_commit_id_arm_of_the_union_through_to_the_rust_resolver` therefore interdiffs a VISIBLE `@` hex (read back through `show`) against the absent-`@` default, and proves the thing Dart CAN prove end to end: that the union's commit arm reaches the Rust COMMIT-id resolver and is routed differently from the change arm — one string, two codes, `CommitIdRef('nothex!')` → `jj.invalid_input` versus `ChangeIdRef('nothex!')` → `jj.change_not_found`. The ambiguous-prefix fixture is DETERMINISTIC, not probabilistic: commit ids are hex, so 17 added commits must collide on their first digit by pigeonhole. The union's malformed shapes are pinned too — a half-written union (verifier, rung 2), an out-of-range discriminant (reader, `ffi.invalid_argument`) and a present arm with no `id` (reader, rung 4, `jj.invalid_input`).

- ~~**`show` is unbounded — one envelope, no limit/cursor, full tree diff + per-parent copy-record stream per entry**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-06 (spec-epic-9-followup-f-show-limit-and-interdiff-conflicts, commit `d592215`)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/commit_views.rs::show`] — `show("all()")` on `medium-1k-commits` materialises a thousand entries each paying `parent_tree`, `get_copy_records` per parent, a full `diff_stream_with_copies` and a `change_from_commit`. CLI parity (its `jj show` is unbounded too) but the CLI streams to a pager while this builds one FlatBuffers buffer; nothing bounds or measures it. `evolog` already has `limit`; `zanshin-graph` streams. **Resolution:** `ShowRequest` gains `limit`, modelled field-for-field on `EvologRequest.limit` at every layer — the presence PAIR `limit: uint; has_limit: bool;` (flatc 25.12.19 rejects optional scalars for Dart, and `limit = 0` is a MEANINGFUL empty result, so absence cannot be encoded as zero), `Option<usize>` in Rust, and a Dart adapter that SATURATES at `0xFFFFFFFF` and refuses a negative limit with `ArgumentError` on both the calling side (`ZanshinWorker.show`) and in the adapter. The **cursor half of the "or" was NOT taken**, and neither was "have the GUI cap the revset": `limit` is the whole mechanism. Absence stays the CLI-parity default (unbounded, byte-identical to the pre-change output) — `jj show` has no `-n/--limit`, so this is documented as a Zanshin-side addition at every layer, which is what keeps NFR-R-2/FR-JJ-17 satisfied. The early stop is `.take(limit.unwrap_or(usize::MAX))` on the COMMIT STREAM, before the loop body, so `parent_tree`/`copy_records_over_all_parents`/`tree_file_changes`/`change_from_commit` are never reached past the limit; the limit applies in RAW REVSET order, the same order the unbounded call returns. **"Does not compute the rest" is a structural property no behavioural test can observe** — a skipped entry leaves no trace in the result — so the perf witness this bullet asked for is `crates/zanshin-jj/benches/show_limit.rs` (`harness = false`, printing only, asserting NOTHING about wall-clock; benches are outside `cargo make test` and outside coverage, while `clippy --all-targets` still compiles them). It measures 1002 entries at **452 ms unbounded vs 345 µs at limit=1, 1.6 ms at 10, 17.5 ms at 100** — linear in the limit, so the early stop is real. Witnessed behaviourally at all three layers: `tests/commit_views.rs::show_applies_the_limit_in_raw_revset_order_and_is_unbounded_when_absent` (first-n-of-unbounded on a parent→child range, above-set-size, zero, empty revset, absent-unchanged), `tests/commit_views_ffi.rs::show_distinguishes_an_absent_limit_from_an_explicit_zero` (the presence pair across the buffer, plus that limit=1 keeps the CHILD), and `test/worker/commit_views_test.dart::show_honours_an_explicit_zero_limit_and_saturates_an_over_large_one` end-to-end through the real cdylib.

- ~~**`InterdiffView` cannot signal that the rebase behind it left CONFLICTS in the computed base tree**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-06 (spec-epic-9-followup-f-show-limit-and-interdiff-conflicts, commit `d592215`)** [`schemas/envelope.fbs::InterdiffView`; `rewrite::rebase_to_dest_parent`] — the 3-way merge can leave conflicts in the returned `MergedTree`; the payload is only `file_changes`, so a caller sees a plausible change list derived from a conflicted rebase with no way to tell (`ShowEntry` at least carries `Change.conflict`). No test constructs a conflicting rebase. The CLI renders markers in the patch text (Epic 5 surface). **Resolution:** `InterdiffView` gains `base_has_conflicts: bool` — a PRESENCE FLAG, not a `conflicts()` path list: the conflicted tree is a transient in-memory artefact a caller cannot resolve, only distrust, and a per-path channel belongs with Epic 5's hunk payload where the CLI's own signal (markers in the patch text) lands. It is derived by `MergedTree::has_conflict()`, literally the same `!…is_resolved()` predicate `Commit::has_conflict()` gives `Change.conflict` (`jj-lib-0.44.0 merged_tree.rs:211-213` / `commit.rs:167-169`), so the two payloads agree; it returns a plain `bool`, so no `jj_lib` type reaches a public signature. The flag is about the LEFT side only — the REBASED `from` tree — and is true whatever the cause: the 3-way merge left the conflict, OR the fast path (`rewrite.rs:456-460`, same parents) returned `from`'s own already-conflicted tree. It describes the TREE, not the filtered result, so a `paths` filter matching nothing still leaves it set, and it never turns the call into a refusal. Because the payload stopped being a bare list, `interdiff` now returns a `pub struct zanshin_jj::InterdiffView { file_changes, base_has_conflicts }` (the `FileSearch` pattern from follow-up E), threaded through `Repo::interdiff`, the FFI writer, a new Dart `InterdiffView` model and both worker seams. **The conflicting rebase this bullet called for is now constructed in-test** from `add_commit_with_files` alone — two parents that already differ at `f.txt`, one child of each editing it differently, so the merge's base is `from.parent_tree()` and both sides changed it; `add_conflicted_merge_off_root` could NOT stand in, because it builds a conflicted merge COMMIT rather than a conflict the rebase produces. Both arms are witnessed: `tests/commit_views.rs::interdiff_reports_a_conflicted_rebase_base_without_refusing` and `tests/commit_views_ffi.rs::interdiff_round_trip_flags_a_conflicted_rebase_base` (each also pinning the unmatched-filter case), the three existing clean rows carry the `false` pole, and the writer unit test walks both bool arms. The Dart `true` arm is decode-contract-only by necessity — no verb reachable from Dart writes committed file content (D2), the scope note that suite already carries.

- ~~**A repo initialised through a symlinked path (macOS `/var/folders` → `/private/var/folders`) records a workspace-store entry its own reads cannot resolve → the workspace's own root reports as absent**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (spec-epic-9-followup-d-symlinked-workspace-path, commits `2d6de57`..`3bb2e7e`)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::init` / `Repo::open` (Story 2.3); surfaced by 9.7's `list_workspaces`/`resolve_root`] — `SimpleWorkspaceStore::add` stores `relative_path(raw repo_path, workspace_root)` while every READ joins onto the canonicalised `Workspace::repo_path()`; the raw path is one `..` short. The real jj CLI never hits it (its loader canonicalises before either side). All three 9.7 test suites work around it with a documented `canonicalize`, so **no test pins the defect**. **Resolution:** the canonicalise lands at ALL FIVE seams that hand a path to jj-lib's `Workspace` constructors — `Repo::{open,init,clone}`, `convert_colocated` and `import_into` — because they funnel into the same `init_with_factories` → `workspace_store.add` write. All five moved together for COMPLETENESS of the fix, **not** to avoid regressing something that worked: a raw-recorded entry never resolved from EITHER base. `relative_path` counts the `..` segments purely LEXICALLY (`jj-lib-0.44.0/src/file_util.rs:136-152` — the common prefix of a raw and a canonical spelling is `/` alone, so the record is N × `..` + `private/var/…`), while `resolve_recorded_root` (`workspace.rs:130-131`) joins that onto the base and then calls `std::fs::canonicalize`, i.e. `realpath(3)`, which resolves the symlink BEFORE applying the `..` — `realpath("/var/..")` is `/private`, not `/`, so the N `..` land one level too deep and the result is `/private/private/var/…` → ENOENT. So repos created through a symlinked path by `clone` / `convert_colocated` / `convert_import` were ALREADY broken and this change FIXES them; the corollary is that canonicalising `Repo::open` can strand nothing written by an older build, because none of those entries ever resolved. `std::fs::canonicalize`, never `dunce::` (the `workspace.rs` house rule). Observable outcomes are unchanged: `Repo::open`'s `!path.exists()` guard is replaced by the canonicalise — `exists()` is an `fs::metadata` probe that follows symlinks, so it returned `false` exactly where `canonicalize` returns `Err` — and every payload (`PathNotFound`, `NotARepo`, `NotAGitRepo`, `AlreadyJjRepo`) keeps the CALLER's spelling; a missing `init`/`clone` target is still `InitFailed`/`CloneFailed`, only detected one step earlier with the unresolvable path named in `reason`. No error code, variant or registry row moved, no `.fbs` edit and no codegen. `Repo::clone`'s `created_dir` binding and its whole-dir rollback arm went with the change (provably dead once a missing target is refused up front; clone never created the dest anyway — `create_jj_dir` needs the parent, and `jj git clone`'s create-the-dest behaviour stays Story 9.20's). `test-helpers::add_workspace` is fixed at its CAUSE rather than worked around — it re-derived `<raw arg>/.jj/repo` after loading a workspace it then ignored, and now takes the repo path from that workspace's own canonical root, which is exactly what **Story 9.23's `workspace add` gets for free** from a `Repo` that is canonical end to end, closing this bullet's "re-check 9.23" ask. All three workarounds are DELETED so the 9.7 suites are the witness (`workspace.rs::add_resolvable_workspace`, `workspace_ffi.rs::open_two_workspace_scenario`'s `fs::canonicalize`, and `workspace_test.dart`'s `resolveSymbolicLinksSync` on the INPUT — its assertions still resolve both sides), plus FOUR new store-round-trip witnesses, one per WRITE seam — `tests/workspace.rs` (`init`, walking both the handle `init` returns and a re-`open`), `tests/repo.rs` (`clone`) and `tests/convert.rs` (`convert_colocated`, `convert_import`); the three beyond `init` were added by the review pass, which found that reverting those seams left the whole suite green because `Workspace::load` never reads the store. Two refusal witnesses in `tests/repo.rs` cover the new `init`/`clone` arms. **`Repo::open`'s canonicalize is forward-defence, not part of the read fix:** the read join finishes with `realpath(3)`, which resolves the symlinked prefix before the record's `..`, so a raw and a canonical base give the same answer — what `open`'s canonicalize buys is a canonical `Workspace::repo_path()` for any future writer that records an entry from an OPENED repo, which is exactly Story 9.23's `workspace add`. Mutation-probed: with `crates/{zanshin-jj,test-helpers}/src` stashed, the new witness and the three de-workaround'd tests fail. The committed fixture is untouched, so its stale-entry rows (`list` degrading, `root --name default` refusing) still hold; the ratchet RAISED `repo.rs` 96.13 → 96.46.

- ~~**A push URL can be SET (`git remote add`/`set-url`, 9.5) but never READ back — `Remote` carries only the fetch URL**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (spec-epic-9-followup-e-payload-widenings, commit 28a9aaa)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/remotes.rs` (`Remote { name, url }`, Story 2.9) + `schemas/envelope.fbs::Remotes`] — the 9.5 tests had to fall back to a raw `git config --get remote.<n>.pushurl` oracle, which is the tell; `jj git remote list` itself prints `(push: <url>)`, so `list_remotes` diverges from the CLI too. **Resolution:** `Remote` gains `push_url: Option<String>` (`.fbs` edited in place, one `cargo make codegen`, Dart model + `_mapRemote` mapped with NO `?? ''` coalesce, unlike its two siblings). It is a **DIFFERENCE test, not a raw `pushurl` read** — and it has to be: gix resolves `url(Direction::Push)` as `push_url_alias.or(push_url).or_else(|| url(Fetch))` (`gix-0.85.0/src/remote/access.rs:38-47`) and exposes neither `push_url*` field publicly, so "was a `pushurl` configured?" is unanswerable through the API Zanshin already uses. The CLI does not ask it either: `cli/src/commands/git/remote/list.rs:49-55` @ v0.44.0 reads both directions through one `get_url` and prints `(push: …)` ONLY on a difference. So a `pushurl` byte-identical to `url` reports `None` — CLI parity, and it keeps the field's meaning the one a UI wants ("this remote pushes somewhere else"). Both directions now come from one `remote_urls` helper (the generalised `fetch_url`), keeping the `to_bstring()`-not-`Display` rule that preserves credentials verbatim, and both are witnessed with credentials in the push URL. **All three `git config --get remote.<n>.pushurl` oracles are retired** — `git_remotes.rs:141`/`:413`/`:435` now assert through a new `remote_push_url` helper beside `remote_url`, and `git_ffi.rs`'s two inline `Command::new("git")` copies now read the `Remotes` payload those tests already decoded; each of the three doc comments that justified the shell-out with "`list_remotes` only reports the FETCH url" was rewritten. `git_try` and its fourth call site (`remote.origin.url`, in the test that destroys `.jj/working_copy` so no `Repo` handle loads) deliberately stay. Five new rows in `tests/remotes.rs` seed through the SHIPPED verbs (`git_remote_add(.., Some(push))`, `git_remote_set_url(.., None, Some(push))`) — distinct push URL, none, added later, equal-to-fetch, credentials — plus `sample_remote()` in `api_stability.rs`, both writer arms in the FFI unit test, and `.pushUrl` end-to-end through the real cdylib in `test/worker/git_test.dart` (whose prose caveat is gone).

- ~~**"This workspace has no git backend" maps to TWO error codes depending on the verb**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (spec-epic-9-followup-c-git-sync-must-haves, commit 824615a)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/remotes.rs::git_fetch` → `jj.remote_not_found` (Story 6.4a) vs `crates/zanshin-jj/src/git.rs` (all six 9.5 verbs) → `jj.not_a_git_repo`] — identical jj-lib `UnexpectedGitBackendError`, two documented codes; a UI cannot branch on "no git backend" with one arm. Reconciling changes a shipped verb's observable code. **Resolution:** `git_fetch`'s `GitFetch::new` arm now maps to `MutateError::NotAGitRepo` → `jj.not_a_git_repo`, so all seven git verbs answer that condition with ONE code — done before 9.20 adds a fourth spelling, as this bullet asked. The remap is exact, not a widening: `GitFetch::new`'s error type IS `UnexpectedGitBackendError` (`jj-lib-0.44.0 git.rs:3032-3047`, verified before editing), so the blanket `map_err` swallows nothing else; an unknown remote NAME is raised later by `fetch()` as `GitFetchError::NoSuchRemote` and still lands on `jj.remote_not_found`, as do the three 9.5 remote-config verbs — none of their assertions were touched. Recorded as a **deliberate observable change** on BOTH registry rows in `docs/rust-error-codes.md`, and followed through `MutateError`'s two variant docs, `envelope.rs`'s two code consts, the `extern "C"` doc and the two Dart `gitFetch` doc lists. Witnessed at both layers: `remotes_write.rs::git_fetch_on_native_backend_returns_not_a_git_repo` (renamed from `…_returns_remote_not_found`) and the new `repo_write_ffi.rs::git_fetch_on_native_backend_returns_jj_not_a_git_repo`, which also pins that `context` is ABSENT.

- ~~**`git_fetch` and `clone_into` still HARDCODE the two `GitImportOptions` booleans that `git_import` now reads from config**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (spec-epic-9-followup-c-git-sync-must-haves, commit 824615a)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/remotes.rs::git_fetch`, `crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::clone_into` vs `config::git_import_options` (9.5)] — `git.abandon-unreachable-commits` / `git.record-synthetic-predecessors` are honoured by one verb and overridden by two others in the same crate; no test asserts either behaviour at the two old sites. **Resolution:** both call sites now route through `config::git_import_options`, so all three ref-import sites read the same user setting — **the 9.20 fold-in this bullet suggested is no longer needed**. The CLI's OWN per-verb asymmetry is reproduced rather than unified: `git fetch` takes BOTH keys from config (`cli/src/commands/git/fetch.rs:235`), while `git clone` spreads the loaded options but pins `record_synthetic_predecessors: false` regardless ("There may be a large number of new commits", `cli/src/commands/git/clone.rs:384-389` @ v0.44.0). This FLIPPED fetch's default for `record-synthetic-predecessors` from the old hardcoded `false` to jj-lib's own `true` — it is not a change that "only matters if the user set a key". `remotes::git_fetch` takes `import_options` as a `pub(crate)` parameter; `Repo::git_fetch` loads it and gains a reachable `MutateError::Config` → `jj.config_error` (registry row updated); a config fault inside `clone_into` stays `RepoError::CloneFailed`, the channel every clone step already uses, rather than widening `RepoError`. `config::load_for_workspace` was split out of `load` because `clone_into` has no `Repo` yet; the six existing `load(repo)` callers are untouched. Witnesses: `remotes_write.rs::git_fetch_honours_abandon_unreachable_commits_false` (a pruning fetch after the only ref to a commit is deleted upstream) and `…_with_a_non_boolean_config_value_refuses_as_config` (against a REAL remote, so the fetch would otherwise succeed), plus the new single-test binary `tests/clone_config.rs`, which repoints `JJ_CONFIG` the way `config_user_layer.rs` does and walks both clone rows. Both new behaviours were mutation-probed and only the intended test failed. `remote_auto_track_bookmarks` deliberately stays EMPTY at all three sites — porting the CLI's `remotes.<name>.auto-track-bookmarks` parser is a separate change this run did not make, and it is not a ledger item: this section's intro parks `auto-track-bookmarks` as staying SPEC-ONLY (Epic-7/9.15/9.17/9.22-owned). **Superseded 2026-09-06:** under Ralph's always-like-the-CLI rule it IS a ledger item now — see `## Lifted 2026-09-06 …` at the end of this file.

- ~~**No `.jj/repo/config.toml` rewrite on `git remote remove`/`rename` — the CLI drops `[remotes.<name>]` and rewrites the `revset-aliases.'trunk()'` alias**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-06 (spec-epic-9-followup-j-remote-config-rewrite, commit `3eb466b`)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/git.rs::git_remote_remove` / `git_remote_rename`; CLI `cli/src/commands/git/mod.rs:195-262` @ v0.44.0] — `config.rs` was deliberately read-only and no TOML writer existed, so a `trunk()` alias pinned to a renamed/removed remote kept naming the old one and a `[remotes.<name>]` section outlived its remote. Pinned as-is by `tests/git_remotes.rs::remove_does_not_edit_the_jj_repo_config`; the 9.5 spec records that the intent's "also rewrite the repo config" was resolved against porting this (Story 2.9 had no TOML-write pattern to mirror). **Resolution:** the FIRST of the bullet's two options, on **Ralph's rule (2026-09-06) that Zanshin always behaves like the CLI — every on-disk, repo-side effect the CLI produces is required parity; only its terminal UX is cosmetic.** So both CLI helpers are ported verbatim minus their `ui.status` lines: `config.rs` gains its first WRITERS (`remove_remote_from_repo_config` / `rename_remote_in_repo_config`, plus `existing_repo_config_path` and a private `trunk_symbol` mirroring the CLI's `get_trunk_symbol`), built on jj-lib's own `toml_edit`-backed `ConfigFile`, so everything the two edits do not touch — comments, blank lines, key order, the user's own quoting — survives the rewrite byte-for-byte (pinned with a whole-file `assert_eq!`). The two edits themselves are NOT byte-preserving: `set_value` re-emits the value, so a `trunk()` the user wrote as `'main@origin'` comes back as `"main@upstream"`, which is the CLI's behaviour too and is pinned as the exact resulting line. **No general config-write API was added** — exactly the CLI's two edits, both keyed on the REMOTE name alone, and no direct `toml_edit` dependency (`data_mut()` → `get_mut("remotes")` → `as_table_like_mut()` reaches everything by method call; `as_table_like_mut` deliberately, so the inline `remotes = { origin = … }` spelling is edited too). **The path is resolved by the same `secure_config_path` the READ side uses** (`<config_dir>/jj/repos/<config-id>/config.toml`, legacy `.jj/repo/config.toml` as the fallback), filtered by existence — so read and write address one file by construction, and with no repo-level file the rewrite is a no-op that **creates nothing**, mirroring the CLI's `existing_repo_config_file`. `config.rs`'s no-side-effect-on-READ stance is unchanged and `SecureConfig` is still not adopted. **CLI ordering is reproduced exactly:** the file is resolved AND LOADED before the transaction opens, mirroring the CLI's `existing_repo_config_file`, which wraps a repo layer parsed at process start — so EVERY read-side fault (a malformed `config-id`, an unreadable file, invalid TOML) refuses with **nothing mutated at all**, git config included, exactly as the CLI fails before `git::remove_remote` runs. Only the loaded `ConfigFile` is moved INTO `mutate::finish_or_no_op`'s closure, where it is written after `git::remove_remote`/`rename_remote` and before the conditional commit — so the rewrite happens even when NO operation is written (the reserved remote `git`, pinned with a repo config present in both reserved-remote rows), and only a `save()` failure drops the transaction, while jj-lib's own git-config edit stands. That last asymmetry is the CLI's `?` and is mirrored, not corrected; the facades' "an `Err` does not mean nothing happened" paragraph now names both orderings. A rewrite failure is `MutateError::Config` → **`jj.config_error`, no new code minted** — the two remote verbs become the registry's first emitters that also WRITE config, recorded on the `jj.config_error` row and added to both `# Errors` blocks, both `docs/ffi-abi.md` rows and both Dart doc lists (`ffi_abi_doc_sync.rs` enforces the rustdoc↔`docs/ffi-abi.md` agreement only — nothing machine-checks the Dart doc lists, which stay a manual follow-through). No `.fbs` edit and no new `extern "C"` symbol; one `cargo make codegen`, whose diff is doc-comment hunks only. Error mapping goes through ONE generic `write_error<E>` rather than per-call-site closures, because `set_value`/`delete_value` can only fail on a TABLE target and both call sites are guarded by `as_str()` — sharing one body keeps `config.rs` at **100.00 %** with no unreachable lines. Witnessed at three layers: eleven new inline rows in `src/config.rs` for the file-shape matrix (absent file, absent/non-table `remotes`, inline table, matching/other/compound/unparseable/non-string alias, comment preservation, unparseable TOML, unwritable file), nine in `tests/git_remotes.rs` for the rows that need a repo (plus the two reserved-remote-`git` rows extended to carry a repo config, so the rewrite's placement before the `has_changes()` guard is what they now pin) — `remove_does_not_edit_the_jj_repo_config` **inverted** into `remove_rewrites_the_jj_repo_config_and_the_builtin_trunk_applies_again`, plus the rename twin, the survivors, the create-nothing row, the canonical `<XDG>/jj/repos/<config-id>/config.toml` layout (every other row reaches the file through the LEGACY `.jj/repo/config.toml` fallback, so without this one the resolver's `repos` join is unpinned — the suite therefore also pins `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` to a process-lifetime temp dir), and the two refusal orderings (a read fault — malformed `config-id`, unparseable TOML, for `remove` and `rename` alike — mutating nothing at all; an unwritable file aborting the transaction while the git-config edit stands) — and three in `tests/git_ffi.rs` (the rewrite visible through the FFI path, and `jj.config_error` crossing the boundary for `remove` and for `rename`, whose `set_value` arm is not `remove`'s `delete_value`). The **same-session read-back** is the assertion that proves read and write agree: after remove, `Repo::eval_revset("trunk()")` on the SAME handle resolves through the builtin alias; after rename it refuses naming `upstream`, not `origin`. **Not ported:** the two `ui.status` lines (terminal UX — the effect they announce IS ported), and `remotes.<name>.auto-track-bookmarks`, which stays its own parked item. `git remote add`/`set-url` are untouched: the CLI writes no repo config for those. **Ralph's rule binds forward, not only backward:** every on-disk, repo-side effect the CLI produces is REQUIRED parity, so no future story may ship a verb that skips one and record it as a deliberate divergence — the only thing Zanshin replaces is the terminal UX. Made binding where a future story will read it, as the anti-pattern bullet "**NEVER diverge from the jj CLI's observable behavior**" in `_bmad-output/project-context.md`.

### Low-hanging fruits

- ~~**`docs/ffi-abi.md` is now SEVEN stories stale (since Story 4.9)**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-06 (spec-epic-9-followup-g-ffi-abi-doc-pass, commit `33edc00`)** [`docs/ffi-abi.md`] — documents none of Story 6.5's six mutation wrappers, none of Epic 9's (`zanshin_restore`, `_absorb`, `_file_list/_show/_annotate/_search`, `_git_import/_export/_remote_*`, `_show`, `_evolog`, `_interdiff`, `_workspace_list/_root`, `_bookmark_create/_delete`), nor the composite request-buffer pattern they all share, nor the `AbsorbSkippedPath` → `SkippedPath` rename. Every 9.x story carried it forward. **Resolution:** one doc pass, exactly as prescribed — a new `## Request-buffer contract` section documents the convention ONCE (how Dart builds the table, the AR-P-5 INBOUND ownership rule as the REVERSE of the outbound token rule, the three-layer refusal ladder `ffi.null_pointer`-with-no-context → `ffi.invalid_argument`-with-the-verifier-message → `jj.invalid_input`-for-an-absent-required-field, the deliberate non-collapse of an empty buffer to `nullptr`, the interior-NUL rule and its two enforcement layers, the 15 request tables with `ChangeIdList` as the only shared one, the append-only field-order contract, and a pointer to the no-`file_identifier` must-have at `:194`), and a new `## Symbol index` carries one row per exported symbol — **all 47, no allow-list** — with `| Symbol | Args | Request table | Success payload | Error codes | Story |`. Every row is DERIVED: signature from `crates/zanshin-ffi/include/zanshin_ffi.h`, request/payload from `schemas/envelope.fbs`, error codes verbatim from that symbol's `# Errors` block, cross-checked against `docs/rust-error-codes.md` — a script comparison confirms all 47 rows match their header block exactly (no extra, none missing) and all 31 named codes are registered. Thirteen falsified statements were corrected in place (the infallible list named 4 of 8 and "infallible ⇒ no envelope" was contradicted by `zanshin_diagnostics`; `zanshin_repo_convert`'s error table; the `_onNativeEvent` sample and the event-demux lookup site; "the raw handle is used only for close + read wrappers", false since 6.5; "the only file allowed to import `dart:ffi`", contradicted by fourteen files — the real invariant is that `dart:ffi` never reaches `lib/src/worker/`; three `v0.42.0` anchors for a pin that moved to `=0.44.0`; the missing `ui.conflict-marker-style` builtin; the `cli_util.rs:991-995` citation; `syntheticElided = true` vs `defaultSyntheticElided`). **Two of the bullet's own premises turned out to be non-issues and are recorded as findings:** the `AbsorbSkippedPath` → `SkippedPath` rename is invisible to this document — neither name ever appeared in `docs/` — so the gap was COVERAGE (a row plus the `AbsorbResult` payload), not a stale name; and the `session_id` correlation section was verified ACCURATE and left alone. **Two `# Errors` rustdoc gaps the cross-check proved** were fixed at the source and regenerated (comment-only, one `cargo make codegen`, no declaration line moved, no `.fbs` edit): `watcher.io_error` was documented on NO symbol although `arm_session` returns it from `zanshin_repo_open`/`_init`/`_clone`/`_convert` and the registry row at `:52` names exactly those four, and `jj.config_error` was missing from `zanshin_graph_slice` although `graph.rs:46-47` maps it and `:749-752` asserts it. The guard against re-staling is `crates/zanshin-ffi/tests/ffi_abi_doc_sync.rs`: two pure `&str` scanners (`exported_symbols` / `documented_symbols`) plus a path-naming reader, asserting the header's export set is a SUBSET of the doc's covered set and naming every miss, sorted, in one message — no allow-list, no exception set, no `#[ignore]`, and it passed with an empty exception set on the first green run. Seven further tests drive the scanners over inline fixtures (a symbol named only in a header comment, a wrapped multi-line declaration, `ZanshinBuf`/`ZANSHIN_FFI_H`, a name in a non-first table cell, a heading, a missing row, an unreadable path), and the negative probe — deleting `zanshin_undo`'s row — fails with `1 of 47 symbols … zanshin_undo`. The check is deliberately ONE-directional (a doc row for a symbol the header no longer exports is not caught); recorded as a scope choice, not an oversight. `coverage-baseline.txt` did not move (a file under `tests/` is excluded by `Makefile.toml:519`'s `--ignore-filename-regex`) and coverage held at 99.22%.

- ~~**`session_from_handle` duplicated byte-for-byte in four `zanshin-ffi` modules**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (spec-epic-9-followup-a-dedupe-ffi-helpers, commit a64d1c5)** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/src/{git,commit_views,workspace,bookmark_ops}.rs`, each with the unbounded-lifetime SAFETY comment that must stay in sync] — 9.8's own rationale for moving `finish_or_no_op` into `mutate.rs` applies unchanged. **Resolution:** one `pub(crate) unsafe fn session_from_handle` in `envelope.rs` under its own `// ── Shared handle borrow ──` banner (it returns a BORROW, so the module doc's "every pub function returns a finished, owned `Vec<u8>`" is now scoped to the envelope builders); the four local copies and their `handle_from_raw`/`RepoSession`/`FFI_INVALID_HANDLE` imports are gone, all 13 call sites unchanged.

- ~~**Six copies of the Dart request-buffer helpers**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (spec-epic-9-followup-a-dedupe-ffi-helpers, commit a64d1c5)** [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/ffi/{mutate,git,file,commit_views,workspace,bookmark_ops}.dart` — `_withRequestBytes`, `_readToken`, the interior-NUL guard] — the exact duplication the Rust half of 9.8 refactored away, left standing on the Dart half. **Resolution:** new `ffi/request_bytes.dart` holds `withRequestBytes`, `rejectInteriorNul`, `readToken` and `callWithRequest` (the last was triplicated too — `git.dart` composed, `file.dart`/`commit_views.dart` with the token-read inlined) with noun-agnostic docs; `_withChangeIdListBytes` and `_rejectPathNuls` stay local and delegate. The three pre-existing INLINE NUL `throw`s in `mutate.dart`'s C-string adapters were deliberately left alone (not copies of the helper).

- ~~**`isolate.dart` holds ~a dozen verbatim copies of the synthesized `ffi.invalid_handle` envelope**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (spec-epic-9-followup-a-dedupe-ffi-helpers, commit a64d1c5)** [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/isolate.dart`, one ~12-line `fbs.EnvelopeObjectBuilder` per worker switch arm] — the count was **33**, not ~a dozen; extracting `_invalidHandleEnvelope(handleId)` removed ~230 lines. **Resolution:** one top-level private helper before `_replyRepoOp`, returning the identical envelope (`ok: false`, `code: 'ffi.invalid_handle'`, `context: 'unknown or closed handleId: $handleId'`); each arm keeps its own per-arm `log.warning`, and the `internal.dispatch_error` envelope is untouched.

- ~~**The "absent revision" doc ambiguity fixed on the 9.8 surfaces survives on the 9.3/9.4/9.6 ones**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (spec-epic-9-followup-b-doc-and-test-low-hanging-fruits, commit b289a3e)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/file.rs` (`revision` doc) and the sibling `restore`/`absorb`/`interdiff` docs it was copied from] — a `# Errors` bullet says a revision that is "malformed, absent, only-hidden, or divergent" refuses, in a paragraph where ABSENT means "apply the CLI's default". Comment-only. **Resolution:** 9.8's wording copied to **14** sites, not three — the identical sentence exists at three layers per verb, so `file_list`/`interdiff`/`restore`/`absorb` plus `FileError`'s and `CommitViewError`'s variant docs were fixed in the module, the `Repo` facade AND the `extern "C"` wrapper (the facade is the rustdoc a consumer actually reads). Each site keeps its own argument names, trailing clauses and CLI flag. `MutateError::ChangeNotFound`'s variant doc and the twelve `describe`/`new_change`/`edit`/`abandon` bullets are deliberately untouched: those ids are REQUIRED, so no omitted-means-default reading exists there. `zanshin_ffi.h` + `bindings.dart` regenerated via `cargo make codegen`; no `.fbs` edited, no other generated output moved.

- ~~**`MutationResult`'s other three fields are asserted nowhere for either bookmark verb**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (spec-epic-9-followup-b-doc-and-test-low-hanging-fruits, commit 054d87d)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/tests/bookmark_ops.rs` + FFI/Dart siblings — only `new_op_id` is checked] — `new_change_ids` / `abandoned_change_ids` (must be empty) and `working_copy_change_id` (from `sync_working_copy`) would ship a regression green. **Resolution:** one assertion helper per layer (`assert_no_change_churn` in the two Rust suites, `expectNoChangeChurn` in the Dart one), called on the create happy path, the delete happy path and delete's NO-OP path — `finish_or_no_op_at` has two exits, and while the no-op one was already covered for `new_op_id` by the pre-existing `delete_matching_nothing_*` tests, the other three fields were witnessed on neither exit; they are supposed to be identical on both. Each oracle is read PRE-CALL, so a regression that moved `@` cannot shift both sides of the comparison together. The `working_copy_change_id` oracle is what `status` reports on each layer; at the FFI layer that read was factored out of `wc_commit_hex` into `wc_change_id`, and both vectors are asserted PRESENT-but-empty because the builder always writes `Some(vec)`.

- ~~**`has_tracked_remote_bookmarks`'s non-git arm is executed but never asserted**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (spec-epic-9-followup-b-doc-and-test-low-hanging-fruits, commit 054d87d)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/bookmark_ops.rs`] — on a git backend the matcher excludes the reserved remote `git`; on a native backend it is `StringMatcher::all()`, so a tracked `name@git` DOES block `create` there. Every Rust test runs on the git-backed `tiny-3-commits`; the Dart worker tests run native but do not assert the difference. **Resolution:** `create_is_blocked_by_a_tracked_git_remote_on_a_native_backend` in `tests/bookmark_ops.rs`, placed directly after its git-backed twin and commenting WHY the two differ — `Repo::init(TempDir, None)`, a tracked `colocated@git` seeded in-test via `set_remote_bookmark`, then `BookmarkTrackedRemoteExists` AND an unchanged head operation. The ledger's stated semantics were confirmed, not corrected. Mutation-probed: flipping the `else` arm to the git matcher fails that test and only that test. The test also falsified five doc statements that asserted UNCONDITIONALLY that a colocated `name@git` never blocks/never triggers the refusal; all five were qualified as git-backend-specific in the same run — `Repo::bookmark_create`'s facade doc, `has_tracked_remote_bookmarks`'s own doc, `MutateError::BookmarkTrackedRemoteExists`, `envelope.rs::JJ_BOOKMARK_TRACKED_REMOTE_EXISTS` and the `docs/rust-error-codes.md` registry row. So this item was NOT purely test-only: it touched four production files, all comment-only.

- ~~**`FileSearch` carries no `skipped` channel, so a GUI cannot tell "no match" from "blob unreadable"**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (spec-epic-9-followup-e-payload-widenings, commit 28a9aaa)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/file.rs::file_search` + `schemas/envelope.fbs::FileSearch`] — `MaterializedTreeValue::AccessDenied` is silently dropped where the CLI prints "Skipping '…' due to permission error"; `FileContents` already has the `skipped` vector for exactly this. **Resolution:** `file_search` now returns a `FileSearch { matches, skipped }` struct mirroring `FileContents`, reusing `SkippedPath` (whose doc gains a third carrier) rather than minting a second type; the change goes through the `Repo` facade, the `extern "C"` doc, the `.fbs` (vtable slot 6, same as `FileContents`), the Dart model and both worker seams. **`skipped` carries ONLY `AccessDenied`** — `jj file search` warns about nothing else (`cli/src/commands/file/search.rs:109-115`), and Symlink/GitSubmodule/OtherConflict are skipped SILENTLY there (`:134`, `:159-160`), the exact opposite of `jj file show`; the reason string is search's OWN sentence (`Skipping '<path>' due to permission error: {e}`), not `render_entry`'s show wording, so the two verbs' contracts stay distinct. The witness is **inline in `src/file.rs`**, not in `tests/`: `MaterializedTreeValue::AccessDenied` comes from exactly one jj-lib error (`BackendError::ReadAccessDenied`), raised by exactly one backend — the `testing`-gated `SecretBackend`, which refuses any path containing `secret` — and `Repo::open` hardcodes `default_backend_factories()`, which this change deliberately does NOT widen, so the test builds the `Workspace` itself with an `add_backend(SecretBackend::name(), …)` factory and calls the `pub(crate)` `file_search` directly. That also keeps the new arm covered. `jj-lib = { workspace = true, features = ["testing"] }` went into `zanshin-jj`'s `[dev-dependencies]` (dev-only, so `cargo build` and the shipped cdylib never enable it; `[dependencies]` or `test-helpers` would have enabled it for `cargo build --workspace`). The eight existing `file_search` rows moved to the new return type, and the symlink row's silent-skip claim plus the no-match row are now CHECKABLE (`skipped.is_empty()`) — that assertion is what makes "nothing matched" distinguishable from "a blob was unreadable". No fixture was regenerated.

Semantically identical to the extracted helper (same code, `context: None`), so nothing is mishandled — but the follow-up that extracted the four NAMED copies was scoped to those four by its intent. **Resolution:** the count was exactly **19**; all now call `envelope::session_from_handle` behind `git.rs`'s existing one-line `// SAFETY: see \`session_from_handle\`.` pointer, with the three-line per-site duplicates and the orphaned `handle_from_raw`/`RepoSession`/`FFI_INVALID_HANDLE` imports gone. `zanshin_repo_close`'s `if let Some` + `handle_drop` close path is the one deliberate non-user (different shape, own close-ordering SAFETY comment); the `── Shared handle borrow ──` banner, which recorded the nine modules as still inline, now records the opposite.

- ~~**`readToken`'s body is still inlined ~23 times in the Dart adapters that do NOT pass a request buffer**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (spec-epic-9-followup-a2-finish-ffi-helper-dedupe, commit 53fc8f6)** [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/ffi/{status,bookmarks,op_log,remotes,log_defaults,change,diagnostics,events,graph,repo}.dart`, plus four blocks inside `mutate.dart`] — `FfiBuffer.fromToken(token)` / `Uint8List.fromList(buf.bytes)` / `buf.dispose()` verbatim. The 2026-09-05 extraction covered the six request-buffer files only, so a change to the token ownership discipline would still have to be made in a dozen places. **Resolution:** all 23 re-pointed at `ffi/request_bytes.dart`'s `readToken` (the `mutate.dart` count was **8**, not four; the ~23 total was right). `FfiBuffer.fromToken` now has exactly one caller. `diagnostics.dart` and `events.dart` decoded INSIDE the `try`; they now decode the `Uint8List.fromList` copy after disposal — same value, shorter native lifetime. ~167 net lines gone.

- ~~**`isolate.dart`'s `_rejectInteriorNuls` hand-rolls the guard that is now shared**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (spec-epic-9-followup-a2-finish-ffi-helper-dedupe, commit 53fc8f6)** [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/isolate.dart` (`ZanshinWorker._rejectInteriorNuls`)] — it repeats `value.codeUnits.contains(0)` plus the literal `ArgumentError.value(value, name, 'must not contain an interior NUL (U+0000)')` instead of delegating to `ffi/request_bytes.dart`'s `rejectInteriorNul`, so the message this run just single-sourced still has a second spelling one layer up. The run that recorded this deliberately left it alone as the calling-side guard rather than one of the six adapter copies its item named — a scope call the follow-up reversed, since the grievance was the second spelling itself. **Resolution:** `_rejectInteriorNuls` deleted; a new `rejectInteriorNuls(Iterable<String>, String)` list helper sits next to the scalar form and replaced all six hand-written `for` loops. The five remaining hand-rolled throws also delegate now — two in `isolate.dart` (`workspaceRoot`'s `name`, `bookmarkCreate`'s `revision`) and **three the spec's map missed** in `graph.dart` (`revset`, `prioritize`, `shortPrefixes`), all non-null so the scalar helper's null-guard is a no-op. `codeUnits.contains(0)` and the literal message each have exactly one hit in `lib/`, both inside `rejectInteriorNul`; a new witness pins the list form's message, `name` and offending-entry `invalidValue`.

- ~~**Ten worker command arms have no closed-handle test**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (spec-epic-9-followup-a2-finish-ffi-helper-dedupe, commit 53fc8f6)** [`packages/zanshin_core/test/worker/` — `Describe`, `NewChange`, `Edit`, `Abandon`, `GitFetch`, `GitImport`, `GitRemoteAdd`, `GitRemoteRemove`, `GitRemoteRename`, `GitRemoteSetUrl`] — 23 of the 33 arms have one; for the other ten the unknown-handle reply is exercised by nothing, so their dispatch-arm wiring rests on textual uniformity alone. Surfaced when the 33 inline envelopes were collapsed into one helper (which reduces, but does not remove, the risk). **Resolution:** ten tests added, modelled verbatim on `test/worker/file_test.dart`'s four — the five mutation arms in `mutate_test.dart`, the five `git` arms in `git_test.dart` beside the existing `gitExport` one. All 33 arms now have a witness. `gitFetch` spawns no `git` subprocess here: the worker's own handle-table lookup refuses before any FFI call, so `mutate_test.dart`'s no-happy-path-fetch caveat does not apply.

## Deferred from: spec-epic-9-followup-c-git-sync-must-haves (2026-09-05)

_The git-sync run closed the two Story 9.5 must-haves above (`jj.not_a_git_repo` for "no git backend" on `git_fetch`; config-driven `GitImportOptions` at `git_fetch` + `clone_into`) and, as a reviewed scope extension, fixed a pre-existing panic on any pruning `git_fetch` under the default config. Its review pass left exactly one pre-existing item open. **That item was closed by `spec-epic-9-followup-e-payload-widenings` (2026-09-05), which rode with the `Remote.push_url` widening in the same module as planned; this section is fully resolved and needs no next pass.**_

- ~~**`git_fetch`'s `MutationResult` is an EMPTY projection — `new_change_ids` / `abandoned_change_ids` hardcoded `Vec::new()`**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (spec-epic-9-followup-e-payload-widenings, commit 28a9aaa)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/remotes.rs` (the `MutationResult` literal at the end of `git_fetch`), since Story 6.4a] — the sibling verb `git::git_import` projects real stats from the same `import_refs` call, but fetch reports nothing, so the abandoning that `git.abandon-unreachable-commits` now lets a user control (and that the new descendant-rebase pass makes reach the op log) is invisible in the returned result; the follow-up-C pruning tests had to observe it through `resolve_revset(&repo, "all()")` instead. Pre-existing, not one of the two bullets that run closed, and a shipped-payload change that wants its own witnesses. **Resolution:** `git_fetch` now binds the `GitImportStats` `import_refs()` already returned (and threw away one line later) and fills `abandoned_change_ids` from it. **This bullet's premise about the sibling was wrong and the fix follows the bullet's own prescription instead:** `git_import` does NOT project into `MutationResult` either — it leaves both vectors empty (`git.rs:103-106`) and puts the abandonments in its own `GitImportResult.abandoned_change_ids`. Fetch has no second result struct, so the stats land in `MutationResult.abandoned_change_ids`, which **widens that field's documented meaning for this one verb**: everywhere else it is the ids `abandon`'s caller echoed back, here it is jj-lib's OWN abandonments. Both call sites go through one `pub(crate) git::project_abandoned(&GitImportStats)` helper so the two verbs cannot drift. `new_change_ids` stays `Vec::new()` with a WHY: `GitImportStats` has no minted-change concept at all (it reports abandonments, changed remote refs, rewritten commit ids, failed ref names), and a replacement `@` for an abandoned one is already reported through `working_copy_change_id`. Every doc that claimed both vectors are always empty for `git_fetch` was corrected at all four layers — `MutationResult`'s two Rust field docs, the `Repo::git_fetch` facade, the `extern "C"` wrapper (so `zanshin_ffi.h`/`bindings.dart` moved, doc-only) and the Dart field docs + `RepoHandle.gitFetch`. Witnessed on the follow-up-C prune fixture at both poles: the default-config row asserts the pruned change IS reported and `new_change_ids` is empty, and the `abandon-unreachable-commits = false` sibling is the negative control (empty list) — a hardcoded `Vec::new()` passes the second and fails the first, a hardcoded echo does the reverse, so the pair proves the projection reads the stats.

## Deferred from: spec-epic-9-followup-d-symlinked-workspace-path (2026-09-05)

_The canonicalise run closed the symlinked-workspace-path must-have above (commits `2d6de57`..`3bb2e7e`) and recorded exactly one deferral of its own. That run deliberately kept it SPEC-ONLY, citing the 9.3–9.8 section's own convention for story-owned items ("stays spec-only: by-design, Epic-7/9.15/9.17/9.22-owned"); lifting it here overrides that call so the central ledger is complete, and the item stays tracked by Story 9.20 either way._

- ~~**`Repo::init` and `Repo::clone` refuse a destination that does not exist yet, where the jj CLI creates it; `Repo::clone` also accepts a non-empty destination, where the CLI refuses**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-10-20 (spec-9-20-git-push-clone-init, commit 9066268):** `init` creates a missing destination (`create_or_reuse_dir`), `clone` creates it with its parents and refuses a file or a non-empty directory with `jj.destination_not_empty` before anything is created (`crates/zanshin-jj/tests/clone_init.rs`, oracle `init_missing_destination`, `clone_non_empty_destination`). [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::init`, `crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::clone`] — NFR-R-2 / ADR-0003 make the CLI's observable behavior the semantic reference. At tag v0.44.0 `cli/src/commands/git/init.rs` calls `file_util::create_or_reuse_dir(&wc_path)` and `cli/src/commands/git/clone.rs` calls `fs::create_dir_all(&wc_path)` before handing the path to jj-lib, and clone refuses a destination that already has content. Zanshin does neither: jj-lib's `create_jj_dir` (`jj-lib-0.44.0/src/workspace.rs:121-130`) uses `create_dir`, not `create_dir_all`, so it never created the workspace root either — the refusal PREDATES follow-up D, which only moved WHERE it is detected (the canonicalise instead of inside the init) and pinned it with two tests. Severity medium. **Follow-up (Story 9.20** — `git push` / `git clone` / `git init`, which ports those two CLI verbs with full parity**):** follow-up D's review pass added a pointer to 9.20 in `Repo::init`'s and `Repo::clone`'s doc blocks, so the divergence is discoverable at the code a 9.20 implementer will edit rather than only on this list.

## Deferred from: spec-epic-9-followup-j-remote-config-rewrite (2026-09-06)

_The repo-config-rewrite run closed the last must-have of the 9.3–9.8 git-sync group (commits `3eb466b`..`def28bb`). Its review pass surfaced exactly one pre-existing item — the third member of the very CLI helper family it ported, sitting on a different verb — which Ralph's rule promotes from "unrecorded silence" to a tracked must-have._

- ~~**`Repo::clone_into` writes none of the repo-level presets the CLI's `jj git clone` writes, so a Zanshin-cloned repo has a different `trunk()` default than a CLI-cloned one**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-10-20 (spec-9-20-git-push-clone-init, commit 9066268):** `config::write_repo_presets` writes the fetch patterns and `trunk()` into the secure repo config like `write_repo_presets` @ v0.46.0; witnessed by a `trunk()` read-back in `tests/clone_init.rs` and the oracle's `jj config list --repo` read-back on every clone scenario. [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::clone_into`; CLI `cli/src/commands/git/clone.rs:289-296` → `write_repo_presets` (`cli/src/commands/git/mod.rs:151-190`) @ v0.44.0] — `write_repo_presets` is the third member of the same helper family as the two ported by this run, writing `[remotes.<name>].fetch-bookmarks`/`fetch-tags` and `revset-aliases.'trunk()' = <default-branch>@<remote>` into the repo-level file through the same `ConfigFile` API; `working_branch` falls back to the remote's default branch, so an ordinary `jj git clone` really does write a `trunk()` preset. `grep write_repo_presets` over `crates/`, `docs/` and `_bmad-output/` has zero hits, so the omission was neither tested nor recorded. **Not caused by this run** — but the run makes it legible twice over: the two remote verbs now MAINTAIN presets Zanshin itself never creates (the "the rewrite finds what the clone wrote" round trip is never closed, every test hand-writes the file), and **Ralph's rule (2026-09-06) that every on-disk, repo-side effect the CLI produces is required parity** turns it from an accepted silence into a must-have. Severity medium. **Follow-up (Story 9.20** — `git push` / `git clone` / `git init`, the story that ports those CLI verbs with full parity**):** the writer pattern now exists in `config.rs`, so this is a third helper next to the two, not new machinery — plus a `Repo::eval_revset("trunk()")` read-back after a clone as its witness.

## Deferred from: spec-epic-9-followup-e-payload-widenings (2026-09-05)

_The payload-widening run closed all three of its targeted items — `Remote.push_url` (9.5), `FileSearch.skipped` (9.4) and `git_fetch`'s import-stat projection (follow-up C) — and its review pass surfaced exactly one pre-existing item, which needs a product decision rather than an edit._

- **A remote configured with only a `pushurl` and no `url` is dropped from `list_remotes` entirely, where `jj git remote list` still prints it** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/remotes.rs::remote_urls` / `list_remotes`, since Story 2.9] — the helper ends its fetch read with `let fetch = render(Direction::Fetch)?;`, so a `[remote "x"]` section carrying a `pushurl` but no `url` yields `None` and the build loop skips the row, even though gix resolves `url(Direction::Push)` for it fine. The CLI does the opposite: its `get_url` returns an `Option` and the row is printed with a `"<no URL>"` sentinel standing in for the missing direction (`cli/src/commands/git/remote/list.rs:60-65` @ v0.44.0) — a citation follow-up E's own Code Map already carried as read evidence. **Not caused or worsened by the `push_url` widening** — such a remote was invisible before too; the widening merely made the divergence legible, since the one URL that remote HAS is now the field Zanshin models. A CLI-parity gap under NFR-R-2, exotic in practice (git itself treats a `url`-less remote as unfetchable). **Follow-up:** decide what a row with no fetch URL reports. `Remote.url` is a non-optional `String` at both the Rust and the wire layer, so this is not a mechanical edit — it is either a sentinel value (matching the CLI's rendered text, but inventing data where the codebase's house rule is null-for-absence) or a fourth payload widening to `Option<String>`, and that choice belongs with the UI that would render it.

## Lifted 2026-09-06 under Ralph's rule "Zanshin always behaves like the CLI"

_Ralph's decision of 2026-09-06 (recorded as the anti-pattern "NEVER diverge from the jj CLI's observable behavior" in `project-context.md`): every on-disk, repo-side or payload-visible effect the jj CLI produces is REQUIRED parity; only its terminal UX (stdout/`ui.status`/`ui.warning` wording, pager, colours, prompts, `$EDITOR`) is cosmetic. A CLI side effect a story cannot port yet is a ledger defect to close, never an accepted divergence. Applying that rule to the items earlier sections had parked as "cosmetic" or "spec-only" yields the entries below. None of them is new code debt — each was a documented, deliberate skip under the OLD reading._

- ~~**`convert_colocated` (2.12) and `convert_import` (2.13) skip the repo-level side effects of the CLI's `jj git init` External branch: the `trunk()` alias and the `.gitignore` seeding**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-06 (spec-epic-9-followup-k-convert-cli-parity, commit `2590d7f`): both verbs now run `do_init`'s `GitInitMode::External` arm step for step — `import git refs` (with `git::export_refs` when colocated), `.jj/.gitignore`, `import git head`, then the `trunk()` alias — each operation written only when its import reports `has_changes()`, so a source with no refs writes neither. The alias goes through jj-lib's own `SecureConfig::load_config` (`config.rs::create_repo_config_file` / `new_repo_config_file` / `set_trunk_alias`, the writer family's third member and the first that CREATES a file), so it lands exactly where `jj git init` puts it — `<config_dir>/jj/repos/<config-id>/config.toml` plus `metadata.binpb` and the atomic `.jj/repo/config-id` — not in the legacy `.jj/repo/config.toml` the CLI would later migrate with a warning. Two of this bullet's own citations were STALE and the oracle won: at v0.44.0 `maybe_set_repository_level_trunk_alias` has NO `get_single_remote` guard (it tries `refs/remotes/upstream/HEAD` then `…/origin/HEAD`, stops at the first remote that HAS such a reference whether or not it parses, and writes only when the symbolic target parses as a `GitRefKind::Bookmark`), and `maybe_add_gitignore` does not seed `.jj/` into the workspace's `.gitignore` — it writes the literal `/*` to `<root>/.jj/.gitignore`, colocated only. **The one half left to Story 9.22** is the working-copy `reset` the CLI pairs with its COLOCATED head import (`cli_util.rs::import_git_head`'s `check_out` + `locked_wc().reset(&wc_commit)`): it needs `start_working_copy_mutation`, the locked-working-copy lifecycle 9.22 owns, and reversing project decision D2 (Zanshin never snapshots) was not this run's call — so after a Zanshin colocated conversion the op log matches the CLI's but `@` has not been re-parented onto the imported HEAD; it settles there on the first snapshot, exactly as 2.12's D3a documents. `convert_import`'s non-colocated `check_out` + materialisation is the CLI's own non-colocated branch and was already there; only its description and its `has_changes()` guard changed. `remote_auto_track_bookmarks` stays empty — that is the separate ledger bullet below, unchanged. Failure handling is unchanged (`RepoError::ConvertFailed`, rollback of `.jj/` only, no new error code), which is stricter than the CLI and can orphan the secure-config directory the alias step created; both are now stated in the rustdoc at every layer.** Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs`; CLI `cli/src/commands/git/init.rs::do_init` @ v0.44.0, `GitInitMode::External` arm (both `--colocate` over an existing `.git` and `--git-repo=<path>` land there) → `maybe_add_gitignore(&workspace_command)` and `maybe_set_repository_level_trunk_alias(ui, &git_repo, &config_env)` (`cli/src/commands/git/init.rs:253-259`, the alias writer at `:324`)] — Story 2.13's Decision D10 classed both as "CLI-UX/convenience, not repo-state behavior" and 2.12 inherited the same skip; under the new rule both are on-disk effects and therefore parity gaps: a CLI-converted repo gets `revset-aliases.'trunk()' = <default>@<single remote>` written into its repo config (when exactly one remote exists) and a `.jj/` line seeded into `.gitignore`, a Zanshin-converted one gets neither, so `log`'s default revset and `immutable_heads()` differ between the two. `print_trackable_remote_bookmarks` is stdout and stays out. The alias writer is the fourth member of the `config.rs` helper family follow-up J started (remove/rename ported; `write_repo_presets` for clone parked under 9.20 above), so it is the same `ConfigFile` pattern, not new machinery. Severity medium. **Follow-up:** port `maybe_set_repository_level_trunk_alias` (guarded by `get_single_remote`, exactly as the CLI) and `maybe_add_gitignore` into both convert verbs, witnessed by a `Repo::eval_revset("trunk()")` read-back and a `.gitignore` assertion. **Decided by Ralph, 2026-09-06:** the op log IS on-disk state and undo granularity IS observable behaviour — a user must be able to undo with the CLI what Zanshin did (and vice versa) and get the SAME result. So 2.12/2.13's single folded operation (`"convert to colocated"` / `"import git repo"`, descriptions the CLI never writes) is a parity defect: split into the CLI's transaction sequence with the CLI's descriptions (`import git refs`, then `import git head` only when the working copy is not shared with git), each written only when `has_changes()`, with the CLI's own import-option overrides for that flow. Same run as the alias + `.gitignore` port.

- ~~**`remotes.<name>.auto-track-bookmarks` is not honoured at any of the three ref-import sites**~~ — REJECTED 2026-10-21 (spec-9-30-git-fetch-parity-default-remotes-filters): no longer applies — Story 9.26 already builds `remote_auto_track_bookmarks` in `config::git_import_options` for every import site; pinned by `tests/it/remotes_write.rs::git_fetch_honours_remote_auto_track_bookmarks`. [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/git.rs::git_import`, `remotes.rs::git_fetch`, `repo.rs::clone_into` — `GitImportOptions.remote_auto_track_bookmarks` stays empty at all three; `config::git_import_options` documents the gap] — the CLI's `load_git_import_options` parses the config key's string-expression list per remote (`cli/src/git_util.rs` @ v0.44.0) so that fetched bookmarks matching the expressions are auto-tracked; Zanshin never tracks any, so after a fetch the user must `bookmark track` by hand where the CLI would already show a tracked bookmark. Story 9.5 recorded it as a deliberate skip because "porting the parser would have to change all three call sites at once"; with follow-up C all three sites already read the SAME `config::git_import_options`, so it is now one change in one function plus one witness per site. Severity medium. **Follow-up:** port the expression parsing into `config::git_import_options` (jj-lib's `StringPattern`/`expect_string_expression` grammar, the same one `bookmark delete` already uses), pinned by a fetch against a remote whose bookmark matches a configured pattern and reads back as TRACKED via `list_bookmarks`; natural to land before or with Story 9.11 (`bookmark track`/`untrack`), which needs the tracked/untracked distinction anyway.

- **`git remote add` / `set-url` store a relative local path VERBATIM where the CLI stores it resolved to an absolute path (`absolute_git_url`)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/git.rs::git_remote_add` / `git_remote_set_url`; CLI `cli/src/commands/git/remote/{add,set_url}.rs` → `absolute_git_url(cwd, url)` @ v0.44.0] — the CLI resolves a relative local path against its process CWD before writing `remote.<name>.url`, so `.git/config` ends up with an absolute path that keeps working from any directory; Zanshin writes what it was given, so a relative path is later resolved by git relative to the repo, which is a different (and fragile) on-disk result. Story 9.5 argued a GUI backend has no CWD worth consulting (FR-JJ-17 pass-through). Severity low. **Follow-up (decision, Ralph):** either resolve a relative local path against the WORKSPACE ROOT (the GUI's natural CWD, matching what the CLI produces when run from the repo) inside the two verbs, or require the UI to pass absolute paths and REFUSE a relative one (`jj.invalid_input`) so the on-disk result can never diverge silently. Pass-through-as-is is the one option the rule excludes.

- **`file_annotate` propagates a store failure for a line's origin commit where the CLI substitutes the root commit** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/file.rs::file_annotate`, documented as `# KNOWN DIVERGENCE`; CLI `cli/src/commands/file/annotate.rs` @ v0.44.0] — when `get_commit_async` fails for an origin (shallow-then-unshallowed repos), the CLI falls back to the root commit for that line; Zanshin returns `FileError::Backend`, i.e. the whole annotate refuses where the CLI degrades one line. Story 9.4 skipped it because no fixture can construct the state and an unreachable branch would trip the coverage ratchet. Severity low. **Follow-up:** either construct the state in-test (a `git` shallow clone that is then unshallowed, the same `Command::new("git")` fixture style `tests/remotes_write.rs` already uses) so the fallback can be ported AND witnessed, or — if it proves unconstructible — port it anyway with a deliberate, reviewed `exclude_prefix`-free justification per `docs/coverage.md` (the ratchet rule allows a reviewed exception for production code that truly cannot be unit-tested; a documented divergence is no longer an allowed outcome).

- **`evolog` interleaves entries from several start commits with no per-start grouping** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/commit_views.rs::evolog`; CLI `cli/src/commands/evolog.rs` @ v0.44.0] — `limit` bounds the OUTPUT of `walk_predecessors`, not the start set the revset evaluates to, and rows carry no marker for which start produced them; the CLI's default graph output re-groups per start, so a multi-start `jj evolog -r 'a|b'` reads as two histories where Zanshin's payload is one interleaved list a GUI has to re-derive from `predecessor_commit_ids`. Payload-structure parity rather than an on-disk effect, so the weakest member of this list. Severity low. **Follow-up:** add a per-entry `start_commit_id` (or group index) to `EvologEntry` (`.fbs` in place + codegen + Dart model) and a multi-start witness; decide with the GUI whether ordering should also follow the CLI's per-start blocks.

## Deferred from: spec-epic-9-followup-k-convert-cli-parity (2026-09-06)

_The convert/`jj git init` parity run closed its ledger bullet in full (op split, `trunk()` alias, `.gitignore`). Its review pass surfaced five items it did not close: the first is the one the run expected to leave behind, the rest are pre-existing or newly-created side conditions._
