The conversions DO build the start-up environment (their alias and `immutable_heads()` warnings are ported; Story 9.34). Comes with the start-up-environment port Story 9.32 deferred; for `clone` it is Story 9.20's parity. Severity low.
  - **2026-10-20, Story 9.20:** `init` and `clone` build the alias tables and `immutable_heads()` (clone twice, as the CLI) with their warnings, and the clone's finishes rebase against the resolved immutable set. (commit 9066268); left: the template / short-prefixes parses, Story 9.32's start-up-environment deferral like every other verb's.
  - **2026-10-20, closing run of Story 9.51 (commit d8e4c60):** `init` / `clone` now load the template aliases and parse `revsets.short-prefixes` too (`creation.rs::workable_env` builds the full start-up environment).
- ~~**`clone` warns `jj.identity_missing` once, the CLI up to three times**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-10-20 (spec-9-20-git-push-clone-init, commit 9066268):** the clone writes the CLI's three operations (`add git remote`, `fetch from git remote into empty repo`, `check out git remote's branch`) through `finish_transaction`, each with its own identity warning and `has_changes` guard (`tests/creation_warnings.rs`). [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::clone_into`] — the CLI finishes `add git remote`, `fetch from git remote into empty repo` and `check out git remote's branch` separately (`clone.rs:352`, `:457`, `:314`) and warns after each; Zanshin writes one `clone` operation and warns once. Converges when Story 9.20 splits the operation. Severity low.
- **No Dart real-cdylib witness for a SUCCESSFUL clone** [`packages/zanshin_core/test/worker/creation_test.dart`] — the clone's fetch runs a `git` subprocess whose `Child::wait()` fails inside any `dart test` process (the Dart VM reaps its children — the same limitation `mutate_test.dart` records for `gitFetch`). The clone's `jj.checkout_files_skipped` witness was `crates/zanshin-ffi/tests/repo_ffi.rs::clone_into_a_colliding_target_warns_that_a_checkout_was_skipped` (plain `cargo test`; since Story 9.20 that colliding clone is refused like the CLI's, so the witness became `clone_into_a_colliding_target_is_refused_and_keeps_the_file`); the Dart test covers `cloneRepo`'s refusal arm. Also means a clone from a Flutter app process may hit the same wait failure — unverified. **Check (Story 6.4a, when it wires the app's fetch):** run a `cloneRepo` and a `gitFetch` from the running macOS app through the worker against a `file://` source; if either fails with "Failed to wait for the git process", the app needs the same fix the test harness lacks. Severity low.
  - **2026-10-20, check:** the clone half passed — the running debug app (`flutter run -d macos`) cloned a local `file://` bare repository through the Projects window's clone form and the real worker (Story 9.52's helper isolate): the folder was created, `README.md` checked out, the worker logged `repo cloned session=1 → handleId=0` and the project window opened on the new handle; no "Failed to wait for the git process" in the log. Left: the same check for `gitFetch` from the running app, and the Dart test witness itself.
- ~~**The conversion reads its snapshot knobs before the snapshot starts**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-09-24 (Story 9.34): `external_arm` builds the start-up environment right after `init_git_refs` and before `maybe_add_gitignore` (`for_workable_repo`, `git/init.rs:252`); the knobs are read inside `maybe_snapshot`'s snapshot step, so a wrong-typed knob fails with a PARTIAL `precondition` (`git_head_imported`, `was_snapshot_taken`) — pinned by `tests/creation_warnings.rs` (`a_wrong_typed_snapshot_knob_fails_inside_the_conversion_snapshot`, `a_bad_immutable_heads_alias_fails_before_the_conversion_snapshot`). (review pass) [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs::external_arm`] — `Knobs::read` runs before `maybe_snapshot`, so a wrong-typed `snapshot.*` knob fails the conversion with NO precondition, while the CLI reads the knobs inside `snapshot_working_copy`, after `maybe_snapshot`'s lock and colocated `import git head` (`cli_util.rs:1331-1416` @ v0.44.0). The verb precondition has the same order (Story 9.34); **extend 9.34's scope to the conversion.** Severity low.
- ~~**`Repo::init` over a directory that already holds `.git/`**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-10-20 (spec-9-20-git-push-clone-init, commit 9066268):** colocated (the `git.colocate` default or `--colocate`) runs the External flow; `--no-colocate` refuses with `jj.git_repo_exists` (oracle `init_over_git_colocate`, `init_over_git_no_colocate`). (review pass) [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::Repo::init`; CLI `cli/src/commands/git/init.rs:198-234` @ v0.44.0] — Zanshin creates an internal-git workspace beside the `.git/`; the CLI runs the External flow (default `git.colocate = true`: refs import, snapshot, warnings) or refuses ("Did not create a jj repo because there is an existing Git repo in this directory."). Pre-existing; Story 9.20's init parity. Severity low.
- ~~**A clone that fails after the CLI's `add git remote` finish lacks that finish's identity warning**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-10-20 (spec-9-20-git-push-clone-init, commit 9066268):** the `add git remote` operation is finished before the fetch, so its identity warning stands when the fetch fails. (review pass) [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::clone_into`] — the CLI finishes `add git remote origin` (`clone.rs:352`) before fetching, so an empty identity warns even when the fetch fails; Zanshin commits no remote operation. Part of the op-split divergence above (Story 9.20). Severity low.
- **No end-to-end witness of `jj.clone_cleanup_failed` through `Repo::clone`** (review pass) [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::roll_back_clone`] — pinned at the helper and on a synthetic FFI envelope only. The one fault-free trigger was the data-loss path above, which `spec-clone-rollback-keeps-existing-jj.md` closed (2026-09-24); an end-to-end witness now needs a fault seam (a `.jj/` the clone created that cannot be removed). Severity low.
- ~~**The `zanshin_core` barrel exports neither `RepoHandle`, `CreatedRepoHandle` nor `Precondition`**~~ — REJECTED 2026-10-30 (spec-10-5-graph-view-model-repo-wiring): no longer applies — the barrel already exports `RepoHandle`, `OpenedRepoHandle`, `CreatedRepoHandle` and `Precondition`. (review pass) [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/zanshin_core.dart`] — the app cannot NAME the creation result's types without a `src/` import (inference still exposes `.warnings` / `.precondition`). `RepoHandle` and `Precondition` were already absent. Severity low; export them when the UI first needs the names. **Picked up 2026-09-26 by Epic 10** (10.1 / 10.5 / 10.8 / 10.9 each export the types they consume).- ~~**A failed creation's warnings are visible nowhere on the Dart side until Story 9.35**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-09-25 (Story 9.35): `_createdOrThrow` / `_handleOrThrow` → `_envelopeToException` go through the shared `decodeEnvelopeErrorInternal`, so a refused `initRepo` / `cloneRepo` / `convert` throws a `ZanshinFfiException` carrying the creation's `warnings` (e.g. `jj.clone_cleanup_failed`) and, for a conversion, its PARTIAL `precondition`; witnessed by `test/worker/error_reports_test.dart::a_failed_conversion_keeps_its_warnings_and_partial_snapshot_report`. (follow-up review) [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/isolate.dart::_createdOrThrow`] — before Story 9.33 the conversion's warnings were at least traced (`log_dropped("convert")`); now they ride the error envelope, which Dart turns into a `ZanshinFfiException` of code and context only, so a failed `cloneRepo`'s `jj.clone_cleanup_failed` or a failed `convert`'s refs-step warnings leave no trace in the app (MCP / Rust callers do get them). Story 9.35's scope (creation verbs' error results) closes it. Severity low.

## Surfaced 2026-09-24 while fixing the clone-rollback data loss (spec-clone-rollback-keeps-existing-jj.md)

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-clone-rollback-keeps-existing-jj.md`
  status: RESOLVED 2026-10-20 (spec-9-20-git-push-clone-init, commit 9066268) — any non-empty destination, an existing workspace included, is refused first with the typed `jj.destination_not_empty`, like the CLI.
  summary: A clone into an existing jj workspace is refused only as a generic `CloneFailed` carrying jj-lib's "The destination repo (…) already exists" text — no typed code, unlike convert's `RepoError::AlreadyJjRepo`.
  evidence: A UI that wants to offer "open it instead" would have to string-match the reason. The CLI refuses any non-empty destination first ("Destination path exists and is not an empty directory", `cli/src/commands/git/clone.rs:187-192` @ v0.44.0) — Story 9.20's destination parity decides the typed refusal (and its code) together with that check. Severity low.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-clone-rollback-keeps-existing-jj.md`
  summary: The conversions' rollback removes a DANGLING `.jj` symlink it did not create.
  evidence: `convert_colocated` / `convert_import` gate on `detect_repo`, whose `.jj` check follows symlinks, so a dangling `.jj` link passes as "no jj repo"; `init_external_git` then refuses it (`DestinationExists`) and the rollback `remove_dir_all(<root>/.jj)` deletes the link (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs::convert_colocated_steps`, `convert_import_steps`). Pre-existing; only a broken symlink is lost (no workspace — a real `.jj/` is refused by `detect_repo` first). Fix: the clone's `NotFound`-only pre-existence guard. Severity low.

## Surfaced 2026-09-24 while reviewing Story 9.34 (snapshot knobs after the Git head import)

_The deferrals of `spec-9-34-snapshot-knobs-after-git-head-import.md` (frontmatter `deferred:`), mirrored here. All pre-existing since Story 9.26 / 9.22 unless noted._

- **A snapshot-knob fault's `jj.config_error` reason carries a `config error: ` prefix, a start-up-environment fault's does not** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/wc_ops.rs::auto_tracking_matcher` / `snapshot_options`, `mutate.rs::cfg`] — the two wc_ops helpers map `ConfigError` with `cfg`, which keeps its Display ("config error: …"), and `PreconditionError::Config`'s Display adds it again ("config error: config error: …"). Pre-existing for `snapshot.auto-track` and file track/untrack's own reads; Story 9.34 moved the precondition's `snapshot.max-new-file-size` read onto the helper (bare before) and the non-table `[fileset-aliases]` fault the other way (bare now, via `CommandEnv::load`). No test pins the exact context text. **Fix:** one form (bare, as `From<ConfigError>` gives) in both helpers + `assert_eq` on the reason. Severity low.
- **The error registry's `jj.config_error` row does not list the pre-verb snapshot** [`docs/rust-error-codes.md:56`] — neither the start-up refusal (before the Git import/export lock, nothing written) nor the snapshot-knob refusal (after a colocated `import git head`, which stays; partial precondition). The FFI wrappers' rustdoc carries both. Severity low.
- **`zanshin_file_track` / `_untrack`'s `jj.config_error` bullets name only their own keys** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/src/file_ops.rs`] — the pre-verb snapshot also refuses an unparseable `snapshot.auto-track` and a bad `immutable_heads()` alias / non-table alias layer on both. Severity low.
- **`jj.stale_working_copy` "nothing is written" is untrue after a colocated head import** [every FFI wrapper's stale bullet, `docs/rust-error-codes.md:79`] — `snapshot_impl` commits `import git head` before `snapshot_working_copy`'s stale check refuses (as the CLI, `cli_util.rs:1298-1326`, `:2079` @ v0.44.0). Severity low.

## Surfaced 2026-09-25 while implementing Story 9.35 (Dart error results keep precondition + warnings)

_Implementing `spec-9-35-dart-error-results-keep-precondition-warnings.md` (bridge-only: no Rust, schema or codegen change) surfaced one Rust-side gap outside its scope:_

- **A verb that panics after its precondition loses the report** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/src/envelope.rs::catch`, panic arm] — `catch` maps `Err(panic)` to `err_never_unwind(INTERNAL_PANIC, …, None)`: the `internal.panic` envelope carries no `precondition` and no `warnings`, even when the pre-verb snapshot already ran (and maybe wrote operations) before the panic. Dart now surfaces every error envelope's report on `ZanshinFfiException`, so this is the one RUST error arm that still arrives empty. The worker's own synthesized envelopes carry none either (`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/isolate.dart`): `ffi.invalid_handle` from `_invalidHandleEnvelope` (no verb ran, so there is nothing to lose) and `internal.dispatch_error` from the dispatch `catch` (a Dart-side dispatch failure, which may follow Rust work whose report is then lost). Only reachable through a panic (the `catch` is a backstop the code aims never to hit). **Fix (Rust):** keep the partial `CallReport` reachable from the panic arm (e.g. record it outside the unwind boundary before the verb body runs). Severity low.

## Surfaced 2026-09-25 while reviewing Story 9.35

_The review pass of `spec-9-35-dart-error-results-keep-precondition-warnings.md` (frontmatter `deferred`) deferred one pre-existing item:_

- ~~**The `zanshin_core` barrel does not export `ZanshinFfiException` either**~~ — REJECTED 2026-10-30 (spec-10-5-graph-view-model-repo-wiring): no longer applies — `ZanshinFfiException` and `Precondition` were already exported. [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/zanshin_core.dart`] — extends the Story 9.33 barrel item above (`RepoHandle`, `CreatedRepoHandle`, `Precondition`): since Story 9.35 the thrown `ZanshinFfiException` carries every failed command's `precondition` and `warnings`, but the app can only name the exception (and the report's `Precondition` type) through a `src/` import. Nothing breaks today (no reference in `app/`). Export them when the UI first reads the error report. Severity low. **Picked up 2026-09-26 by Epic 10** (10.5 exports `ZanshinFfiException` / `Precondition`).
## Surfaced 2026-09-25 while implementing Story 9.36 (Finder launch sees the login-shell environment)

_Implementing `spec-9-36-finder-launch-login-shell-environment.md` left these gaps by design (the spec's Never list) or outside its scope:_

- **jj-lib's other subprocesses and gix's in-process reads still see the launchd environment** [`jj-lib-0.44.0/src/gpg_signing.rs:187,269`, `ssh_signing.rs:157`, `git_backend.rs:925` (`run_git_gc`); gix's config reads — e.g. `wc_ops.rs::base_ignores`' `config_snapshot()` / `gix::config::File::from_globals()` — read `HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `GIT_CONFIG_*` from the process] — jj-lib offers no environment hook for signing or `git gc`, and gix reads the process environment directly. A Finder-launched Zanshin can therefore sign with a different `gpg`/`ssh-keygen` (or none on `PATH`) and resolve a different global git config than the terminal `jj`. Fix needs an upstream hook (or `std::env::set_var` at start-up, which the spec rules out). Severity medium for signing users.
- **The UI does not surface `env.login_shell_unresolved` yet** [`app/lib/main.dart::_adoptShellEnvironment` (was `_startApp` before the 2026-09-25 redirect)] — the warning is only logged (`zanshin.bootstrap`, `<code>: <message>`). A user whose rc file hangs or fails silently gets the launchd environment with no visible hint. UI is Ralph's scope.
- **Variables the rc file UNSETS stay in the `git` subprocess environment** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/environment.rs::git_subprocess_options`] — jj-lib applies `GitSubprocessOptions::environment` with `Command::envs` on top of the inherited process environment (no `env_clear`), so the map can add and override but never remove. The config stack is unaffected (it reads the adopted map alone). Severity low.
- ~~**A timed-out interactive shell started from a terminal is still killed**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-09-25 (Story 9.36 redirect):** the login shell is now read in Dart (`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/environment/login_shell.dart`) with `Process.start(…, mode: ProcessStartMode.detachedWithStdio)` — the Dart runtime `setsid()`s the child, so the shell has no controlling terminal to take over, and a timeout kills a process that holds none. zanshin-jj spawns nothing any more. Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/environment.rs::read_login_environment`, timeout arm] — a real `zsh -i` started under a controlling terminal (e.g. Zanshin run via `flutter run`) makes itself the terminal's foreground process group; on success Zanshin now lets it exit on its own, but on TIMEOUT it is SIGKILLed and can leave the terminal's foreground group dead (the launching tool stopped by SIGTTIN). The fix needs `setsid` for the child, which is unstable in std and `unsafe` is not allowed in `zanshin-jj`. Severity low.
- **`zanshin-mcp` never adopts** [`crates/zanshin-mcp`] — the MCP server is launched by its client with the client's environment and keeps reading the process environment; no adoption call exists there (spec: no `zanshin-mcp` change). Revisit if an MCP client launched from the Dock shows the same drift.

## Surfaced 2026-09-25 while implementing Story 9.37 (secure-config fix-ups at every command start)

_Implementing `spec-9-37-secure-config-fixups-every-command.md` left these residual divergences from the CLI (the spec's Never list or outside its scope):_

- **The `&self` accessors run no fix-up** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::log_defaults` / `workspace_root` / `resolve_workspace_root`] — the spec keeps them side-effect-free, so they read the state as of the handle's last open or precondition-running verb. The CLI's `jj workspace root` is a command and runs the start-up fix-up (a copied repo forked, a deleted secure dir regenerated, a legacy file migrated) before it answers. Severity low.
- **A malformed id file keeps Zanshin's path-named refusal** [`config.rs::SecureLayer::fix_up` → `resolved_path()` pre-check] — `<dir>/config-id: invalid config ID (expected 20 ASCII hex characters)` (`jj.config_error`), where the CLI fails with its internal "Failed to determine the secure config for a repo: Found an invalid config ID" (`command_error.rs:754-758`). Same refusal, nothing written either way; only the text differs. Severity low.
- **The `edit` halves fix up under a broken stack** [`config_cmd.rs::file_read_with` / `file_write_with` → `config::fix_up_layers`] — they run the fix-up of both layers even when a user/system/repo file does not parse, whereas every CLI command (its `jj config edit` included) refuses at start-up before the fix-ups (a broken system/user file) or between them (a broken repo file stops the workspace fix-up). Deliberate: the halves are the GUI's repair path and load no stack. Severity low.
- **`JJ_RANDOMNESS_SEED` seeds separate RNGs** [`config.rs::secure_rng`, used by `SecureLayer::fix_up` (one per pass) and `SecureLayer::create_path` (one per call)] — the CLI shares ONE seeded `ConfigEnv.rng` per command, so a fix-up followed by a `config path --workspace` draws different ids than the CLI would. Worse, a seeded COPY whose fork draws its original's id writes into the original's secure dir, and the kind is then classified `ConfigRegenerated` (same id before and after) instead of `ConfigCopied`. Only with the seed set (tests, reproducibility runs). Severity low.
- **The creation paths' final re-open runs the fix-up** [`repo.rs::init_workspace` / `clone_reported`, `convert.rs` → `Repo::open`] — a no-op on the freshly created workspace (no id file, no legacy file); its warnings, if any, are dropped via `warning::log_dropped("Repo::open", …)`. Severity none today.
- **Every test-binary run leaves its XDG temp dir behind** [`crates/test-helpers/src/lib.rs::leaked_xdg_config_home`, `TempDir::keep`] — one kept dir under `$TMPDIR` per test binary per run (the pinned `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` must outlive every test thread), plus whatever the fix-ups migrated into it. The OS temp cleaner reclaims them eventually. Severity low.
- **The UI does not show the fix-up notices yet** [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/handle.dart::OpenedRepoHandle.warnings`] — `openRepo`'s warnings (and the config results' / verb results' `jj.config_*` notices) have no consumer in `app/`; a user whose legacy config was migrated is not told. UI is Ralph's scope.
- ~~**No test pins that the bootstrap adopts BEFORE the app exists**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-09-25 (Story 9.36 redirect):** the adoption moved into `ZanshinBootSequence` as the injected `adoptShellEnvironment` step (started alongside `openSettings`, awaited before `startApp`); `zanshin_bootstrap_test.dart` pins that `startApp` is not called before it completes, and that its failure fails the boot. Original entry: [`app/lib/main.dart::_startApp`; found by the 9.36 review] — `_startApp` is the private composition root and `zanshin_bootstrap_test.dart` injects its own `startApp`, so dropping the `adoptLoginShellEnvironment()` call, or moving it after `zanshinAppFactory.create()`, would ship with every witness green. Needs a seam (e.g. the adopt-and-log step as a small bootstrap helper the app tests drive). UI/bootstrap wiring is Ralph's call. Severity low.

## Surfaced 2026-09-26 while researching the commit-graph view and authoring Stories 4.10–4.12 / Epic 10 (console session, Ralph)

_Ralph showed a mockup of the main view (commit cards at (row, lane), elbow lines, two-axis scrolling; `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/research/commit-graph-mockup-2026-09-26.webp`) and asked how to build it without a `ListView`, how to virtualize it and how to size the scrollbars before everything is loaded. Research with verified evidence and a tested POC: `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/research/technical-commit-graph-virtualization-research-2026-09-26.md` (POC: `app/lib/main4.dart` + `app/lib/src/features/graph_poc/`). That became Stories 4.10–4.12 (graph backend/bridge; Epic 4 reopened) and Epic 10 (the view itself, built by Claude at Ralph's explicit request). The items below fall outside those stories:_

- **The CLI draws at most one missing-edge stub per node; Zanshin emits every `Missing` edge.** Low.
  - Evidence: `cli/src/commands/log.rs:250-272` @ v0.44.0 keeps only the LAST `Missing` target and appends it after the direct/indirect edges. `crates/zanshin-graph/src/stream.rs:56-60` and `crates/zanshin-graph/src/lane.rs:266-279` keep each `Missing` edge in stream order.
  - Lanes are unaffected: a `Missing` edge never reserves a lane and never continues the own lane.
  - But a renderer that draws one stub per `Missing` edge shows more stubs than `jj log` for a node with several out-of-set parents.
  - Found while verifying the graph research at the pinned tag. Not in the 4.8 parity catalog (`_bmad-output/specs/spec-4-8-log-rows-cli-parity/cli-log-parity.md:71`).
  - **Follow-up:** collapse the edges in `stream.rs` (parity at the source) or draw one stub per row in the view (Epic 10). Decide together with Story 4.12's choice on whether a `Missing` edge's target id stays on the wire.
- **No lane cap — `all()` over many long-lived heads can be hundreds of lanes wide.** Low until measured.
  - Evidence: `crates/zanshin-graph/src/lane.rs:211-220` (slots are cleared, never removed), `:249-251` (each child's continuation keeps its own lane for a parent that has not arrived yet), `:389-396`.
  - Width is the peak number of concurrently open segments: every branch emitted above its fork point holds a lane down to the fork row.
  - The committed fixtures peak at lane 3 (`crates/zanshin-graph/tests/stream.rs:388-392`), so this is unmeasured on a real repo. Story 4.11's `lane_count` makes it visible.
  - Whether the view needs horizontal compaction or a cap is a product decision for Epic 10.
- **The graph perf numbers come from a loose-object fixture.** Low.
  - Evidence: `large-10k` has 30,003 loose git objects and no pack (research 2026-09-26). A cold per-commit load costs 25-37 µs in batches of up to 200 ids, but 115-190 µs in longer runs; the cause was not investigated.
  - Story 4.10's bench runs on the same fixture, so its content-page numbers may not transfer to a packed repo.
  - **Follow-up:** one run on a `git gc`-packed copy before tuning the page size.
- **The default `revsets.log` selects every commit on the committed and generated fixtures.** Low.
  - Evidence: `crates/zanshin-jj/src/revset.rs:33` `DEFAULT_LOG_REVSET`. `cargo xtask gen-fixture` sets local bookmarks only — no tags, no remote bookmarks (`crates/xtask/src/tasks/gen_fixture.rs:263-268`, `:327-332`) — and tiny ships no tags (`crates/zanshin-jj/tests/view.rs:88`). So `trunk()` is `root()` and `builtin_log()` is effectively `all()`.
  - The research's "the default log grows with mutable work, not with history" is inferred from the definition (`cli/src/config/revsets.toml` @ v0.44.0), not measured.
  - Every default-revset number taken on these fixtures is an `all()` number.
- **`--limit` / `--reversed` stay unported, and their revisit trigger disappears if windowing is replaced.** Low; decide when the view offers "oldest first".
  - Evidence: `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md` Story 4.4 "Delivered" note; `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/epic-4-context.md:28` says to revisit "when windowing returns".
  - Story 4.10 proposes replacing row-range windowing with complete geometry. If Ralph accepts, windowing never returns.
  - `--reversed` is not presentational: the CLI collects the (limited) topo stream and renders `reverse_graph` of it (`cli/src/commands/log.rs:235-239` @ v0.44.0). Those lanes differ from a flipped forward layout, so an "oldest first" view needs its own geometry pass.
- **Fallback if whole-revset geometry ever gets too big: let the scroll extent grow while rows stream in.** Low; not needed at the 10k baseline.
  - Evidence: rows are final when emitted (`crates/zanshin-graph/src/lane.rs:149-154`; research §3).
  - A view could therefore append rows and grow its content height monotonically as the stream delivers them, with the scrollbar thumb shrinking.
  - Caveat: until the stream ends, dragging to the end means nothing, and "scroll to @" works only once `@`'s row has arrived.
  - Record it as the escape hatch, not a plan.
- **`abandon` does not report the bookmarks it deleted** [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/ffi/mutate.dart:167-211`; CLI `cli/src/commands/abandon.rs:138-163` @ v0.44.0]
  - `MutationResult` carries `newOpId`, `newChangeIds`, `abandonedChangeIds`, `workingCopyChangeId`, `finish` and `warnings`, and nothing else.
  - The CLI diffs the local bookmarks before and after and prints "Deleted bookmarks: …". When a deleted bookmark had tracked remotes, it also prints a push hint.
  - 10.9 cannot show this fact and must not work it out in Dart. Backend follow-up: a field on the abandon result. Severity low.
- **The shipped verbs lack CLI parameters that the UI would have to offer** [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/handle.dart:228` `describe`, `:242` `newChange`, `:271` `abandon`, `:145` `graphSlice`; CLI @ v0.44.0 `abandon.rs:61`, `:65`; `new.rs:72`, `:76`, `:118`, `:158`; `describe.rs:53-57`; `log.rs:93`, `:143`, `:236`]
  - `abandon` has no `--retain-bookmarks` and no `--restore-descendants`.
  - `new` has no `-m`, `--no-edit`, `-A` or `-B`.
  - `describe` takes one change id where the CLI takes revsets.
  - `graphSlice` has no `--limit`, no `--reversed` and no path arguments.

  Under the pass-through rule each of these is a backend gap. Epic 10 offers only what the verbs expose (10.9). Severity medium (parity).
- ~~**No backend path from a file edit to a graph refresh**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-01 (spec-10-12-adopt-backend-layout, commit 6b9feacb): the project window's Reload button (bar's ACTION group) runs the graph reload and ProjectSession.refresh(), coalesced; with focus (20b5b0d) and Cmd-R (11.13) Ralph's refresh rule is complete. [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/events.dart:17-33`; jj-lib 0.44.0 `lib/src/config/misc.toml:10`, `lib/src/fsmonitor.rs:290`; `handle.dart:961` `snapshot()`]
  - The watcher reports only op-heads and checkout changes. A file edit writes no operation until something snapshots, so @'s `empty` badge and its content go stale while the user edits.
  - jj's own answer is `fsmonitor.watchman.register-snapshot-trigger`. It is off by default and runs the `jj` binary. FR-RX-2 (Story 3.10's external-edit test) assumes that trigger.
  - 10.5 asks Ralph for a UI refresh policy. A Zanshin-side snapshot trigger, such as a debounced `snapshot()` on file events, would be its own backend story. Severity medium.
  - **2026-10-31, Ralph:** window focus + a reload button, no timer and no file watcher ("nichts ist schlimmer als wenn ein User warten muss"); the focus trigger reloads the graph and the project reading (spec-fix-10-2-10-5-review, commit 20b5b0d); left: the reload button — Story 10.12 (in the window) and Story 11.13 (View › Reload, ⌘R).
  - **2026-11-01, Story 11.13:** View › Reload ⌘R runs `ProjectSession.refresh()` and a bound graph's `reload()` (commit 0d7811d); left: Story 10.12 publishes the graph to the menu (so the graph half runs) and adds the in-window reload button.
- **The UI has no open-project flow** [`app/lib/src/features/projects/home_dialog.dart` lists the mock `kProjects` (`app/lib/src/shared/data/mock_data.dart:377`)] — the graph needs an open `RepoHandle`, and the FR-JJ-17 detect → open/convert UI has no story. Until one exists, 10.5 opens an explicitly given path. Severity medium: it blocks real use, not development.

[`app/lib/src/features/app/zanshin_app.dart:26-28`, `:124-149`; `app/lib/src/shared/theme/zanshin_colors.dart:170`]

  - `context.zc` force-unwraps a `ZanshinColors` extension that the current themes do not register.

[mockup; the existing mock widgets in `app/lib/src/features/shell/main_nav_rail.dart`, `status_bar.dart` and `window_title_bar.dart`]

  - The nav rail (history / branches / graph / comments / undo / settings).

  "Pull" has no jj verb: `jj git fetch` plus a rebase is not one command, so it needs a decision under the CLI-parity rule. Severity low.

- ~~**The bookmark list cannot show the design's rows before Story 9.29**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-01 (spec-10-14-bookmark-list-panel, commit e1f77309): the rail's Bookmarks panel shows 9.29's rows and flags. [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/ffi/bookmarks.dart:26-67`; `handle.dart:183`; design GD:21, GV:242, GV:481-497]. Medium (the designed panel's main use case).
  - `bookmarks()` returns only local and tracked-remote entries, each with a target change id: no synced or conflicted flag, and no untracked remote rows.
  - The design's own hidden example, `release/1.5.x@origin`, is an untracked remote bookmark. The panel exists to reveal exactly such rows.
  - "Synced" cannot be derived by pairing entries: after a local rewrite, both share a change id but point at different commits (9.29's motivation).

- ~~**The "shown / total" counter needs `|all()|`**~~ — REJECTED 2026-10-31 (spec-10-7-graph-toolbar): no longer applies — 4.10 already returns `all_count`, and 10.7's count reads it through `GraphLayout.totalCount`. Original entry: [design GV:223, :304]. Low.
  - The design's total counts every commit, independent of the revset.
  - Story 4.10 as written returns `total_rows` for the requested revset only.
  - An index-only `|all()|` count must be added to 4.10's geometry result, or a separate count call. Otherwise 10.7 shows the shown count only.
  - **2026-10-31, Story 10.7 (spec-10-7-graph-toolbar):** settled, nothing to fix — 4.10 already returns `all_count` (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/topology.rs`, `packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/ffi/graph.dart`), `GraphLayout.totalCount` carries it, and the count reads "**N** of M" / "**M** changes" from it ("**N** changes" while it is null); 10.12 maps `all_count` to `totalCount` for the real source.
- **Every time-based mode needs every commit's timestamp before layout** [jj-lib 0.44.0 `default_index/readonly.rs:693-696`: generation numbers, no timestamps; design GV:161-172; spike SPEC §2, §4.2]. Medium; it decides the cost of Time and free at 10k–100k.
  - Time rows depend on each commit's bucket, and the spike's `free` y depends on each commit's time. The index carries no timestamps.
  - Story 4.10 as written moves timestamps into lazily loaded content.
  - One of three options must be chosen in 4.10/4.11:
    - the geometry pass loads every commit, about 1.2 s at 10k, giving back most of 4.10's gain;
    - a persistent commit-id → timestamp cache (commit-intrinsic, so it never goes stale);
    - the time-based modes are restricted to small revsets.
  - Story 10.13's outcome does not remove this; only a Compact-only product would.
- **What Epic 10 needs from the re-plans of 4.11 and 4.10** [GV:140-225; `spikes/graph-layout-ab/SPEC.md`]. Medium.
  - **4.11, modes.** All three modes behind a mode enum (compact, time, free), with rows in 1/U units plus `unit` and `rows` (SPEC §3). 10.13 decides which ones the product exposes, but 4.11's build does not wait for it.
  - **4.11, still open** (the spike does not fix these; each needs Ralph or the re-plan):
    - blocked-merge routing, which runs behind cards and collinear with the chain and pushes the continuation off its lane (prototype defect 2; SPEC D9, §11.4). The spike's synthetic inputs have many, e.g. 229 blocked merges on `synth-10k` (compact, greedy), so Epic 10's route invariant stays a counted report until this is fixed;
    - free-mode buses passing behind cards (SPEC D8, §11.1), if free mode ships;
    - a real source for the hand-set `priority` (SPEC §11.2; a stand-in today);
    - lane-order stability across reloads (SPEC §11.5): "Min. crossings" is global, so one operation can reshuffle columns.
  - **4.10, elided counts.** The layout neither outputs Missing edges nor counts elided commits (SPEC §0, §3, D2). So the stub comes from 4.10's Missing edges, and N, if 10.4 design question (a) keeps it, needs a 4.10 geometry field or a separate count call. The prototype's per-parent DFS is O(h) per Missing parent, and jj's bounded Missing walk differs from it.
  - **Already fixed by the spike**, credited here so the re-plan does not redo them: the Time topological clamp (D1); a loop instead of the `rowOf` recursion (§4.2); no duplicate parent edges (§2, V3); O(n log n) lane packing and O(E log n) blocked checks (§4.3–§4.4); budgeted sifting instead of the factorial / O(E²) optimiser, which also counts crossings of edges that share a node (§4.6, D5).
  - Also: the mock's `wq` default-revset flag is wrong. jj shows 13 of the 22 commits, with the stub under `vr` (GD:1-2, :17), so `graph-data.js` is not a test oracle for the default revset. The spike's design oracle keeps the mock's flags on purpose; 10.1 records a CLI-faithful variant next to it.
- **Design chrome and panels outside Epic 10** [APP:135-171, :206-308, :313-350, :956-972, :1054-1080, :1983-2178, :2263-2271]. Low. They are Ralph's UI, and no Epic 10 story covers them:
  - the window header: Fetch / Pull / Push / New, the system/dark/light theme switch, and a "?" help button with no action;
  - the status bar: "@ branch", taken from `data.js` BOOKMARKS and stale relative to the graph's @ (APP:957), and a "⌘K palette" hint although no palette exists;
  - the nav rail, and the placeholder Workstreams / Reviews / Undo Timeline panels; *(Resolved 2026-10-16 for Workstreams: dropped everywhere, decision 16.26; 11.10 removes the remnants.)*
    - **2026-10-30, Story 11.10:** the Workstream remnants are gone with the mock files; `app/lib` and `app/assets` hold no `workstream` (commit 9a7f3c6); left: the bullet's other chrome items, for their own stories.
  - the Projects (Home) dialog, and the Density tweak;
  - dead flyout code that is never opened;

## Surfaced 2026-09-26 while re-planning Stories 4.10–4.12 after the layout A/B (console session, Ralph)

_Ralph decided to run the commit-graph layout in Dart on the worker isolate (`spikes/graph-layout-ab`, README "Review 2026-09-26"), and Stories 4.10–4.12 were re-planned on that. Measurements were taken the same day on a copy of a real 1.4k-commit repo kept outside the repo; only counts and timings are recorded. The items below fall outside the re-planned stories, or update earlier bullets of the 2026-09-26 section:_

- **Status of the earlier 2026-09-26 graph bullets after the re-plan.** Low.
  - "The graph perf numbers come from a loose-object fixture": now measured. A commit load costs 3.4–4.8 µs packed against ~122 µs loose, and plain git and the jj CLI show the same split. 4.10 item 8 benches a `git gc`-packed copy next to the committed fixture.
  - "Every time-based mode needs every commit's timestamp": now 4.10 item 4. Loading them per call on request is recommended; a commit-id cache is its open question 1, with a measured trigger.
  - "What Epic 10 needs from the re-plans of 4.11 and 4.10": covered by 4.10 items 1–4 (parent count, Missing edges, `all_count`, anchors, timestamps) and 4.11 items 5–9 (input builder, priority, stability, kept defects, result view). Two gaps remain: its line "no duplicate parent edges (§2, V3)" under "Already fixed by the spike" is wrong (next bullet), and N has no owner (the bullet after).
  - "The PRD's graph FRs still describe the `jj log` lane model": FR-GR-1 / FR-GR-2 / FR-GR-5 get dated notes with the re-plan. Re-pointing FR-GR-4 / NFR-P-2 and `project-context.md:14` is 4.10 item 9.
  - "The CLI draws at most one missing-edge stub per node": moot once 4.12 lands. The topology drops the Missing target id, and the view draws one stub per node (10.4).
  - "No lane cap": about the pvigier lanes, which 4.12 proposes to remove. The design's packing has its own width: 10–12 lanes on the real repo's `all()`, 15–16 on jj's own history, 134–173 on the synthetic stress inputs. Whether the view needs horizontal compaction stays an Epic 10 question.
  - "`--limit` / `--reversed` … revisit trigger disappears": it did, because the windowing amendment was accepted. An "oldest first" view would be a layout option (4.11), not a jj pass-through.
  - `syntheticElided`: the re-plan's Epic 10 fixups stop sending it (10.5 item 2, 10.7 item 9), because the topology call has no synthetic nodes. What `ui.log-synthetic-elided-nodes` means for this view stays 10.4 design question (a).
- ~~**SPEC §2's "jj never yields duplicate targets" is false on real data.**~~ — REJECTED 2026-10-26 (spec-10-1-graph-viewport-2d-virtualized): no longer applies — Story 4.11 amended SPEC §2 (`docs/graph-layout.md:148-158`) and applies the one-edge-per-target rule in the dumper (`input.rs:145-163`) and the input builder (`input_builder.dart:44-75`); every 10.1 recording passes the checker's no-duplicate-edge rule (the `tools/fuzz.mjs` repeated-targets line was not checked). Medium; it decides whether goldens, recordings and the product agree on merges.
  - On the real repo's default revset (12 nodes), a merge carries a Direct and an Indirect edge to the same parent.
  - The stage-2 dumper (`spikes/graph-layout-ab/rust-inputs/src/jj_dump.rs`) keeps the first edge and records the other in its `.meta.json` sidecar (`dropped_duplicate_parents`).
  - 4.11 item 5 keeps V3 as the engine's validation and puts the rule into the input builders: one layout edge per (child, target) at the first position, `direct` if any duplicate is direct (open question).
  - **Follow-up:** SPEC §2 and the dumper adopt the rule before 4.11's goldens and 10.1's recordings are made. `tools/fuzz.mjs` should generate repeated targets for the builder's tests.
- ~~**The elided count N has no owner.**~~ — REJECTED 2026-10-30 (spec-10-4-graph-edges-rounded-routes): no longer applies — the Epic 10 header answers design question (a) with "no counts" and moves the elided marker to 10.19. Low; only if 10.4 design question (a) keeps "~ N elided".
  - N depends on the revset (a parent is hidden relative to what the revset shows), so it is neither topology nor commit-intrinsic content, and 10.11's commit-id cache cannot hold it. It needs an index walk per Missing parent.
  - 4.10's Non-goals exclude it, and the re-plan's Epic 10 fixups now say "a follow-up count call".
  - **Follow-up:** if 10.4 (a) keeps N, create a story for an op-pinned count call for the visible nodes, e.g. `elided_counts(operation_id, revset, commit_ids)`.
  - Resolved in Story 10.4 (2026-10-30): the Epic 10 header (2026-09-30 note, epics.md:8988-8994) answers design question (a) with "no counts" and moves the elided marker to 10.19, so N needs no owner and no `elided_counts` call is created.
- ~~**The spike's dumper is the reference input builder and must move onto the product path.**~~ — REJECTED 2026-10-26 (spec-10-1-graph-viewport-2d-virtualized): no longer applies — Story 4.11 switched the dumper to `graph_topology` (`jj_dump.rs:52-53`); 10.1's medium-narrow recording comes from its tracked output. Low.
  - `jj_dump.rs` calls `Repo::walk_graph` and computes the `priority` stand-in itself (`assign_priorities`, `rust-inputs/src/input.rs:147`).
  - 10.1 records its layouts from these dumps until 10.12, and 4.11's end-to-end tests compare the Dart input builder against them.
  - **Follow-up:** 4.11 item 10. The dumper calls `Repo::graph_topology` (+ `commit_contents` for the sidecar) and computes the classes from its anchors exactly as SPEC §11.2 (amended) and 4.11 item 6 do. Re-record 10.1's recordings after the switch.
- ~~**Story 9.25 builds on `graphSlice`.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-25 (spec-9-25-graph-search-backend-revset-counts-change-search, commit b6da7c2): the refined 9.25 counts through its own `revset_counts` call, tested against the topology's node count; 4.12's leftover line got a dated settle note. Low.
  - Evidence: epics.md Story 9.25, Apply evaluates via `graphSlice` and has an "N revisions" line; its open question says Apply "should" reuse `graphSlice` directly.
  - If 4.12 removes the slice path, Apply evaluates through the topology call and uses its node count.
  - **Follow-up:** re-scope 9.25 when it is refined.
- **The worker is one serial isolate for every open repo.** Low until measured.
  - Every handle's commands and the watcher events share one isolate (`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/isolate.dart:1674-1708`). A layout, a timestamped topology call on a loose store (~1.2 s at 10k) or any other long synchronous command delays everything queued behind it.
  - 4.11 item 9 measures a content page queued behind a layout and names a second `Isolate.spawn` layout isolate as the fallback.
  - **Follow-up:** if the measurement shows stalls that matter, decide between that second isolate and splitting long commands; the same question applies to other slow commands once real repos run in the app.
- **Zanshin never packs the git store, and loose commits load ~30× slower.** Low.
  - Measured 2026-09-26: 3.4–4.8 µs per commit load packed, ~122 µs loose.
  - jj-lib writes new objects loose. Neither jj-lib nor Zanshin runs `git gc` / `jj util gc`, and git's own auto-gc runs only from git commands (e.g. a `git fetch` subprocess).
  - A repo worked only through Zanshin therefore accumulates loose commits. That slows content pages and every timestamped layout (4.10 item 4), and it is one trigger for 4.10's option (c).
  - **Follow-up:** measure on a long-lived, Zanshin-only repo. `jj util gc` parity would be an Epic 9 verb, not a background job.
- **`empty` on merge commits dominates content pages.** Low.
  - `Commit::is_empty` first asks jj's changed-path index (jj-lib 0.44.0 `commit.rs:160-165`). Only when that index does not cover the commit does it merge the parent trees of a merge: 0.78 ms per merge, measured without one.
  - On the real repo that is 115 ms of today's 163 ms `collect_lanes(all())`. The CLI's default `jj log` template pays the same (138 ms).
  - `large-10k` has one merge, so fixture numbers hide this.
  - **Follow-up:** tune 10.11's page and buffer size on a real repo, not on the fixtures, and measure a content page with a changed-path index present (4.10 item 8). Building one is an Epic 9 verb for CLI parity (`jj debug index-changed-paths`), never a background job.
  - **2026-10-31, Ralph:** the tuning moves to Story 10.12 (its real `content` call), measured on a copy of the real 1.4k repo kept outside git and on a packed `large-10k`; recorded in epics.md Story 10.12 item 7.
  - **2026-11-01, Story 10.12:** measured on medium-1k and large-10k, loose and packed, with and without the changed-path index: every content page stays under one frame (300 ids on loose 10k included), so 10.11's page and buffer stay (commit 6b9feacb, numbers in spec-10-12-adopt-backend-layout.md); left: the copy of the real 1.4k repo — its location is Ralph's.
- **Real-repo data stays out of git.** None; a standing rule.
  - The 2026-09-26 real-repo dumps, metadata sidecars (descriptions, authors, bookmarks) and layout outputs live outside the repository.
  - The spike's `.gitignore` covers `/inputs/`, `/outputs/` and `/viewer/data/`.
  - 4.10's and 4.12's benches accept a local repo path and record only node counts and timings.
  - Any new tool that writes real-repo data writes it to a gitignored path or outside the repo.

  - A segment is atomic. A long-lived branch that keeps receiving trunk merges moves as a whole. On the real repo it brings back the pattern of Ralph's screenshot for ~40 pitches (spike README "Lane order review 2026-09-26" §6). Candidate: split segments at quiet stretches.

  - a single card left between two stacks by a gap;

  - the @ lineage through a gap.

  Its `groupStacks` also counts only Direct children (GV:232 at `a399daf`), unlike 4.13.
  - **Follow-up:** Claude wrote Ralph a prompt for Claude Design. It asks for this case, for sample data that contains it, and for the stub position of 10.4 design question (b). A new export folds into 10.4 (design questions (a) and (b)) and 10.17.
  - **Resolved 2026-09-30** (export `e243dd2`). The export shows all four cases, and its sample contains them (52 commits). Its `groupStacks` now counts children behind a gap (GV:264-276). The export answered 10.4's design questions (a)–(c), and the gap work became Story 10.19 (the Epic 10 header, "Design update 2026-09-30").

## Surfaced in Story 4.10 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-09-30)

_The spec (`spec-4-10-graph-topology-content-split-op-pinned.md`) carries the first three items in its `deferred:` list (two decisions for Ralph and the gap the run left open); the fourth is a record. Numbers: its § Measurements (the bench's second run, 2026-10-01)._

- **Option (c)'s trigger is met at 10k packed — decision for Ralph.** Medium.
  - The decision (OQ 1 = (b)) says: adopt the per-handle timestamp cache when a first timestamped topology call exceeds ~50 ms on a packed store, or loose commits accumulate.
  - Measured: the FIRST `graph_topology(all(), with_timestamps)` call on a fresh handle is 53 ms (median) on `large-10k` packed, of which ~31 ms are the commit loads; 105 ms on `medium` and ~1.04 s on `large-10k` as committed (every object loose). A repeat call on a reused handle costs the same. Zanshin never packs, so a repo that only Zanshin writes accumulates loose commits.
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph decides whether (c) (a commit id → timestamp cache on `zanshin_jj::Repo`, with its ADR-0002 Addendum clause and bound) or (d) (a Dart-side cache in the worker) is built now, or whether 10k packed at 53 ms stays acceptable. Nothing in 4.10 depends on it.
- ~~**Memoising the pinned `ReadonlyRepo` — does its load dominate a page? Decision for Ralph.**~~ — DECIDED 2026-10-31 (Ralph): larger pages, no repo cache. 10.11's `GraphContent` already sends every missing id of the visible range + buffer in one uncapped call; 10.12 item 7 tunes the buffer (or a minimum page) so the pinned load stays a small share, and a per-operation memo becomes a story only if 10.12's real-repo numbers still show it dominating. Low.
  - The non-goal says: memoise the pinned repo within one operation id only if `load_operation` + `load_at` dominates a page.
  - Measured (empty-list call ÷ page, medians, cold commit cache, no changed-path index): the pinned load is 0.19 ms on `medium` loose, 0.86 ms on `large-10k` loose and 0.78 ms on `large-10k` packed — ~8 %, ~28 % and ~45 % of a 60-id page (~51 % packed with a changed-path index), and ~3 %, ~12 % and ~33 % of a 200-id page. So on a packed 10k store it is about half of a 60-id page.
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph decides whether that dominates enough to memoise the pinned repo per operation id (handle state, an ADR-0002 Addendum clause) or whether 10.11 should page in larger batches instead. Nothing in 4.10 depends on it.
- **No real-repo numbers.** Low.
  - The bench measures the committed fixtures and a packed copy of `large-10k`; the optional local real repo needs a path (decisions-review-4-10-to-4-13.md, "Not done unattended").
  - **Follow-up:** run the bench against a copy of one of Ralph's repos if the fixture numbers are not convincing — never against the live repo, since the topology call snapshots.
- **A changed-path index has no measurable effect on the fixtures.** Low; a record.
  - Within noise: 5 samples per row, and several index rows are slower than their no-index rows. The fixtures' one octopus merge merges tiny trees, so `empty`'s tree merge is cheap there. The 0.78 ms per merge measured on the real repo (2026-09-26) is where the index would pay.
  - **Follow-up:** none; building the index is the user's `jj debug index-changed-paths`, not a Zanshin decision.
