## Surfaced in Story 4.13 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-01)

_The spec (`spec-4-13-commit-stacks-layout-engine.md`) carries every item in its `deferred:` list. Numbers: its § Measurements; decisions: `decisions-review-4-10-to-4-13.md` ### 4.13._

- ~~**D11: a split run's stack node leaves its parent edge unprotected below the card.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-30 (spec-10-4-graph-edges-rounded-routes, commit 4ad948d): router B sends the edge up a gutter, so the painter marks nothing and `rows` does not grow (Claude's proposal); a D11 hand case and the Compact route-report gate witness it; confirmed by Ralph 2026-10-31 after a catalogue demo of both variants. Low; a known limit, recorded in `docs/graph-layout.md` §4.8 and §9.
  - The stack reserves its column from its own card, so its parent edge (a branch-off from m_1's parent) rises in the stack's column without a reservation and may pass behind a lower card there. Reserving from one pitch above the parent (§4.3) would let collapsing grow `rows`. The 1,000-DAG invariants test produces 637 split runs (compact, greedy).
  - **Follow-up:** 10.4 / 10.17 decide whether the painter marks such an edge, or Ralph prefers a growing `rows` (a SPEC amendment + goldens).
  - Resolved in Story 10.4 (2026-10-30), Claude's proposal: the painter marks nothing and `rows` does not grow. Router B classifies a first-parent edge into another column as `fork` only when the child's column is free between the rows, else `gutter` (docs/graph-layout.md §14.2), so the D11 edge climbs a gutter instead of the stack's column; the route report's Compact gate and a D11 hand case (`layout_invariants_test.dart`) witness it; §4.8 and §9 carry a dated note.
- **The design sample's compaction with the `compact` order is measured, not derived by hand.** Low.
  - By hand (and asserted): `compact` mode, greedy, every run collapsed — all 19 → 15, default 9 → 8 (the planning estimate 9 → 7 missed a binding column constraint at sxmtvqno). With the `compact` order the engine gives 19 → 16 and 9 → 8; the stack goldens pin those outputs, but no hand derivation backs them.
  - **Follow-up:** none needed unless a review wants the derivation.
- **A group of equal Y with a long δ = 0 chain costs quadratic passes.** Low; a record.
  - §4.8's level sweep repeats passes over a group of equal Y until nothing changes; the implementations alternate the direction, so a chain of δ = 0 constraints (`free` nodes less than a minute apart with no edge between them, or (C)'s merge terms) settles in a few passes unless it runs against both directions. Not seen on any fixture or synthetic input (the `stacks` phase is 0.3–0.45 ms on fixture-large-all and ~0.8–1.0 ms on synth-10k, ~9–11 ms on synth-100k); an `assert` holds the bound in tests.
  - **Follow-up:** a worklist per group if a real repo shows it.
- **The stacks path has no all-inputs differential.** Low.
  - The product engine equals the spike's Dart on the committed stack goldens (the design sample × 3 requests × 2 modes × 2 orders, 60 seeded fuzz cases × 4), and both pass the invariants; `tools/compare.mjs --core` still compares only stack-less outputs (stacks have no Rust reference).
  - **Follow-up:** give `compare.mjs --core` a `--stacks` pass over `tools/stacks.mjs facts` inputs if the engine's stack code changes again.
- **No real-repo numbers for stacks.** Low.
  - Measured on the committed fixtures and the spike's synthetic inputs with synthetic facts (`node tools/stacks.mjs facts`); a real repo needs a path.
  - **Follow-up:** dump a copy with the spike's `rust-inputs`, add facts, bench with `graph_layout_cli bench --stacks`.
- ~~**`cargo make test` is red on `app/lib/src/shared/theme/zanshin_icons.dart` since `40a2a58` ("update icon font").**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-10-08:** fixed by Ralph's regenerated file in `c88a864` (SPDX header + `ignore_for_file: constant_identifier_names`); `test-dart-analyze` / `test-dart-format` (app) pass, and the full `cargo make test` is green again (verified during the jj-lib 0.46.0 bump). Original entry: Medium; needs Ralph (the story may not touch `app/lib`).
  - Both of the story's gate runs stop at `test-dart-analyze` (app): 103 info-level lints (`--fatal-infos`), all in that regenerated file; `test-dart-format` (app) reports the same file. The file is unchanged since the baseline `40a2a58`.
  - Every other gate task passed when run on its own (`cargo make --allow-private <task>`): cargo test, clippy, fmt, flutter test, test-core (785 tests), all package analyze/format steps, cargo deny, hygiene, coverage (`coverage-check: OK — 99.47 %`, `extern-coverage: OK`).
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph fixes or excludes the generated icons file (e.g. `dart fix --apply lib/src/shared/theme/zanshin_icons.dart` + `dart format` in `app/`, or an analyzer exclude for generated files); the gate is green again after that.

## Surfaced 2026-10-08 while bumping jj-lib 0.44.0 → 0.46.0 (console session, Ralph)

_The bump absorbed the API churn inside `zanshin-jj` and ported the CLI behavior the new API forces (per-workspace Git HEAD, `jj git import` without the HEAD import, the non-colocated conversion's `import_head_commit`, colocation detected through `open_git_repo_at_workdir`, `ResolvedRevset::evaluate` for `at_operation()`, `untracked_remote_tags()` in the default `immutable_heads()`). These CLI 0.45/0.46 behavior changes need a public-API decision and were left at the v0.44.0 behavior:_

- ~~**`undo` does not refuse a cross-workspace operation**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-10-08 (Story 9.39, ported together with the undo stack):** `Repo::undo(allow_cross_workspace)`, `jj.undo_cross_workspace`, `jj.undo_workspace_removal`. Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/op_log.rs::undo`; CLI `cli/src/commands/undo.rs:120-157` @ v0.46.0] — since jj 0.46 `jj undo` refuses an operation performed in another workspace ("Refusing to undo operation … because it was performed in workspace …", hint `--allow-cross-workspace` / `jj op revert`), and warns when the undone operation is a `remove workspace`. Porting needs a new `allow_cross_workspace` parameter through `Repo::undo`, the FFI and `zanshin_core`, plus a new error code. Severity medium (Zanshin's undo can silently undo another workspace's work).
- ~~**`workspace root --name` still refuses an unreachable recorded path; `workspace list` still drops it**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-08 (console session): `workspace.rs::recorded_root` returns the store's path verbatim; `list` reports it, `resolve_root` returns `Warned<PathBuf>` and warns `jj.workspace_path_inaccessible` ("Cannot access workspace path: {path}") when the directory is gone; `WorkspaceError::PathUnresolved` / `jj.workspace_path_unresolved` removed (Rust, FFI, Dart, docs). Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/workspace.rs::resolve_recorded_root`; CLI `cli/src/commands/workspace/root.rs:42-66` @ v0.46.0, commit `d7e6ca981`] — jj 0.46 no longer canonicalizes: `root --name` prints the recorded path and only warns "Cannot access workspace path: …", and `list` shows every recorded path. Porting removes `WorkspaceError::PathUnresolved` / `jj.workspace_path_unresolved` and adds a warning kind (FFI + Dart). Severity low.
- ~~**`config set/unset/edit/path` without a path still answer `AmbiguousFile` when a scope has several files**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-08 (console session): `zanshin_jj::ConfigTarget` = `Scope(scope)` (the scope's FIRST file, else the new-file default) | `File(path)` (`--file`, ported `resolve_file_to_edit` incl. its create-forced repo/workspace check and `conf.d/*.toml`; a relative path joined onto the workspace root); `ConfigCmdError::AmbiguousFile` / `jj.config_ambiguous_file` replaced by `UnrecognizedFile` / `jj.config_unrecognized_file`; the wire keeps `scope` + `path` (exactly one, both/neither → `jj.invalid_input`); Dart `ConfigScopeTarget` / `ConfigFileTarget`. `config path` was never affected (its `ConfigLevelArgs` has no `--file`). Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/config_cmd.rs` (candidate selection); CLI `cli/src/commands/config/mod.rs:105-173` @ v0.46.0] — since jj 0.45 the CLI takes the FIRST loaded file of the scope instead of prompting, and `--file <PATH>` targets any recognized config file (a `conf.d/` file, a system file, `$JJ_CONFIG`). Severity low.
- ~~**The graph walk's log set does not merge `at_operation()` indexes**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-25 (spec-9-42-graph-walk-at-operation-like-jj-log, commit 5c45951): `LogParse::evaluate` evaluates through `ResolvedRevset::evaluate` and keeps its `at_operation()` repos for the topology's `ParentCount` passes; prioritize = `prioritize ∩ revset.within_visibility(head)`; oracle `log_at_operation.rs` compares with the pinned `jj log`. — **MOVED 2026-10-08 → Story 9.42** (`9-42-graph-walk-at-operation-like-jj-log`, low priority; also covers `within_visibility` on the prioritize expression). Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/graph.rs::LogParse::evaluate`] — `jj log` evaluates through `ResolvedRevset::evaluate` (`cli/src/commands/log.rs:161` @ v0.46.0), so a log revset with `at_operation(<sibling op>, …)` now works there; Zanshin evaluates the resolved expression against the repo's own index because the topology passes refine that expression, so such a revset still fails. Severity low.
- ~~**Config migration rule not ported**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-23 (spec-9-9-metaedit-duplicate-fix): ported with the `fix` verb — `config::resolve_raw` applies the CLI's `default_config_migrations()` rule to every resolved stack, `Repo::start_call` (and the config verbs) warn `jj.config_deprecated`; `fix` reads the migrated `line-range-args`. Re-check the rule at every pin bump (the CLI drops it in jj 0.51). [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/config.rs` module docs] — the CLI's `default_config_migrations()` now rewrites `fix.tools.<name>.line-range-arg` → `line-range-args` (and warns); no Zanshin verb reads `fix.tools`. Port it with a `fix` verb. Severity none today.
- ~~**Workspace stories must adopt the 0.46 workspace model**~~ — DONE 2026-10-08: the 0.46 model is in the texts of Stories 9.13 (incl. the new `workspace remove`), 9.23 (incl. the `snapshot.auto-update-stale` recovery arm) and 9.21 (`redo --allow-cross-workspace`) in `epics.md` and `sprint-status.yaml`; `jj git colocation` for child workspaces became Story 9.45. Original entry: [Stories 9.13 / 9.23] — `jj workspace add --[no-]colocate` (+ `git.colocate`) creates a Git worktree for a child workspace, `forget` removes it, and `jj workspace remove` (new) deletes the directory; `remove_workspace` now also drops the workspace's Git HEAD. The colocation check already recognizes linked worktrees.
- **New CLI verbs since v0.44.0 recorded as stories** (2026-10-08, console session): `jj converge` (0.45) → Story 9.43 (product decision pending, Ralph); `jj file edit` / `jj file delete` (0.46) → Story 9.44 (**DONE 2026-10-25**, spec-9-44-file-edit-file-delete, commit ff4f515); `jj git colocation status|enable|disable`, per workspace since 0.46 → Story 9.45 (**DONE 2026-10-25**, spec-9-45-git-colocation-status-enable-disable, commit 53b5f87); `jj git push` to several remotes + `revsets.git-push` (0.46) → Story 9.20's addendum; the 0.46 `finish` location and the new verbs → Story 9.40's update.
- ~~**No full v0.44 → v0.46 parity audit of the other ported verbs yet**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-26 (spec-9-46-v044-v046-parity-audit-ported-verbs, commit e565857de6d13466ac7b00631955c4b60c93610a, I1). Every hunk of the CLI sources behind shipped verbs was classified, plus the shared `cli_util.rs`, `git_util.rs`, `config.rs`, `command_error.rs`, `revset_util.rs`, `commit_ref_list.rs`, `diff_util.rs` and the hand-ported `commit_templater.rs` parts. The per-verb table is in the spec. Two `jj git remote list` divergences were ported (push-only remote, refusal on a remote gix cannot load). The native-backend list is deferred ("Surfaced in Story 9.46"). — **MOVED 2026-10-08 → Story 9.46** (`9-46-v044-v046-parity-audit-ported-verbs`, Ralph asked for a story; it lists the changed CLI sources per shipped verb). Original entry: the CLI changed `describe`, `status`, `log`, `file show/list/annotate/untrack`, `bookmark list/track/move/advance`, `git init/clone/remote add/remote list`, `op log/show/diff` between the tags (`git -C ../jj diff v0.44.0 v0.46.0 -- cli/src/commands`). The bump only covered what the compiler and the gate surfaced; source comments citing `@ v0.44.0` line numbers stay correct for that tag but need re-checking at v0.46.0 when touched.

## Surfaced 2026-10-08 while analysing "fork jj instead of re-porting the CLI" (console session, Ralph)

_The analysis and POC live on branch `poc/jj-cli-reuse` under `spikes/jj-cli-reuse/`. The differential parity oracle there (`poc/tests/parity_zanshin.rs`) runs the same operation through `zanshin-jj` and the stock jj CLI and compares repo state._

- ~~**`describe` and `new` never apply `templates.commit_trailers`**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-20 (spec-9-51-jj-templates-trailers-draft-push-bookmark, commit 74338d0): `describe`, `new`, `commit`, `squash`, `split` and `workspace add` apply `templates.commit_trailers` through jj-cli's template engine (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/template.rs`); the oracle's `describe_commit_trailers` matches the CLI and its ledger row is gone. Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/op_support.rs::apply_trailers` (called only from its inline tests); CLI `cli/src/commands/describe.rs:153-161` and `new.rs:234` @ v0.46.0, already present @ v0.44.0] — with `templates.commit_trailers = 'format_signed_off_by_trailer(self)'`, `jj describe -m T` stores `T` + a blank line + `Signed-off-by: …`, while Zanshin stores `T`. This predates 0.46. Found by reading the code; the parity oracle confirmed it for `describe`. `new` is the same pattern but was not run through the oracle. The not-yet-ported `commit`, `squash`, `split` and `workspace add` apply trailers too (`add_trailers`). No story covers it. Severity medium: a silent divergence on shipped verbs.
  - **2026-10-20, Ralph:** one story for every jj-template gap → **Story 9.51** (`9-51-jj-templates-trailers-draft-push-bookmark`).

## Surfaced 2026-10-14 while folding the floating client and its design system into Epics 10 and 11 (console session, Ralph)

_Ralph re-downloaded the Claude Design export (`e74ced3`): the reference is now `JJ Desktop Client Floating.html`, and a new design-system project sits in `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/design_system/` (synced through #9). He decided that the export wins where it explicitly changed a recorded decision, that motion follows the client's FLIP model, that the inspector, the card menu, the Bookmarks rail, the search and the Configurations dialog are built to the design, and that Claude now builds the whole Flutter UI (new Epic 11; Story 11.0 recorded the scope change). The stories were updated the same day: the Epic 10 header ("Design update 2026-10-14", decisions (a)–(g), prototype defects 25–76, amendments to defects 2, 8, 16, 17 and 21–23, the house rules Theme and Design-system sync), new Stories 4.14, 4.15, 9.48–9.50, 10.21–10.27 and 11.0–11.10, the refined 9.25, and dated notes in 4.11, 4.13, 5.1, 5.6, 9.15–9.17, 9.19, 9.31 and most Epic 10 stories. Six older entries above are marked as moved, moot or superseded. The items below fall outside those stories:_

### Graph layout and lines

- ~~**The spike's design-input tool cannot read `e74ced3`.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-27 (spec-4-14-shared-columns-free-compact, commit 3739e9a): `spikes/graph-layout-ab/tools/design-layout.mjs` evaluates `graph-layout.jsx` whole (`revsetMatch` stubbed as LSH:54, `placeGapMarkers` a no-op); `design-input.mjs --rev e74ced3` works again, byte-identical to the `e243dd2` inputs, and Story 4.15 reuses the loader. Low: no recording needs it.
  - `node tools/design-input.mjs --rev e74ced3` throws "no top-level declaration of G". It extracts six declarations by name from `graph-view.jsx` (design-input.mjs:33-54), which now imports them from `graph-layout.jsx` (GV:6-7).
  - GD and the gap walk are unchanged (GL:435-460 = GV@e243dd2:145-170), so the `e243dd2` inputs stand for this export too.
  - **Follow-up:** 4.14 and 4.15 add a loader that evaluates `graph-layout.jsx` whole. It is plain JS: stub `revsetMatch` as LSH:54 does, and stub `placeGapMarkers` or extract it.
- ~~**The joint column packing may widen the graph.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-29 (console session, measured on a copy of a real 4.7k-commit repo, `dart-intellij-third-party`): no port of the exact colouring needed — the width barely grows (numbers in the sub-bullet below). Medium.
  - Chains may share a lane only when they are apart in both layouts, so 4.14's packing can need more columns than either per-mode packing.
  - The export compensates with an exact chain colouring for small graphs and with its whole-lane optimizer (GL:492-568). 4.14 ports neither.
  - **Follow-up:** 4.14's measurements on the real 1.4k-commit repo and the scale inputs. If the width grows noticeably, port the exact colouring for small graphs.
  - **2026-10-27, Story 4.14:** measured on the design samples, fixture-medium/large `all()`, synth-10k and synth-100k (spec-4-14 § Measurements): Compact widens on the fixtures (3 → 4 lanes, +28 % mean width vs per-mode compact), Free on synth-100k narrows (mean 98.6 → 42.9); the exact colouring is not ported, since it runs only at ≤ 40 chain heads and saves no lane there (commit 3739e9a); left: the measurement on the real 1.4k-commit repo, whose copy was not reachable (Ralph).
  - **2026-10-29, real repo** (a copy of `dart-intellij-third-party`, spike dumper + Dart AOT `--columns mode` vs `joint`, `tools/lane-metrics.mjs`): `all()` (4,674 nodes): 11 lanes in every mode × order, per-mode and joint alike; meanW C greedy 2.62 → 2.66, C compact 2.30 → 2.46 (+7 %), F greedy 2.60 → 2.83 (+9 %), F compact 2.59 → 2.58; crossings C compact 112 → 126, F compact 112 → 115. Default revset (51 nodes): Compact 4 = 4 lanes; Free 3 → 4 lanes (meanW 2.11 → 2.39) — the joint lower bound, since per-mode Compact already needs 4, so exact colouring could not save it. Layout time with the `compact` order at 4.7k: 7.1 → 31.2 ms (C), 6.5 → 32.5 ms (F). The exact colouring is not ported.
- ~~**Router B's cost on the UI isolate is unknown.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-27 (spec-4-15-line-router-b-library, commit 33cd76d): measured at fixture-large to synth-100k, `routeSyncNodeLimit` = 20,000 (sync up to it, `Isolate.run` above); wiring the switch is Story 10.4's view work. Medium.
  - The geometry stage runs per layout and per card size, and track assignment is quadratic in a cluster's size (GL:87-112).
  - **Follow-up:** 4.15's timings at large-10k and synth-100k set the `Isolate.run` threshold (its item 4).
  - **2026-10-27, Story 4.15:** measured AOT (spec-4-15 § Measurements): class + geometry 1.5 ms at fixture-large-all, 3.8 ms (free) at synth-10k, 19.5 ms at synth-50k, 40.7 ms (free) / 27.9 ms (compact) at synth-100k; a bridge window ≤ 20 µs; the quadratic track assignment never shows (clusters stay small). `routeSyncNodeLimit` = 20,000 (synchronous up to it, `Isolate.run` above, GLD §14.5); left: 10.4 wires the switch.
- ~~**Free detours use a unit threshold.**~~ — REJECTED 2026-10-27 (spec-4-15-line-router-b-library): nothing to tune — on the router oracle's 132 cases no direct run is judged differently by the unit rule and GL's pixel test; the rule's card-height bound (H ≤ about 90 px) is stated in GLD §14.2. Low.
  - The export's pixel test leaves room from 13 units at H 62 and from 12 at H 40 (GL:162-172). 4.15's unit rule (12) detours a little more often with compact cards.
  - **Follow-up:** 4.15's oracle lists the cases; tune the rule if they show.
  - **2026-10-27, Story 4.15:** the router oracle (132 cases: the sample and the Lines Study at W 160/240/400 × H 40/62 × both modes) lists none — no direct run is judged differently by the unit rule and GL's pixel test, and all 132 cases equal GL's routing; the rule is not tuned. Left: nothing, unless a real repo shows visible detours where GL's line would sit cleanly above a card.
- ~~**Day dividers can cross cards around midnight.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-31 (spec-4-16-free-day-boundary-band): Free's walk step across a day change is at least one pitch (GLD §4.2 free.3, I5 and D12 amended), so the band around a divider holds no card; the core checkers and the app's `checkLayoutInvariants` (`dayBandClear`) enforce it. Low; accepted in 10.2.
  - Commits on either side of midnight less than about 25 min apart (about 11 min with compact cards) overlap vertically. So SYNC #8's "the band around a divider never holds cards" fails there (GL:597-602; prototype defect 45).
  - **Follow-up:** none, unless real repos show it often.
  - **2026-10-31, Ralph (after a catalogue demo):** fix it in the layout engine — in Free the first card of a new day keeps at least one card height plus gap above the last card of the day before, so the band around a divider holds no card; Story 4.16.
- **Equal timestamps sort differently from the export.** Low; a record.
  - A rebase gives many commits the same committer time. GL orders ties by generation depth, priority and design index (GL:395); the engine orders them by index (D3; LE:324-329). Both keep parents first.
  - Only unrelated commits with identical timestamps can end up at different heights.
  - **Follow-up:** none.
- ~~**Gutter overflow.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-29 (console session, measured on a copy of a real 4.7k-commit repo): one overfull gutter in Free, none in Compact or in the default revset; the budget is not widened (numbers in the sub-bullet below). Low.
  - More than 9 lines on one gutter × step ±6 px each into the neighbouring cards (GL:193-197).
  - **Follow-up:** 4.15 reports it. Widen the gutter's budget only if real repos show it.
  - **2026-10-27, Story 4.15:** reported (`RouteClasses.overfullGutters`; the edges are derivable from `gutters` + `gutterSteps`); none on the fixtures, the design sample or the Lines Study; 8 on synth-10k (free) and 14 / 28 on synth-100k (compact / free), whose outer lines are Compact's only segments behind cards (975 at 100k). The budget is not widened (commit 33cd76d); left: a real repo (none measured — no dump reachable, as for 4.14).
  - **2026-10-29, real repo** (a copy of `dart-intellij-third-party`, `router_cli check`, W 240, `compact` order, H 62 and 40): `all()` (4,674 nodes, 5,010 edges): Compact 0 overfull gutters, 3 gutter lines, 0 card hits; Free 1 overfull gutter, 21 gutter lines, 3 direct fallbacks, 36 (H 62) / 35 (H 40) card hits, all explained. Default revset (51 nodes): 0 overfull gutters in both modes. Class + geometry 1.2 ms at 4.7k (Free). One overfull gutter in 4.7k commits does not justify a wider budget.

### Cards, motion and the minimap

- ~~**Compact's minimap labels need a day per node.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-02 (spec-10-16-graph-minimap, commit 953bd69): the minimap labels Compact rows with their newest commit's day, shown only when older than the label above. Medium.
  - The design labels a Compact row with the local calendar day of its newest commit, shown only when that day is older than the row above's (GV:438-449).
  - 4.11 emits day buckets only for Free (4.11 open question 9), and the view never computes days.
  - **Follow-up:** 4.11 (or 4.14) emits a local day key per node in both modes; 10.16 labels Compact rows from it.
  - **2026-10-27, Story 4.14:** `GraphGridView.nodeDays` gives every node its local day key in both modes (root `noDay`, a stack its newest member's day) (commit 3739e9a); left: 10.16 labels Compact rows from it (which day it reads: "Surfaced in Story 4.14").
- **The Animation Show Case is superseded.** None; a record.
  - AS stays in the export as a playground.
  - Its values are no longer planned (decision (c)): the 250 ms stack slide, the 1.8 px/ms tracing, the 600 ms leave with a 16 px blur and a 16 px rise.
  - **Follow-up:** none. 10.20's demo host keeps the slow-motion switch.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: the demo host becomes the Widgetbook "Graph" folder, and the slow-motion switch is Widgetbook's TimeDilation addon (decision 16.30; Stories 10.1, 10.20, 11.11).)*
- **The design system's id-length rule disagrees with its card.** Low; a record.
  - DS readme.md:65 says change ids have 5 characters on cards.
  - DS ChangeCard and the client show 8 on detailed non-@ cards, and 5 on compact cards and on @ (DS components/graph/ChangeCard.jsx:25; GV:278).
  - **Follow-up:** the next design-system sync. 10.3 keeps 8/5.
- **The design system's legend chip is wider than the decided pill.** Low; a record.
  - DS components/graph/graph.css:22 gives the card's chip `max-width: calc(100% − 20px)`.
  - Decision (g) keeps W/2 − 34 px, painted above the edges (prototype defect 26).
  - **Follow-up:** mention it at the next design-system sync.
- **Nothing shows or sets the horizontal position.** Low.
  - The graph has no scrollbars (FC:18-19), and the minimap moves only vertically (GV:496-499).
  - Only a pan and the @ pill's left or right arrow reach a far column.
  - **Follow-up:** watch it on wide repos in the demo host; add a horizontal hint only if Ralph asks.

### Search

- ~~**The search matches text over every change.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-25 (spec-9-25-graph-search-backend-revset-counts-change-search, commit b6da7c2): `find_changes` over `description(substring-i:)` / `subject()` searches every change at the shown operation. None; a record.
  - 10.21's Change scope and Any's Changes group match subjects and descriptions across all changes (SR:94-126), while 10.11 loads content only for the visible range.
  - **Follow-up:** covered: 9.25 item 2 (`find_changes` over `description(substring-i:)` / `subject()`) is the backend path.
- **Path suggestions see only `@`'s files.** Low.
  - The design gives every mock commit the inspector's changed paths and indexes the paths and folders of all commits (SR:14-20, :94-107). So it also finds deleted and historical paths.
  - 10.21 lists the files of `@`, as the CLI's `jj log` completion does (`cli/src/complete.rs:1143-1152` @ v0.46.0). A typed path or glob still filters the whole history.
  - **Follow-up:** only if it is missed: a backend listing of changed paths over jj-lib's changed-path index (`src/default_index/changed_path.rs`; `files()` already reads it, `revset_engine.rs:1361-1371`).
- **Author suggestions come only from loaded content.** Low.
  - The design lists every author of the repository (SR:96-98, :131-137). Neither Zanshin nor the CLI has an author list.
  - "Authors containing “x”" and typed patterns still reach every author.
  - **Follow-up:** only if it is missed: an op-pinned distinct-author read. It loads every commit, like 4.10's timestamps.
- ~~**Counts on large repositories are unmeasured.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-01 (spec-10-21-graph-search, commit 5258103): File (`files()`) rows are counted only while they are the keyboard row; counts are capped at 10,000, batched per 150 ms typing pause and cached per (operation, expression). Medium.
  - Without a changed-path index, a single `files()` count diffs every commit. At 100k it can hold the serial worker for seconds and delay a reload queued behind it.
  - The cap bounds dense matches only.
  - **Follow-up:** measure 9.25's counts on large-10k (loose and packed) and on the synthetic inputs once 9.25 lands. If they are too slow: count File rows only when they are highlighted, or rely on the changed-path index where the repo has one.
  - **2026-10-25, Story 9.25:** measured (`crates/zanshin-jj/benches/revset_counts.rs`, commit b6da7c2) — at 10k, commit-loading functions ~1.2 s loose / ~33 ms packed, `files()` ~2.4 s loose / ~55 ms packed and ~2 ms with jj's changed-path index; the synthetic inputs have no repository, so nothing can be counted there; left: choosing a mitigation (10.21 / Ralph).
- **Global configurations name one repository's paths and people.** Low.
  - Decision (g) keeps configurations and the default search global, as designed (RC:23, :40). A `files()` or author rule names one repository; in another it matches nothing.
  - A configuration chosen as the default search then opens other repositories on an empty graph, and ✕ leads back to it.
  - **Follow-up:** revisit after use. The options: per-repository configurations, or a default search per repository.
- ~~**No raw revset in the floating client.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-02 (Story 10.28, commit 97d460eacae953f5801fbc660eff2c5cb2e9775e): a seventh rule function `revset` (free text, grouped when joined or negated) in the Configurations window; jj's error text and 9.25's candidates ("Did you mean …") show at its row. Low; deferred (decision (g), Claude's default).
  - The six rule functions cannot express `trunk()..@`, `bookmarks()`, `conflicts()` or a user's own `[revset-aliases]`. Only `revsets.log`, set outside Zanshin, reaches such a revset (as the `jj log` built-in).
  - **Follow-up:** 10.22's design question (b). If it is built, 9.25's `NoSuchFunction` candidates gain a consumer.
  - **2026-11-02, Ralph:** yes, as a seventh rule type → new Story 10.28.
- **Design debris around the search.** None; a record for citations.
  - FC:68-72, :301 and :303 still style the retired `.gtb-select` inside the capsule.
  - AF:5 still describes SYNC #5's variant segment.
  - RC:2 and RC:116 describe the narrowing model that #6 removed.
  - The docked client (`JJ Desktop Client.html`) keeps a `RevsetSelect` with configurations and the docked dialog shell (RC:129-180). It is not the reference (`design/CLAUDE.md:3`).
  - **Follow-up:** none; a later export may clean them up.

### Actions, drag-and-drop and the toast

- **No non-drag path for rebase, bookmark moves and hunk moves.** Medium (accessibility).
  - The design moves changes, bookmarks, files and hunks only by drag, and the panel's "Rebase…" button is gone, so keyboard and VoiceOver users cannot rebase (10.10, 10.27, 10.15; GV:792-930; IN:193-206).
  - Deferred by decision (g). The bookmark popover's "Move to…" (10.15) is the one non-drag move.
  - **Follow-up:** a later story once Ralph wants it.
- **⌘Z works only while a toast is visible.** Low.
  - An app-wide Edit › Undo and Redo (⇧⌘Z = `jj redo`, 9.21) is not designed (GV:757-762; 10.23).
  - **Follow-up:** with 9.21, if Ralph wants a menu-bar Undo.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: undo has three places (decision 16.7): the toast's Undo; ⌘Z and Edit › "Undo ‹action›" for Zanshin's own latest operation while it is still current, with ⇧⌘Z = `jj redo`; the Undo Timeline for older operations. Every undo ends in the toast "Undid ‹action› · Redo" (decision 16.46). The menu bar is the new Story 11.13; Redo needs 9.21.)*
- **The DS UI kit deviates from the client on drag-and-drop.** None; a record.
  - The kit rests 520 ms before the menu (the client: 260 ms), places the menu below the card first (the client: above), and binds ⌘Z always (the client: only while a toast is visible) (DS ui_kits/desktop/app.jsx:272, :305; GV:819, :842, :757-762).
  - **Follow-up:** the client is the reference; raise it at the next design-system sync.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16 for ⌘Z: the spec is now decision 16.7 (DS components/overlays/Toast.prompt.md:14), so neither the client's "only while a toast is visible" nor the kit's "always" holds; 11.13 builds it. The drop menu keeps 260 ms (decision (a4); the DS's `--delay-drop-menu` 520 is not adopted). The kit itself is still wrong; the 2026-10-16 section below records it among the DS leftovers.)*

### The inspector and the diff

- ~~**Viewed state needs a per-repository store.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-01 (spec-10-24-inspector-diff-view, commit 6b6b2e66): `ProjectEntry.viewedFiles` through `ProjectViewedStore` (keys change id : fingerprint : path, newest 2000), next to 10.17's stack toggles. Low.
  - The design keeps "viewed" in `localStorage` per change id and path (SH:56-76). Decision (g) keys it per repository by (change id, path, diff fingerprint), and no store holds that kind of per-repository UI state yet.
  - **Follow-up:** 10.24 picks the store, next to 10.17's per-repository stack toggles (Claude's proposal).
- **jj prints no hunk-header symbol for Dart.** Low.
  - The design shows a symbol on Dart hunks (DF:14, :26). jj's symbol patterns have no Dart (`../jj` v0.46.0 `cli/src/source_symbol.rs:57-93`), so CLI parity shows none.
  - **Follow-up:** none in Zanshin: a Zanshin-only pattern would diverge from the CLI, and an upstream pattern arrives with a pin bump.
- **Line-level selection inside a hunk.** Low.
  - jj's builtin editor toggles single lines (scm-record); the design selects whole hunks. 9.50's ranges could express single lines.
  - **Follow-up:** a later story, if Ralph wants it.
- **Conflicted files have no designed diff view.** Low.
  - `jj diff` materializes conflict markers into the diff text (`cli/src/diff_util.rs:1884`); the design shows only the "Conflict" flag (IN:233).
  - **Follow-up:** 10.24 shows jj's text; a designed conflict view comes with 9.17's `resolve`.
  - **2026-10-24, Story 9.17:** `Repo::resolve_list` / `zanshin_resolve_list` / `RepoHandle.resolveList` give each conflicted file its sides and base hunk by hunk (`ConflictedFile.hunks`, `jj_lib::files::merge_hunks` terms in `Merge` order) plus labels and whether `resolve` can take it (commit aeefd82); left: the designed conflict view itself (Epic 10/11 UI work) and 10.24's jj-text display.
  - **2026-11-01, Story 10.24:** partly done (commit 6b6b2e66) — the inspector's diff shows a conflicted file's hunks as the backend materializes them (jj's conflict-marker text, `crates/zanshin-jj/src/hunks.rs:362`); left: the designed conflict view with 9.17's `resolve`.
- **Word-level and syntax marks have no consumer story.** Low.
  - With line-level first (decision (g)), 5.2–5.4 come later, and no Epic 10 story renders segments.
  - **Follow-up:** a follow-up story to 10.24 when 5.2–5.4 are scheduled.
- **No commit affordance on an empty @.** Low.
  - The composer needs files on @ (IN:177). `jj commit -m` on an empty @ is `describe` plus `new` in one operation (`cli/src/commands/commit.rs:35-36`).
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph decides whether 10.25 shows the composer on an empty @ (Claude's proposal: no; describe and "New change" cover it, in two operations).
  - **2026-11-02, Ralph:** no composer on an empty @ — the title and "New change" already do it (two operations instead of one is fine). Decided; 10.25 is built so.
- ~~**9.47's ledger row `describe_gui_text` retires with 10.8's Save.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-31 (spec-10-8-inspector, I1). Low.
  - The row exists because the GUI was assumed to send the text without the CLI's trailing newline (`crates/zanshin-parity/tests/oracle/ledger.rs:71-78`).
  - **Follow-up:** when 10.8 lands, its scenario sends "S\n\nD\n" and the row goes.
  - **2026-10-31, Story 10.8 (I1):** `describe_gui_text` sends what Save sends ("C described\n\nDetails\n" against `jj describe -m 'C described' -m Details`) and agrees with the CLI; its ledger row is gone, and the CHANGED witness `a_ledgered_aspect_with_other_values_fails` now uses `new_unresolvable_immutable`'s row.
- ~~**A hunk selection's staleness under constant snapshots.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-23 (spec-9-50-hunk-selection-select-tree, Story 9.50, I1; commit 90612be): confirmed — stale is the revision's parent tree no longer being the selection's `left` (`ShowEntry.parent_tree_ids`), or a key that is not a current section, refused `jj.stale_selection` by `select_tree` and by each tree arm; an edit to the revision's own content is not stale (`crates/zanshin-jj/tests/it/select_tree.rs::commit_takes_the_tree_and_leaves_the_rest_and_a_later_edit_in_the_new_at`). Medium.
  - Every verb snapshots @ first (9.26), so an editor that autosaves changes @ between selecting hunks and running the composer. If staleness were judged by @'s commit id, commits would keep failing.
  - **Follow-up:** 9.50 judges staleness by the left side, the revision's parent tree (Claude's proposal); confirm it in 9.50's spec.
- ~~**The combined description must be known before squash runs.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-21 (spec-9-31-drop-actions-capability-query, commit 708deda, I3): every squash row carries `combined_description_needed`; `squash_plan` (9.15) gives the text. Medium.
  - The CLI decides it inside the command and opens an editor (`cli/src/commands/squash.rs:363-396`); the GUI has to ask first (10.25, 10.27).
  - **Follow-up:** 9.15's plan phase returns the combined text when needed (its note); 9.31's `diff` source can carry the flag.
  - **2026-10-19, Story 9.15:** `Repo::squash_plan` / `zanshin_squash_plan` / `RepoHandle.squashPlan` return the text the CLI's editor holds, saved unchanged, from the same `prepare_squash` the verb runs; the oracle's `squash_plan_as_message` passes it back as `-m` against the CLI's editor (commit 96a3b35); left: 9.31's `diff` source carries the flag.
  - **2026-10-21, Story 9.31 (I3):** every squash row (`drop_actions`' `Squash`, `change_actions`' `squash_into_parent`) carries `combined_description_needed`, from ONE rule shared with `squashed_description` (`squash_split::single_description`: Combine mode and `try_combine_messages` → `None`); the UI then asks `squash_plan` for the text.
- ~~**10.8's and 10.9's `handle.dart` citations are stale.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-31 (spec-10-8-inspector, I2). Low.
  - 10.8 cites `handle.dart:690` for `show`; 10.9 cites :228, :242, :257, :271, :284, :861 and :891. The file is `packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/handle.dart`: describe :344, newChange :358, edit :373, abandon :387, undo :407, show :813, bookmarkCreate :986, bookmarkDelete :1016.
  - **Follow-up:** re-point them when each story is refined.
  - **2026-10-31, Story 10.9:** Story 10.9 item 1 in epics.md cites `RepoHandle` method names instead of line numbers (commit 4d32de6a); left: 10.8's citation.
  - **2026-10-31, Story 10.8 (I2):** both 10.8 citations (the 2026-10-14 note's "Depends on" and Scope item 2) name `RepoHandle.show` (`handle.dart:2088` on that day).

### The floating chrome and the shell

### The design system

- **macOS never reports Reduce motion to Flutter.** Medium: every reduce-motion rule in Epic 10 (10.6 item 12, 10.17, 10.19, 10.20) and the splash (`app/lib/src/features/bootstrap/splash_card.dart:54`) is inert on macOS today.
  - Only the iOS embedder sets the flag (`engine/src/flutter/shell/platform/darwin/ios/framework/Source/AccessibilityFeatures.swift`). Nothing under `darwin/macos` or `darwin/common` calls `FlutterEngineUpdateAccessibilityFeatures` (checked in the local SDK checkout `flutter-3.47-candidate.0`, 2026-10-14).
  - Reduce transparency has no Flutter flag at all.
  - **Follow-up:** 11.1 item 7 feeds `MediaQuery.disableAnimations` from `zanshin_shell`, and 11.2 reads `reduceTransparency` from the same place. An upstream Flutter issue only if Ralph wants one.
- **The DS icon files lost `currentColor`.** Low.
  - 46 of the 48 SVGs in `DS assets/icons/ui/`, and all 46 in `design/icons/ui/`, stroke with `#000` and embed a C2PA manifest of about 7.8 KB. Only `change.svg` and `description.svg` keep `currentColor`.
  - `DS components/brand/Icon.jsx:4-72` holds the same geometry with `currentColor`. `ZanshinIcon`'s srcIn tint hides the difference; an icon-font generator or an untinted `SvgPicture` would not.
  - **Follow-up:** 11.1's icon rule copies from `Icon.jsx`. Ask Claude Design to keep `currentColor` only if Ralph wants the files themselves fixed.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: in the DS downloaded 2026-10-16 every glyph is a file with `currentColor`, 58 of them (DS readme.md:344, :350). The app copies the files themselves, stripped of `<metadata>` and `xmlns:c2pa`, into a precompiled SVG pipeline with an `IconRegistry`, and the gate checks their bytes (decisions 16.6, 16.32, 16.33; 11.1 item 11 as amended 2026-10-16).)*
- **Some component values have no token.** Low.
  - `DS readme.md:90` lists radius 13 for glass menus, and `DS components/menus/menus.css:18`, `:36` use it, but `DS tokens/effects.css:5-17` has no such token.
  - The radii 4.5, 5 and 7, the glass field well rgba(8,10,22,.45) and the gold primary's mixes are literals too (`DS components/forms/forms.css:31`, `:38`, `:45-46`; `DS components/graph/graph.css:51`, `:97`; `DS components/actions/actions.css:8-12`).
  - **Follow-up:** 11.1 names them in the token layer, marked as untokenised; the parity test cannot cover them. A DS sync request if Ralph wants them tokenised.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: the DS's design review tokenised them: one radius scale with `--radius-tag` 5, `--radius-chip` 7, `--radius-well` 12 and `--radius-popover` 13 (DS readme.md:321); every mix a control uses is a fixed token, such as `--primary-action` (DS readme.md:298); literal colours became tokens such as `--seg-inset-bg` and `--field-bg` (DS readme.md:286, part C). 11.1's 2026-10-16 note takes them as tokens.)*
- **The dialog entrance differs between the DS and the client.** Low.
  - The DS's `Dialog` enters with `zs-pop` in 160 ms (`DS components/surfaces/surfaces.css:28`).
  - The client's Settings and Configurations dialogs use `gset-in` in 240 ms (`STC:28`, `FC:271`), and so does the DS's own `RevsetDialog` (`DS components/search/search.css:99`). There is no duration token for it.
  - **Follow-up:** 11.2 uses 240 ms (Claude's proposal). The next DS sync can align `Dialog`.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: the DS unified the entrance to `--dur-dialog` 220 ms for Dialog, Settings and Configurations (DS readme.md:302; DS tokens/motion.css:32), and 11.2 uses 220 (its 2026-10-16 note). The only sheets left are ConfirmDialog and Push (decision 16.17); Settings, Projects and Configurations are windows now, with no entrance (decisions 16.13, 16.19; DS readme.md:90).)*
- **The dot grid survives in the DS.** None; a record.
  - `--grid-dot` (`DS tokens/colors.css:38`, `:70`), `--grid-step` (`DS tokens/spacing.css:19`) and `.zs-dots` (`DS components/base.css:7`) remain, although the floating client dropped the grid (`FC:20`; `DS readme.md:84`).
  - **Follow-up:** the token layer carries them for the parity test only; nothing paints them.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: the DS removed `--grid-dot`, `--grid-step` and `.zs-dots` (DS readme.md:327).)*
- **The shadow names are crossed.** Low.
  - `--shadow-popover` is the context menu's shadow (`DS components/menus/menus.css:2`), while `--shadow-menu` belongs to popovers and glass menus (`DS components/surfaces/surfaces.css:10`; `DS components/menus/menus.css:36`).
  - `DS guidelines/elevation.html:11` labels them that way; `DS readme.md:94` calls the latter "menus".
  - **Follow-up:** 11.1 names the Flutter shadows by role (context menu, popover, callout, dialog) and keeps the CSS name beside each.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: one menu material for every menu, popover, callout and the peek: 13 px on `--fl-sheen` over `--fl-bg-solid`, 1 px `--fl-border`, `--shadow-menu`; `--shadow-popover`, `--shadow-callout` and `--bg-elevated` are retired (DS readme.md:90, :284, :322). 11.2 builds it (its 2026-10-16 note).)*
- **`nami_loader_pulse.json` is not in the app.** None; a record.
  - `DS assets/motion/` and `design/lottie/` have it. `app/assets/lottie/` has the other two loaders, byte-identical to the DS's.
  - **Follow-up:** the story that first shows the pulse loader copies it.
- **The app icon is an older Nami variant.** Low.
  - The app uses `app/macos/Runner/NamiIndigo.icon` (`ASSETCATALOG_COMPILER_APPICON_NAME = NamiIndigo` in `app/macos/Runner.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj`). It has one `waves.svg` layer on a Display P3 gradient, with translucency .5.
  - The DS's bundle has three wave layers, #f4cf80, #dca85b and #b58346, on #1d2540 → #131831 (`DS assets/app-icon/README.txt`). Its `icon.json` uses a hand-written schema (angle and stops), not Icon Composer's, so it cannot be dropped in as it is.
  - **Follow-up:** 11.10 item 6 re-authors the bundle with the three layers in Icon Composer's format. Ralph checks it in the Dock.
  - **2026-10-30, Story 11.10:** `NamiIndigo.icon` re-authored in Icon Composer's schema with Wave-Top/Mid/Bottom stroked in their golds (no solid fills, which render crescents), compiled and rendered with actool (commit 9a7f3c6); left: Ralph's check in the Dock.
- **DS prompt examples show retired parts.** Low.
  - `DS components/surfaces/Island.prompt.md:5` still uses `CommandField`, retired in syncs #5 and #6.
  - `DS components/surfaces/Popover.prompt.md:6` offers the Layout "Compact, Time"; Time is gone since #7.
  - **Follow-up:** mention it with the next DS sync. Stories cite the components' code, not these examples.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: the DS fixed `Island.prompt.md` (GraphSearch, not CommandField) and `Popover.prompt.md` (Free · Compact, not Time) (DS readme.md:303).)*
- ~~**Density has no tokens.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-07 (spec-11-20-density-spacing-tokens, commit 8b364e7a): ZsMetrics carries the three density sets (rows +0/+3/+6, graph cards, gaps), switched live in every window; the DS tokens sit in tokens/spacing.css under the parity test; density no longer scales text. Low.
  - The DS's Settings card has a Density row, Compact · Balanced · Comfy (`DS components/settings/settings.card.html:30`), and its UI kit defaults to compact.
  - No DS component reads it. In the client it changes only the body font size (`AC:69-71`, `:80`).
  - **Follow-up:** 11.7's design question (a) asks Ralph whether the row stays. The token layer carries the DS's compact sizes only.
  - **2026-11-03, Ralph:** the row stays (11.7 built it as a text scale); spacing tokens per density, with a design round first → **Story 11.20**. Covered by Story 11.20.

## Surfaced 2026-10-16 while validating the design-system rounds 1–3 and folding them into Epics 9–11 (console session, Ralph)

_Ralph re-downloaded the design-system project twice (rounds 2 and 3; the DS readme still says "Synced through #9") and validated each round against the stories (V1 = `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/research/design-system-validation-2026-10-15.md`, V2 = `…-round-2-2026-10-16.md`, V3 = `…-round-3-2026-10-16.md`; RC = `technical-ui-controls-yaru-material-custom-and-macos-accents-research-2026-10-15.md`). On 2026-10-16 he accepted every recommendation of the three reports, Claude's proposals included ("mit deinen Vorschlägen"). The stories were updated the same day: the decisions 16.1–16.47 in the Epic 11 header, a "Design update 2026-10-16" in the Epic List and the Epic 10 header (with the new house rule Commands), new Stories 11.11 (Zs control library, Widgetbook catalogue, Marionette), 11.12 (window foundation) and 11.13 (menu bar and shortcuts), and dated notes in 9.20, 9.21, 9.23, 9.32, most Epic 10 stories and 11.1–11.10. `DS <path>:<line>` below refers to the DS downloaded 2026-10-16 (round 3). Ten entries of the 2026-10-14 section and one older entry above are marked resolved. The items below fall outside those stories:_

  - The DS dates its own rounds differently from this file ("RUNDE 2026-10-16", "RUNDE 2026-10-17"; DS readme.md:260, :227). Commit `b49a5cd` records the 2026-10-16 download and is the anchor for the citations dated 2026-10-16 (Epic 10 header, "The reference").

  - GraphSearch.prompt.md:15 (the pill; 10.21 follows rule A2, DS readme.md:140); StaleNotice.prompt.md:9 `--warn` and :11 "· Undo" (no Undo after `update-stale`, decision 16.28); Icon.prompt.md:13 (the rx rule); surfaces.card.html:34 (⌘, on the Appearance row; decision 16.14 shows none); settings.card.html (the 18 px sheet; Settings is a window); Window.prompt.md:14, :17, :18 ("both float above", two windows).

  - Push: PushDialog.jsx:218 drops the `--allow-*` flags; readme.md:217 and :219 claim behaviour jj v0.46.0 does not have (decision 16.25).
  - `--delay-drop-menu` 520 against the decided 260 (decision (a4) of 2026-10-14); ConfirmDialog's body does not name the evolog loss of `jj op abandon` (DS ConfirmDialog.prompt.md:4–5; 9.21); menu examples with bare commands (decisions 16.39, 16.44); the menu bar's Title Case (readme.md:200–207) against the sentence-case rule (readme.md:49; decision 16.42 (o) keeps Title Case for the menu bar only); the pressed scope pill in night yellow at 4.40:1 (left as is under decision 16.12); the Fetch card's ⇧⌘F (decision 16.42 (m)); "branch" in ProjectRow and ProjectMenu (11.5 says "bookmark"); ResizeEdge's "detach" and "splitter" (decisions 16.23, 16.24).

### Declined or deferred by Ralph

- **A Workspaces section in the Project menu and a `<name>@` go-to.** None; declined by Ralph on 2026-10-15 (decision 16.27; round-2 prompt header item 4; V2 §6).
  - Workspaces stay visible in two places without a new surface: `name@` on cards (10.3, decision 16.28) and the workspace name in Undo Timeline rows (11.9 item 5).
- **Reads with `--ignore-working-copy`.** None; declined (CLI parity, decision 16.28).
  - While the working copy is stale only the no-snapshot reads work (`commit_contents`, `log_defaults`, `workspace_root`); the way out is 9.23's `update-stale`.
- **Patrol and Patrol MCP.** Low; deferred (decision 16.31).
  - **Follow-up — revisit trigger:** Patrol's `$.platform.macos` ships AND its binding works with a real windowing owner (several views). Until then Marionette 0.7.0 plus the CGWindowList probe cover it.

### Windows and the shell

- **Configurations floats over Settings and Projects too.** Low; watch (decision 16.42 (b), Claude's proposal; 11.12 item 6).
  - The `revset-configs` window takes `.floating` while Zanshin is active, so it also sits above Settings and Projects.
  - **Follow-up:** if that annoys in use, float it only above the project window, for example by lowering it while Settings or Projects is key (Claude's proposal).
- ~~**Blur-triggered settings writes fire on every window switch.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-02 (spec-11-7-settings-window, commit 9522ef8): a Settings field writes on ↩ or blur only when its value changed. Low (11.7, 11.12 item 9; r3out windows.md C7).
  - Every window switch blurs the focused text field, so 11.7's write-on-blur rows write each time the user moves to another Zanshin window.
  - **Follow-up:** check it in 11.7; write only when the value changed (Claude's proposal).
- ~~**Settings' top-anchored resize needs native work.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-19 (spec-11-12-window-foundation-dialog-windows, commit 0dbd2fb): the runner's position-only frame keeps the top edge (`WindowFrameStore.swift`), seen on the catalogue's Content-sized scaffold (170 → 362 → 170 pt, top held); the app root's content-sized switch for Settings is 11.7's (Surfaced in Story 11.12). Medium (11.12 item 3; r4out windows W-6).
  - `setContentSize` keeps the bottom-left origin, so a Settings window whose height follows its tab moves its top edge. The runner has to re-anchor the top.
  - **Follow-up:** 11.12 builds it; if it flickers, a fixed height per tab is the fallback (Claude's proposal).
- **Several workspaces of one repository in the project registry.** Low; open (r3out workstream.md critique 2, MISSED).
  - The registry (11.5) is keyed by path. With no Workspaces section (decision 16.27) a user can still add a sibling workspace's folder. Whether it is its own entry or a child of its repository is not decided.
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph decides when 11.5 is refined.

### Tooling: Widgetbook, Marionette, icons

- **Widgetbook's dev plugins reach the release build.** Low (11.11; decision 16.29).
  - Widgetbook's dev-only plugins (`url_launcher_macos`) end up in release builds (flutter#163874), and adding it changes the app's `GeneratedPluginRegistrant`. Catalogue builds also run the `zanshin_core` build hook.
  - **Follow-up:** open. Once 11.11 adds Widgetbook, check whether `url_launcher_macos` ships in the release bundle, and keep it out if it does (Claude's proposal; 11.11 does not cover it).
  - **2026-10-18, Story 11.11:** checked — it ships: Widgetbook adds `url_launcher_macos` to `app/macos/Flutter/GeneratedPluginRegistrant.swift` and the generated SwiftPM plugin package, which serve every build mode, so it links into a release `Zanshin.app` (flutter_tools filters dev plugins only on Android, Linux and Windows; flutter#163874) (commit ff1ccc6); left: Ralph decides how to keep it out — accept it until flutter#163874 lands (registered, never called), a pure-Dart stub override of `url_launcher_macos` in the workspace root, or the catalogue as its own app package (against 11.11's non-goal).
- **Marionette's limits.** Low (decision 16.31; r3out tooling.md).
  - Coordinate gestures still hit the first view; screenshots are "all views, in attach order", unlabeled and Flutter-layer only; bounds carry no view id. Hence the rule: match by Semantics identifier or text, never by coordinates, and scope listings with `ancestor_keys` on keyed window roots.
  - **Follow-up:** retire the marionette fork branch `fix/multi-view-gestures` once `marionette_flutter` ^0.7.0 is in (11.11 non-goal).
- **The IconRegistry needs a hot restart.** Low (decision 16.32).
  - The registry loads the precompiled icons once at boot, so a changed icon shows only after a hot restart.
  - **Follow-up:** measure the paint cost of `ZanshinIcon`'s saveLayer per icon on a large graph; batch or cache only if it shows in frame times (Claude's proposal).

### Platform and debt

- **Flutter on macOS still does not report Reduce motion.** Medium; still open (the 2026-10-14 entry "macOS never reports Reduce motion to Flutter" above).
  - Decision 16.4 makes reduce motion a per-property rule; it stays inert on macOS until 11.1 item 7 feeds `MediaQuery.disableAnimations` from `zanshin_shell`.
- **Selected glyphs in read-only text keep their colour.** Low; known debt (decision 16.10).
  - Text fields recolour selected glyphs through `buildTextSpan`. Read-only text (the diff, ids) paints only the highlight for now.
  - **Follow-up:** a later story, if the contrast is missed (DS readme.md:175–179 measures it).

The only exception is the deliberately historical `tests/it/graph.rs:867`.

## Surfaced in Story 11.1 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-17)

- ~~**A real Multicolor Mac resolves to the Blue accent family until Story 11.4 adds the Runner AccentColor asset.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-18 (spec-11-4-window-chrome-floating-layout, commit fdd9817): the runner's asset catalogue holds the `AccentColor` colour set (#4757a1, dark #5162ad) and the Runner target sets `ASSETCATALOG_COMPILER_GLOBAL_ACCENT_COLOR_NAME`, so the built Info.plist names `NSAccentColorName` = `AccentColor` (`app/test/macos/runner_chrome_assets_test.dart`); the exact-match item below stays unverified until a Multicolor Mac reports. Medium.

## Surfaced in Story 11.2 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-17)

- **The cost of glass (design question (a)) is not measured: profile-mode frame times over a scrolling 10k graph with the six islands need 10.1's synthetic recording and 11.3's host.** Medium.
  - epics.md Story 11.2 Design status (a) and its witness "In profile mode, frame build and raster times over a scrolling 10k graph with six islands are recorded, not gated"; `sprint-status.yaml` lists `10-1-graph-viewport-2d-virtualized` and `11-3-floating-overlay-host` as backlog.
  - **Follow-up:** once 10.1 and 11.3 land, measure in profile mode with the three top-bar islands, the rail, the inspector and the minimap open, record the numbers in that story's notes; a missed frame budget goes to Ralph (solid while scrolling, or fewer blurred surfaces).
  - **2026-11-01, Ralph:** handed to Story 10.12 — it mounts all six islands in the project window, so its run measures (10.1 is done). Covered by Story 10.12.
  - **2026-11-02, console session (Ralph asked):** measured with five islands (no minimap yet) over a scrolling synthetic 10k graph, profile mode, M4 Pro at 60 Hz (commit 4d41b41e; `docs/perf/glass-profile-2026-11-02.md`, harness `app/integration_test/glass_profile.dart`): glass raster avg 5.8 / p99 6.6 ms vs solid 2.5 / 3.4 ms, build ≤ 4.3 ms; 16.7 ms budget holds (1–3 frames of ~17 ms per 600, one 86 ms frame after the cold build). No decision needed; left: re-measure with the minimap (10.16) and at 120 Hz on the built-in display.
  - **2026-10-29, Ralph:** 10.1 and 11.3 are done, but the measurement waits until the inspector (10.8) and the minimap (10.16) exist, so all six islands are open. Covered by Stories 10.8 and 10.16.
- ~~**Fading a glass surface through an Opacity ancestor drops its backdrop blur for the whole fade, and 11.3's calm scrolling plus the panel, toast and minimap entrances fade glass.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-02 (spec-10-16-graph-minimap, commit 953bd69): the minimap, the last surface left, fades its glass only through `ZsFade`; a test checks no `Opacity` or `FadeTransition` sits above it. Medium.
  - Engine reading, not run on a device: Impeller opens an offscreen subpass for an opacity layer that holds a backdrop filter (`engine/src/flutter/impeller/display_list/canvas.cc` `SaveLayer`, :1830-1912), and `FlipBackdrop` reads only that subpass's texture (`render_passes_.back()`), which is still empty; `ZsSheet` (Story 11.2) fades its scrim through the filter's alpha instead.
  - **Follow-up:** 11.3 (calm scrolling, panel-in), 10.16 and 10.23 fade glass the same way (alpha in the filter's colour matrix plus an opacity on the content inside the `BackdropFilter`), e.g. through an opacity input on `ZsGlass`; confirm the behaviour on a Mac first.
  - **2026-10-18, Story 11.3:** `ZsFade` is the one way to fade glass — each `ZsSurface` under it puts its box under an `Opacity` inside the filter, gives the filter the alpha a(1 − Lα)/(1 − a·Lα) and fades its drops by colour; calm scrolling, the panel's entrance and exit, the flyout, the minimap slot's motion and the stale notice use it; confirmed on a Mac in the running catalogue (through `ZsFade` the backdrop stays blurred, under an `Opacity` ancestor it turned sharp) (commit 7ca412b); left: 10.16 (minimap) and 10.23 (toast) fade their own glass through `ZsFade`.
  - **2026-11-01, Story 10.23:** the toast fades its glass only through `ZsFade`, a test checks that no `Opacity` sits above it (commit e2da414); left: 10.16 (minimap).
- ~~**The DS drop menu enters on its own keyframe (--dur-pop, scale .94), while the epics' design update lists it among the command menus (--dur-menu, scale .97); 11.2 follows the epics.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-03 (spec-drag-leftovers-alt-announce-entrance-tracked, commit ebc3f36995e60b08fdce5760dc3a0b12eafd4e8c; Ralph: like the command menus): the DS dropped `zs-drop-in`, `GraphDropMenu` enters on `--dur-menu`, glide, from scale .97. ## Surfaced 2026-10-17 while reviewing where 11.1 and 11.2 put their code (console session, Ralph)

_Ralph asked whether the design, theme and UI code that 11.1 and 11.2 put in `app/lib/src/shared/` should live in packages of their own. He decided yes: Story 11.14 moves the tokens, the materials and the DS icons into `packages/zanshin_ui` before 11.11, and 11.11 builds its controls there. It supersedes 11.1's design question (a) ("a package only once a second consumer outside the app appears"). Epics.md and sprint-status.yaml carry it; what follows is only what 11.14 leaves open._

- **`zanshin_ui` depends on `zanshin_settings` and `zanshin_shell`.** Low; not in 11.14's scope (its non-goal).
  - `zs_appearance.dart` reads `AccentSource` from the settings package and `ShellTheme` from the shell package, and `zs_root.dart` reads `ShellTheme`. So a change to either package reruns the UI package's gate steps, and the design layer knows the settings model.
  - **Follow-up:** if it ever hurts, the appearance scope takes plain values (an accent-source enum of its own, a theme snapshot), and the app maps the settings and the shell onto them.
- **Older paths in this file.** Info. The "Surfaced in Story 11.1" and "Surfaced in Story 11.2" sections cite `app/lib/src/shared/design/`, `materials/` and `widgets/`. After 11.14 those files are in `packages/zanshin_ui/lib/src/design/`, `materials/` and `icons/`; read the old paths that way. The sections stay as written.

## Surfaced in Story 11.14 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-17)

- ~~**The gate-plan rules list each Dart package's dependents by hand, and nothing checks them against the pubspecs, so a new path dependency makes the gate under-plan without an error.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-17 (spec-11-14-closing-gate-rules-and-font-licences, commit cc8f353): an inline test derives each workspace member's dependents from the pubspecs and checks every package rule against them; a changed member pubspec now plans every step. Medium.
  - `crates/xtask/src/tasks/gate_plan.rs` `RULES` hard-codes the dependents of `zanshin_settings`, the three shell packages and `zanshin_ui`; the inline test `every_package_zanshin_ui_depends_on_plans_zanshin_ui_and_the_app` hard-codes the same paths. Pre-existing since `d81ebd9`; 11.14 added one more package.
  - **Follow-up:** an inline test that reads the `path:` dependencies of `app/pubspec.yaml` and `packages/*/pubspec.yaml`, follows them transitively and asserts every package rule plans each dependent's group (the test module already reads `Makefile.toml` that way). Worth doing before 11.11 adds a dependency to `zanshin_ui`.
- ~~**The OFL texts of Inter, JetBrains Mono and Shippori Mincho may not reach the shipped app's licence notices.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-17 (spec-11-14-closing-gate-rules-and-font-licences, commit 4795404): the TTF name tables fall short of OFL §2, so `zanshin_ui` lists the three `OFL.txt` under `flutter: licenses:`, and an app test checks the bundle's `NOTICES.Z` holds each verbatim. Medium (unverified).
  - The debug bundle's `NOTICES.Z` holds no OFL text; `packages/zanshin_ui/assets/fonts/*/OFL.txt` sit next to the fonts, but no `LICENSE` file or `LicenseRegistry` entry brings them in. Pre-existing before the move.
  - **Follow-up:** check whether the licence fields in the TTFs' name tables satisfy the OFL; if not, add a `LICENSE` to `packages/zanshin_ui` (flutter_tools collects package licences into the notices) or register the texts with `LicenseRegistry.addLicense`.

## Surfaced in the closing run of Story 11.14 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-17)

- **The Shippori Mincho subset lost the name table's licence description and licence URL (name IDs 13 and 14), so the two shipped TTFs carry only their copyright line.** Low.
  - `packages/zanshin_ui/assets/fonts/Shippori_Mincho/ShipporiMincho-{Medium,SemiBold}.ttf` hold only name IDs 0–6 (pyftsubset's default `--name-IDs`; the command is in `SUBSET.txt`), while Inter and JetBrains Mono keep 13/14. The OFL text now ships in the bundle's NOTICES, so the licence still travels with the app; OFL-FAQ 2.4/2.8 ask a modified font to keep its licence metadata.
  - **Follow-up:** re-run the subset with `--name-IDs='*'` (or `0-6,13,14`) from the full fonts in git history and note it in `SUBSET.txt`.
- **The fonts' OFL entries in the NOTICES belong to no package, so a licence page (`LicensePage`, `showAboutDialog`) would not list them.** Low.
  - The three `OFL.txt` files use CRLF line endings; Flutter's runtime parser (`services/binding.dart` `_parseLicenses`) splits a chunk's package names off at the first `\n\n`, finds none and files each OFL text under no package. The texts are in `NOTICES.Z` (the OFL's containment condition holds); the app has no licence page yet, and before the closing run the texts were not in the NOTICES at all.
  - **Follow-up:** with the story that adds a licence page or About box: register the texts under their family names (an LF copy headed by the name, or `LicenseRegistry.addLicense`).
- ~~**The app bundles Yaru's Ubuntu and Ubuntu Mono fonts without the Ubuntu Font Licence in its NOTICES.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-30 (spec-11-10-retire-docked-mock-ui, commit 9a7f3c6): Yaru and its fonts are retired; `font_manifest_test.dart` checks the bundle lists no Yaru, Ubuntu or `Zanshin` family. Medium.
  - The test bundle's `FontManifest.json` lists `packages/yaru/Ubuntu` and `packages/yaru/UbuntuMono`; the gunzipped `NOTICES.Z` holds no "UBUNTU FONT LICENCE". Ralph's fork (`../yaru.dart/assets/fonts/LICENCE.txt`) has the text but no `flutter: licenses:` entry. UFL 1.0 asks every copy to carry the notice and the licence, like OFL §2. Pre-existing.
  - **Follow-up:** Story 11.10 retires Yaru and its fonts; if Yaru stays past it, add `flutter: licenses: [assets/fonts/LICENCE.txt]` to the fork's pubspec.

## Surfaced in the closing run of Story 11.3 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-18)

- **`ZanshinFfiModule`'s version wrapper never frees the Rust-allocated C string it reads, against `zanshin_ffi_version`'s caller-frees contract.** Low.
  - `packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/ffi/zanshin_version.dart:15` returns `bindings.zanshin_ffi_version().cast<Utf8>().toDartString`, a tear-off over a pointer never passed to `zanshin_string_free` (contract: `bindings.dart:792`, `crates/zanshin-ffi/src/lib.rs:65-72`, `docs/ffi-abi.md:28`). Pre-existing; since the closing run no production code calls it, only `app/test/native_asset_smoke_test.dart:12` and `packages/zanshin_core/test/ffi/zanshin_version_test.dart:26`.
  - **Follow-up:** copy the string and free the pointer in the wrapper, or retire `ZanshinFfiModule` in favour of `GetDiagnostics` (`diagnostics.dart:15-29` already carries `appVersion` and `gitSha` and frees its buffer); the raw-path test in `zanshin_version_test.dart:36-47` stays the extern fn's coverage witness either way.
- **Two DI bindings in `MainComponent`, `GetDiagnostics` and `IconRegistry`, are registered but consumed by nothing, so every build_runner run in `app/` prints two unused-binding warnings.** Low.
  - The regenerated `app/lib/main.inject.dart` builds `getDiagnostics$Provider` and `iconRegistry$Provider` and hands them to nothing (modules at `app/lib/main.dart:118-124`); the warnings were also in Story 11.3's build_runner output. Pre-existing.
  - **Follow-up:** the first story that consumes diagnostics or the registry through DI wires it; otherwise drop the unconsumed modules from the component, so a new unused-binding warning stands out.
  - **2026-10-18, closing run of Story 11.6:** `GetDiagnostics` now has a DI consumer, the Projects environment's `jjLibVersion()` (commit 23ddab3); left: `IconRegistry`.

## Surfaced in Story 11.4 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-18)

- **AppKit posts no notification when entering full screen fails, so the window chrome watch takes a full-screen entry that has not arrived after 2 s as failed.** Low.
  - The spec's trigger list (spec-11-4-window-chrome-floating-layout, Design Notes "Measurement") names `didFailToEnterFullScreen`; `NSWindow` has no such notification (only the delegate's `windowDidFailToEnterFullScreen(_:)`, and the engine owns the delegate), and `flutter build macos` failed on it. `WindowChromeHost.swift` clears its entering flag on `didEnterFullScreen` / `didExitFullScreen` and, failing both, after `enterFullScreenTimeout` (2 s, Claude's proposal).
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph's full-screen check (⌃⌘F in and out) covers the normal path; if a failed entry ever shows the bar at 12 for those 2 s, or the engine starts forwarding window-delegate calls, switch to the delegate.
- **The native half of Story 11.4 is verified only by `flutter build macos --debug`, one live block report and the asset test: the rings' colour and theme switch, full screen, the opaque rounded window, the 880 × 560 drag stop and Multicolor's native tint were not seen from this seat.** Medium.
  - No Swift test target exists (see the Story 11.1 entry above on `Package.swift`), and this seat has neither screen recording nor accessibility; the running app's log reported the block x 19–79, centre y 26, for view 2, and Marionette read the bar at 91 / 4–48 and the drag row at 0–60. This Mac runs the Orange accent, so the AccentColor exact-match item above stays unverified.
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph's manual checks as listed in spec-11-4-window-chrome-floating-layout § Verification; later a Swift test target or a runtime smoke check.
- ~~**The catalogue's windows wear the app's chrome but their roots have no `WindowChromeBuilder`, so the real Windows › Floating shell window lays the bar out from the reference geometry and ignores full screen.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-18 (spec-11-4-closing-catalogue-window-chrome, commit 84a47af): `CatalogueWindowRoot` wraps the catalogue windows' root in `WindowChromeBuilder`, `catalogueAppBuilder` copies full screen and the block into its `ZsRoot`, and `FloatingShellDemo` no longer forces `fullScreen: false`. Low. (Surfaced in the review.)
  - `app/widgetbook/folders/windows.dart` opens it through `AppWindows.create` (`chrome: true`, `app/lib/src/features/window/app_windows.dart`); its root is `catalogueAppBuilder`, a `ZsRoot` without the builder (`app/widgetbook/catalogue.dart`), so in full screen the first island stays at 91 / 4 and the drag row at 60.
  - **Follow-up:** 11.12's per-window roots wrap every window's `ZsRoot` in `WindowChromeBuilder`, the catalogue's included.

  - **2026-10-19, Story 11.12:** interim (Claude's proposal): an open whose outcome carries warnings keeps Projects with its notice and brings it forward after the new project window's first focus (2 s at most); without warnings Projects closes (commit 0dbd2fb); left: 10.23's toast takes the report, and Projects then always closes.

Low.
  - No config-change event exists (9.28's verbs are request/response only); 11.7 will write identity rows; an external `jj config set` is invisible until the next read.
  - **Follow-up:** 11.7 re-reads after its identity writes (or the content re-reads on window activation, 11.12) (`app/lib/src/features/projects/projects_window.dart`, the footer).
  - **2026-10-19, Story 11.12:** the Projects content reads the identity again when its window becomes key (its appearance turns from inactive to active) (commit 0dbd2fb); left: 11.7 re-reads after its own identity writes.
- ~~**Clone shows no progress and cannot be cancelled, and the CLI's destination rules (create the folder, refuse a non-empty one, remove a folder it created after a failure) run app-side until 9.20 brings them into the verb.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-03 (spec-11-8-sync-fetch-push-poll-badge, commit 3516be5): ProjectSession.clone's destination port is gone and the clone form shows live progress and Cancel; Ralph's confirmation of how Cancel identifies git stays under "Surfaced in Story 9.52". Low.
  - **2026-10-20, Story 9.20 (commit 9066268):** ~~the destination rules in the verb~~ — `cloneRepo` now applies the CLI's destination rules and clean-up scope itself; ~~progress over the bridge~~ — it reports progress (`operation: "clone"`, the new handle's session id), delivered after the call returns ("Surfaced in Story 9.20", live delivery). Left open: cancel (jj-lib 0.46.0 has no hook; Ralph decides the mechanism), removing `ProjectSession.clone`'s app-side destination port, and showing progress in the form.
  - `ZanshinWorker.cloneRepo({url, targetPath})` takes no progress or cancel and requires an existing target, accepting a non-empty one (`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/isolate.dart:421`; 9.20 backlog); the CLI's rules are `cli/src/commands/git/clone.rs:186-196`, `:259-270` @ v0.46.0; `ProjectSession.clone` ports them (`app/lib/src/features/projects/project_session.dart`).
  - **Follow-up:** 9.20 gives `cloneRepo` the CLI's destination rules, progress (6.4a's callback) and cancel; the session's port then goes and the form shows progress (`project_session.dart`, `projects_window.dart`).
  - **2026-10-20, Ralph:** cancel = kill git's process group (git first gets a group of its own; jj-lib spawns it with a plain `Command::new`) → **Story 9.52**; removing `ProjectSession.clone`'s port and showing progress in the form → **Story 11.8** item 10.
  - **2026-10-20, Story 9.52:** live clone progress and Cancel (`NetworkOperation.cancel()`, `jj.cancelled`, the clone removes the folder it created) (commit 910179b); left: removing `ProjectSession.clone`'s port and progress/Cancel in the form (11.8 item 10), and Ralph's confirmation of how Cancel identifies git ("Surfaced in Story 9.52").
- ~~**A successful clone is witnessed only on a fake port in the app: under `dart`/`flutter test` the VM reaps git's child, so jj-lib's fetch fails; whether the running app's clone works is unverified.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-20 (manual check, console session): the running debug app (`flutter run -d macos`) cloned a local `file://` bare repository through the Projects window's clone form and the real worker (Story 9.52's helper isolate): the folder was created, `README.md` checked out, the worker logged `repo cloned session=1 → handleId=0` and the project window opened on the new handle; no "Failed to wait for the git process" in the log. The child-reaping failure is limited to `dart test` / `flutter test`. Medium.
  - `packages/zanshin_core/test/worker/creation_test.dart:63-90` (only the refusal runs); this file's "No Dart real-cdylib witness for a SUCCESSFUL clone" (the running app); Rust witnesses `crates/zanshin-ffi/tests/repo_ffi.rs:280`, `repo_write_ffi.rs:679-725`.
  - **Follow-up:** clone a `file://` bare repository from the running app (11.12's Projects window or the catalogue's Windows › Projects window over the real worker); if it fails with "Failed to wait for the git process", fix the child reaping for the app (`app/lib/src/features/projects/worker_project_repos.dart`, clone).

  - `packages/zanshin_shell_macos/test/zanshin_shell_macos_test.dart` pins the method names and argument maps against a mocked channel; `ZanshinShellPlugin.swift` (`chooseDirectory`, `revealInFinder`) and `DirectoryPanel.swift` have no test harness (`app/macos/RunnerTests` is a stub); the run's visual check could not drive the panel (Marionette taps reach the catalogue's view only).
  - **Follow-up:** a manual check in the catalogue's Windows › Projects window: Add directory opens the panel as a sheet on that window with an "Add" button and adds the chosen folder; Clone's Choose… shows "Choose"; ••• › Reveal in Finder selects the folder in Finder (Ralph, or 11.12's run on its CGWindowList probe seat).
