## Surfaced in Story 11.12 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-18)

- ~~**A window sized to its content cannot hold a Navigator: its Overlay takes the largest size the window's constraints allow, so the window never shrinks to its content.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-02 (spec-11-7-settings-window, commit 9522ef8): ZanshinWindowRoot(sizeToContent:) uses Overlay.wrap with no navigator for Settings. Medium.
  - `_RenderTheater.performLayout` sizes to `constraints.biggest` whenever that is finite and `alwaysSizeToContent` is off (`flutter/lib/src/widgets/overlay.dart`); a content-sized dialog has finite maximum constraints (r3out windows.md C3), and `MaterialApp(home:)` builds a Navigator over such an Overlay. `ZanshinWindowRoot` (`app/lib/src/features/window/zanshin_window_root.dart`) still puts the content under `home:` (every app window is sized today); the catalogue's Content-sized scaffold uses `MaterialApp(builder:)` with `Overlay.wrap(alwaysSizeToContent: true)` and no navigator (`catalogueAppBuilder(sizeToContent:)`, `app/widgetbook/catalogue.dart`), and grew 170 → 362 → 170 pt on screen.
  - **Follow-up:** 11.7 gives `ZanshinWindowRoot` the same switch for the Settings window (Claude's proposal).
- ~~**A window sized to its content takes no minimum constraints: the engine's first metrics carry the still empty view, and metrics below a minimum are refused, so the window never gets its first frame.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-02 (spec-11-7-settings-window, commit 9522ef8): settingsWindowSpec = maxWidth 620 / maxHeight 740, not resizable, position frame, no minimum. Low.
  - Seen on the catalogue's Content-sized scaffold with `minWidth: 620`: `FlutterEngineSendWindowMetricsEvent` returned `kInvalidArguments` ("Width and height must be within the specified constraints") and the window never showed (`FlutterEngine.mm` `updateWindowMetricsForViewController:` sends the view's bounds with the sizing delegate's min/max). `AppWindows.create` now refuses such a spec with an `ArgumentError` (`app/lib/src/features/window/app_windows.dart`); the width comes from the content (a stretched column takes `maxWidth`).
  - **Follow-up:** 11.7 passes Settings as `DialogWindowSpec(constraints: BoxConstraints(maxWidth: 620, maxHeight: 740), resizable: false, frame: WindowFrameMode.position)`; DS readme.md:189's "620 wide" holds through the content.
- **The window coordinator's native paths are witnessed only by Dart tests: the project window's red button, the Dock reopen, a launch that reopens a stored project (and one whose reopen fails), closing Projects to quit, and a theme switch restyling every native window.** Medium.
  - `app/test/features/window/app_window_coordinator_test.dart` and `app_windows_test.dart` drive the delegates and the `reopen` channel on flutter_test's windowing owner; the run's native check saw the launch with nothing stored (Projects as the only window), the catalogue's dialog windows and the floating level, but cannot press a traffic light or the Dock (no click injection on this seat) and opened no real repository (Ralph's registry is empty); no in-app theme switch exists before 11.7.
  - **Follow-up:** a manual pass on the running app: add a project in Projects (it closes, the project window opens), red button (Projects returns, the app keeps running), red button on Projects (quits), relaunch (the project window first), minimise and click the Dock (it comes back); and the theme switch once 11.7 has it.
- ~~**Projects' header centres its controls on the row's middle (y 26), not on the traffic-light block's line `--tl-mid-y`, so with a block other than the reference they sit off the buttons' line.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-19 (spec-11-12-closing-projects-scaffold-window-plan, commit 630f3f5): the Projects content uses `ZsWindowScaffold`'s sidebar form, so its header follows the measured traffic-light line; a widget test pins y 27 for a block centred at 27. Low.
  - `ProjectsWindow._header` (`app/lib/src/features/projects/projects_window.dart`) lays a `Row` into the 52 pt `SizedBox`; `ZsWindowScaffold` shifts its row by `(tl-mid-y − hd/2)·2` as window.css:29 does (`packages/zanshin_ui/lib/src/materials/zs_window_scaffold.dart`). On macOS 27's reference block both give 26.
  - **Follow-up:** move the Projects content onto `ZsWindowScaffold` (its sidebar form) when it is next touched (Claude's proposal).
- **A dialog window's first frame may show the Multicolor accent: every window root makes its own view model, which asks the shell for the theme asynchronously.** Low (unverified).
  - `ZanshinWindowRoot` builds a `ZanshinAppViewModel` per window through the DI's `ViewModelFactory`; until `ZanshinShell.getTheme` answers, `ZsAccentFamily.resolve` gives Multicolor (`packages/zanshin_ui/lib/src/design/zs_appearance.dart`). The engine shows a window on its first frame. The run's screenshots of opened windows showed the right accent, taken after the answer.
  - **Follow-up:** if a flash shows on a non-Multicolor Mac, seed a new root with the last report (one shared view model, or the last `ShellTheme` passed in) (Claude's proposal).
- ~~**The engine-bound window creation paths — the boot host's Projects window and `createRememberedWindow`'s mapping of a `DialogWindowSpec` (chrome kind, resizable, frame, level) onto the controllers — run in no test.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-19 (spec-11-12-closing-projects-scaffold-window-plan, commit 076c75e): `planNativeWindow` maps an `AppWindowRequest` to a `ProjectWindowPlan` / `DialogWindowPlan` that tests pin per window kind, and `ZanshinWindowHost.openProjects` runs in a test over the fake factory; the controllers' engine-bound `create(plan)` is the closing run's own deferral below. Medium. (Surfaced in the review.)
  - Every window test injects `FakeWindowFactory`, and the catalogue tests override `openOrActivate`; `ZanshinWindowHost.openProjects` (`app/lib/src/features/bootstrap/boot_window_host.dart`) and `createRememberedWindow` (`app/lib/src/features/window/app_windows.dart`) build the `*MacOS` controllers, which need the engine; only `restoreArguments` is pinned (`app/test/features/window/remembered_window_args_test.dart`).
  - **Follow-up:** a seam that maps an `AppWindowRequest` to the controller's and the runner's arguments as a pure function the tests pin, or the runtime smoke check; until then the DEBUG `zanshin.window` lines and the CGWindowList probe are the check.

## Surfaced in Story 9.23 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-19)

- ~~**The StaleNotice's "Update working copy" action, its no-Undo rule and the error toast with Retry (epics 9.23 items 3, 4 and 6) wait for Story 10.5, which builds the StaleNotice; Story 9.23 ships only the wire (`RepoHandle.workspaceUpdateStale` and its `StaleRecovery` result).**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-01 (spec-10-12-adopt-backend-layout, commit 6b9feacb): with GraphToasts created in the project window, the stale notice's result and error toast with Retry reach the running app. Low.
  - `app/lib` has only the shell's empty `staleNotice: Widget?` slot (`app/lib/src/features/shell/floating_shell.dart:75`) and a widgetbook placeholder (`app/widgetbook/folders/shell.dart:167-169`); `10-5-graph-view-model-repo-wiring` is backlog, and epics 9.23 item 6 says 10.5 owns the notice.
  - **Follow-up:** 10.5 adds the notice's quiet Zs Button with `jj workspace update-stale` in its tooltip, busy while it runs, then the `StaleRecovery` result; no Undo afterwards (item 3), Retry only on the failed action's error toast (item 4).
  - **2026-10-30, Story 10.5:** `StaleNotice` with "Update working copy" (quiet `ZsButton`, `jj workspace update-stale` tooltip, busy while it runs), the `StaleRecovery` result kept as `lastStaleRecovery`, a failure as `staleUpdateError`, no Undo (commit 5d5548c); left: the result toast and the failure's error toast with Retry — Story 10.23's.
  - **2026-11-01, Story 10.23:** the result toast (jj's sentences, no Undo) and the error toast with Retry (`jj workspace update-stale`) are built in `GraphToasts` and tested (commit e2da414); left: 10.12 creates `GraphToasts` in the project window so they reach the running app.
- ~~**The pre-verb snapshot's head load now reconciles divergent operations AS THE WORKSPACE, like the CLI.**~~ — RECORD 2026-10-20 (Ralph): kept for every verb (CLI parity), not narrowed to the recovery. Decision taken in the implementation; low risk.
  - The 9.23 oracle scenario `update_stale_dirty` showed jj-lib's `RepoLoader::load_at_head` writing `reconcile divergent operations` with no workspace name, where the CLI's `resolve_operation` (`cli_util.rs:800-829` @ v0.46.0) names the workspace. `snapshot::load_head` ports the CLI's path; the spec asks the recovery to use "the same head-load path `snapshot::run` uses", so `snapshot::run` uses it too — a parity fix for every verb that reconciles in its precondition. Other `load_at_head` calls (inside verbs, after the precondition) are unchanged.
  - **Follow-up:** none unless Ralph prefers the narrower change (recovery only).
- ~~**`RequestKind` gained only `WorkspaceAdd`, not the spec's `WorkspaceUpdateStale`.**~~ — RECORD 2026-10-20 (Ralph): no reserved tag (an unused tag would contradict the `.fbs` rule). Low; deviation from the spec text.
  - `zanshin_workspace_update_stale` is handle-only (the CLI verb takes no argument) and has no request table; the `.fbs` header's rule is "the variant name plus `Request` IS the table name", so an unused tag would contradict it.
  - **Follow-up:** add the variant if Ralph wants it reserved anyway.
- **The update-stale "concurrent tree" refusal has no end-to-end witness.** Low.
  - The check (`stale.rs::recover_at`, the CLI's `update_stale_working_copy`) needs the working-copy state to change between the recovery's stale snapshot and its second lock — a race no in-process test can stage deterministically (the stale snapshot rewrites the recorded tree first). Its mapping is pinned (`jj.concurrent_working_copy`, unit tests in `snapshot.rs` and `zanshin-ffi/src/workspace.rs`).
  - **Follow-up:** none planned.
- ~~**`snapshot_locked`'s error arm still reloads the head with jj-lib's `load_at_head`, so a reconcile written there carries no workspace name.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-19 (spec-9-23-closing-snapshot-error-arm-head-reload, commit bbeb79a): the error arm only reads the op heads and writes nothing, like the CLI; the in-verb `load_at_head` calls stay with 9.46. Low; pre-existing (surfaced by the 9.23 review). RESOLVED 2026-10-26 for the in-verb calls too (Story 9.46 I3, nothing left to change): commit 2b7076d7f28743f95f5020c87a91145cf260dd96 already routed every production head load through the CLI-style `snapshot::reload_head` (`resolve_operation`), with reconcile witnesses. `snapshot.rs`'s `no_production_code_calls_load_at_head` guards `src/` and passes.
  - 9.23 moved `snapshot::run`, the stale recovery and `workspace add` onto the CLI-style `snapshot::load_head` (`resolve_operation`, `cli_util.rs:800-829` @ v0.46.0); the reload in `snapshot_locked`'s failure path (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/snapshot.rs`) and the in-verb `load_at_head` calls were left as they were. Only observable when divergent op heads exist at that moment.
  - **Follow-up:** switch the remaining head loads to `snapshot::load_head` in 9.46's v0.44 → v0.46 audit, with a reconcile-op witness per site.
- ~~**No verb reports "There are unresolved conflicts at these paths" after a checkout onto a conflicted `@`.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-20 (spec-9-53-parity-cleanup-after-2026-10-19-loop, commit 56f5868): `jj.unresolved_conflicts` with the CLI's path lines from `mutate::finish_transaction` (only when `@` moved) and `stale::recover_at`; the oracle's `new_conflicting_merge` warnings entry is gone, `edit_conflicted_merge` added. Low; pre-existing across every verb, newly visible in 9.23's stale recovery (surfaced by the 9.23 review).
  - The CLI's `print_updated_working_copy_stats` (`cli_util.rs:2297-2331` @ v0.46.0) warns when the new `@` has conflicts; `mutate::finish_transaction` and `stale::recover_at` emit only `jj.checkout_files_skipped`, and there is no warning kind for it. The GUI can read `@`'s conflict state from the graph.
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph decides whether a `jj.unresolved_conflicts` warning (with the paths) joins the 9.32 warning codes for every checkout.
  - **2026-10-20, Ralph:** yes, a `jj.unresolved_conflicts` warning code → **Story 9.53** item 1.

## Surfaced in the closing run of Story 9.23 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-19)

- ~~**When the auto-update-stale recovery's trailing snapshot fails, the partial report's `was_snapshot_taken` is measured against the recovered operation, not the one `run` loaded, so it can read `false` although the recovery wrote operations.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-20 (spec-9-53-parity-cleanup-after-2026-10-19-loop, commit 56f5868): on any recovery error `run` re-reads the op heads against its loaded op (helper shared with `snapshot_locked`); witness `a_recovery_failing_after_its_reset_reports_that_it_wrote` (a malformed `remotes.<name>.auto-track-bookmarks` — a wrong-typed `git.*` key already fails the Git backend's load). Low.
  - `snapshot::run` returns `stale::recover`'s error as is (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/snapshot.rs:429-437`); the flag was last set by `snapshot_locked` inside `trailing_snapshot` (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/stale.rs:362`), whose baseline is the recovered repo — against `run`'s doc ("whether the head moved off the operation loaded here"). Pre-existing since 9.23, untested; the CLI computes no `changed` on an error path, so it is a Zanshin-only report fact.
  - **Follow-up:** on a recovery error, `run` re-reads the op heads against its own loaded op id (read-only, as `snapshot_locked`'s error arm now does), with a witness that fails the trailing snapshot.
  - **2026-10-20, Ralph:** fix → **Story 9.53** item 3.

## Surfaced in Story 9.15 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-19)

- ~~**The tree arm of `squash` and `split` waits for Story 9.50.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-23 (spec-9-50-hunk-selection-select-tree, Story 9.50, I2; commit 90612be): `SplitOptions.tree` and `SquashOptions.trees` (one `SourceTree` per source; a source without one moves everything) take `select_tree`'s tree plus `left`; a split with neither paths nor a tree still refuses `jj.invalid_input`, now naming the tree arm; oracle `split_tool_sections_1_3`, `squash_tool_sections_1_3`. Medium.
  - A GUI-selected tree plus `left`, its staleness refusal, and split's interactive default with no paths (`cli/src/commands/split.rs:218` @ v0.46.0) are 9.50's to decide ("The verbs that take a tree id (9.15, 9.49) take `left` as well, and refuse the same way. This story decides the refusal code", epics.md Story 9.50 item 3). 9.15 ships the paths arm only and refuses a split without paths (`jj.invalid_input`); `SelectedTree::from_tree_id` (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/op_support.rs`) stays unwired, its WHY naming 9.50.
  - **Follow-up:** 9.50 adds the tree arm to `crates/zanshin-jj/src/squash_split.rs`' selection (`plan_squash` / `prepare_split`).
- ~~**`jj squash -o/-A/-B` is not ported.**~~ — RECORD 2026-10-20 (Ralph): not ported until a design asks for it. Low.
  - Experimental at v0.46.0: the squashed changes go into a NEW commit at that location (`squash.rs:113-146`, `:274-326`). No design row needs it — the drops "Into a new change after/before" are `split -A/-B` — and epics.md Story 9.15 lists only `-r`, `--from/--into`, `-m/-u/-k` for squash.
  - **Follow-up:** port it into `crates/zanshin-jj/src/squash_split.rs` if a design asks for it.
- ~~**`SquashRequest` carries one `RequestKind` for two wrappers.**~~ — RECORD 2026-10-20 (Ralph): stays one kind; no `SquashPlan` tag. Low.
  - `zanshin_squash` and `zanshin_squash_plan` both accept `RequestKind::Squash`, unlike `ChangeIdList`'s two kinds: a mis-routed buffer can only run or preview the SAME squash (`schemas/envelope.fbs`, `docs/ffi-abi.md` § The `RequestKind` tag).
  - **Follow-up:** a `SquashPlan` kind if Ralph wants every wrapper strictly per-kind.
- **`squash` reports its abandonments in `MutationResult`.** Low.
  - The abandoned sources ride `abandoned_change_ids` (as `git_fetch` reports jj-lib's own abandonments there) and the replacement of an abandoned `@` rides `new_change_ids` (as for `abandon`); `split` names its one new change id there too. The field docs (Rust and Dart) say so.
  - **Follow-up:** none unless 10.9's toast needs another shape.
- **`split` without `-m` cleans both descriptions as a saved editor buffer would.** Low.
  - The CLI's `edit_multiple_descriptions` runs `cleanup_description_lines` over each block, so a description lacking its final newline gains one and `JJ:` lines in it are dropped; Zanshin applies the same cleanup (`split_builders`). Common descriptions are unchanged; the oracle's `split_paths_editor` matches the CLI, and `split_without_a_message_cleans_the_description_like_a_saved_buffer` (`crates/zanshin-jj/tests/squash_split.rs`) pins the cleanup.
  - **Follow-up:** none.
- ~~**The "editor saved unchanged" rule of `squash` and `split` ignores user templates.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-20 (spec-9-51-jj-templates-trailers-draft-push-bookmark, commit 74338d0): the text is `templates.draft_commit_description` rendered on the CLI's temp commit and cleaned up (`Templates::editor_text`), so `default_commit_description` and custom drafts are honoured; oracle `squash_custom_draft`, `squash_plan_custom_draft_as_message`, `split_editor_default_description`. Medium.
  - The CLI's editor buffer is `templates.draft_commit_description`, whose default body is `coalesce(description, default_commit_description, "\n")` (`cli/src/description_util.rs` `description_template` @ v0.46.0); with `template-aliases.default_commit_description` set, `jj split <paths>` without `-m` on an undescribed revision writes that text, while Zanshin writes `""` (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/squash_split.rs` `split_builders`; `op_support.rs` `combined_editor_text`). ## Surfaced in Story 9.52 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-20)

- ~~**Cancel identifies stock macOS git by its `--git-dir` argument rather than by the token in its environment, and creates no process group — Ralph to confirm this attribution rule.**~~ — RECORD 2026-10-20 (Ralph): the rule stays (one handle per project, calls on it serialized; the residual needs two handles on one repo at once). Medium.
  - macOS hides the environment of Apple platform binaries (`/usr/bin/git`, the CLT git, `/bin/sh`, `ssh`) from `sysctl(KERN_PROCARGS2)`, even for our own children, so "kill only processes carrying `ZANSHIN_CANCEL_TOKEN`" never matches stock git. `crates/zanshin-jj/src/cancel.rs` (`Marks::matches`, `poll`) therefore also signals a hidden-env descendant that names `--git-dir <dir>`, but only when exactly one live call watches that dir. Residual: a git without a token on that dir (e.g. a concurrent `workspace add` on another handle of the repo) cannot be told apart. Documented in `docs/ffi-abi.md` § Cancel.
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph keeps the rule, or picks an exec wrapper with `setpgid` (needs a shipped executable; Dart hooks ship only dylibs, and macOS has no `setsid(1)`), or cancel-by-checkpoints only for Apple git.
- **No Dart witness for a successful worker clone (the reserved handle id is registered and usable).** Medium.
  - Inside `dart test`, a helper-isolate clone still fails jj-lib's wait with "Failed to wait for the git process" (3 of 3 runs, re-checked in 9.52). `packages/zanshin_core/test/worker/network_operation_test.dart` accepts that outcome, so the `CreatedRepoHandle` branch of the `CloneRepoCommand` reply in `isolate.dart` never runs in Dart, and the Dart clone-cancel test may end at the pre-git checkpoint. The mid-git kill and clean-up are witnessed in Rust (`crates/zanshin-jj/tests/clone_cancel.rs`).
  - **Follow-up:** fix the child-reaping limit (deferred-work "No Dart real-cdylib witness for a SUCCESSFUL clone"), then add a Dart success-clone witness (`handleId` equals the announced id; `status()` works on it).
  - **2026-10-20, check:** the running app's clone works and its handle id is usable (the project window opened on it); only the `dart test` harness hits the reaping limit, so a Dart witness still needs that limit lifted.

- ~~**The `.claude/skills/story-loop/SKILL.md` gate-rule sentence Story 9.55 amended reads ambiguously.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-21 (console session, doc fix): reworded: all integration tests of `zanshin-jj`/`zanshin-ffi` are one target `it`, `--test it <module>::` selects one former file. Low.

## Surfaced in Story 9.24 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-21)

- ~~**The remote-change poll diffs every advertised branch and tag, ignoring the bookmark/tag filters the CLI's `jj git fetch` applies per remote, so a ref the fetch would never bring stays "new upstream" forever.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-21 (spec-9-30-git-fetch-parity-default-remotes-filters, commit 7e51a50): `remote_changes` filters advertised and view names with the fetch's per-remote filters and refuses what the fetch refuses. Medium.
  - `cli/src/commands/git/fetch.rs:211-231` @ v0.46.0 (`parse_remote_fetch_bookmarks`, `load_default_fetch_bookmarks`, `parse_remote_fetch_tags`: `remotes.<name>.fetch-bookmarks` / `fetch-tags`, else the Git fetch refspecs); Zanshin's own `Repo::git_fetch` still fetches `glob("*")` for both (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/remotes.rs`), so today the poll matches Zanshin's fetch. `crates/zanshin-jj/src/remote_changes.rs` (advertisement filter).
  - **Follow-up:** Story 9.30 brings the CLI's filters to the fetch; apply the same per-remote filter to the poll's advertisement then.
- **A tag whose target is not a commit (a tag of a tree or blob) is reported as "new upstream" on every poll.** Low.
  - The ref advertisement carries no object type and no objects are downloaded; jj imports only refs that peel to a commit (jj-lib 0.46.0 `git.rs:442-492`), so such a tag never reaches the view. `crates/zanshin-jj/src/remote_changes.rs` (tag diff).
  - **Follow-up:** none unless a user hits it; a fix needs the object type (an object download, or `git cat-file` after a fetch).
- **No Dart witness of a SUCCESSFUL remote-change poll: inside `dart test` the poll's `git ls-remote` wait fails with `jj.network_error: Failed to wait for the git process`, so Story 9.24's first acceptance criterion (moved `main` read from Dart) is witnessed in Rust only.** Medium.
  - The Dart VM's process manager reaps every child of its process while it has a child of its own running (in `dart test` the resident frontend-server), so `Child::wait()` on a git that exited on its own gets ECHILD — the limit "No Dart real-cdylib witness for a SUCCESSFUL clone" above records for fetch and clone. The poll spawns git exactly as jj-lib does (`create_command` / `wait_with_output`, as the spec asks), so it inherits it. `packages/zanshin_core/test/worker/remote_changes_test.dart` accepts that one outcome and checks the result whenever the wait wins (it lost in every local run); the moved/new/deleted diff is witnessed by `crates/zanshin-jj/tests/it/remote_changes.rs` and `crates/zanshin-ffi/tests/it/remote_changes_ffi.rs`, the Dart decoder by `test/ffi/remotes_test.dart`.
  - **Follow-up:** run `RepoHandle.remoteChanges('origin')` from the running app (where the clone's wait was verified to work, 2026-10-20) — or lift the reaping limit for the test harness (e.g. waiting on the child's exit through `kqueue` `NOTE_EXITSTATUS` instead of `waitpid`, which would diverge from jj-lib's wait) — Ralph's call.
  - **2026-10-21, Ralph:** Story 11.8 runs `remoteChanges('origin')` once in the running app (Marionette); epics.md 11.8 dated note. Covered by Story 11.8.
- ~~**`cargo make codegen` reformats hand-written `packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/ffi/*.dart` at 80 columns.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-21 (spec-9-24-closing-codegen-dart-format-width, commit fe7b82a): the cause was a missing workspace `package_config.json`, not the cwd; `codegen-flatc-dart` now runs `dart pub get` first and formats only the flatc output from inside the package. Low.
  - `codegen-flatc-dart` runs `dart format packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/ffi/` from the repo root; with the current Dart SDK (3.14.0-271.0.dev) that run did not pick up the package's 120-column `formatter` setting and rewrapped 29 hand-written files (and the generated accessors, whose re-format inside the package then kept the 80-column splits). This run reverted those files and re-ran `flatc --dart` + `dart format` from inside `packages/zanshin_core`, which reproduces the committed style. `Makefile.toml` `[tasks.codegen-flatc-dart]`.
  - **Follow-up:** format the generated file only, from inside the package (`cwd = "packages/zanshin_core"`, `dart format lib/src/ffi/envelope_zanshin.ffi_generated.dart`).
- ~~**A timer-driven remote-change poll runs git with the user's full auth and no non-interactive guard, so each tick might raise a credential-helper or SSH-agent confirmation prompt (unverified).**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-03 (spec-11-8-sync-fetch-push-poll-badge, commit 3516be5): the poll uses no special auth environment; a failed tick is silent, the next tick retries, ticks never overlap (sync_model_test). Medium.
  - `crates/zanshin-jj/src/remote_changes.rs` `ls_remote` spawns git the way jj-lib spawns fetch (stdin null, no `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, no ssh `BatchMode`), as the story asks ("auth = the user's git … for free"); a fetch is user-initiated, a poll is not. Whether prompts appear for a Finder-launched app (no tty) is unverified.
  - **Follow-up:** poll an SSH remote behind a confirming agent (1Password) and an HTTPS remote with a GUI credential helper from the running app; if prompts appear, Ralph decides on a non-interactive environment for the poll only.
  - **2026-10-21, Ralph:** no special handling — dialogs may appear as in SourceTree; an unconfirmed tick fails quietly and the next tick retries, ticks never overlap (Story 11.8's dated note). Covered by Story 11.8.

## Surfaced in Story 9.31 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-21)

- ~~**A rebase row's no-op check applies jj-lib's whole move on a dropped transaction, so a hover pays the tree merges of every descendant it would rebase.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-03 (spec-10-10-graph-drag-and-drop, commit c5d42306): measured 1.5–4.6 s per query on a 200-deep stack; now one cached query per target and a write-free no-op check (26–71 ms). Low.
  - `crates/zanshin-jj/src/rebase.rs::rebase_is_no_op` (`compute_move_commits` + `apply`) — exact by construction (it is the move the verb runs), accepted by the spec's design note; a long stack under the dragged change makes `drop_actions` as slow as the rebase itself.
  - **Follow-up:** measure on a deep stack when 10.10 lands; if it bites, ask only once per drop target or move the check off the hover path.
  - **2026-10-21, Ralph:** measure on a deep stack in Story 10.10 before deciding (epics.md 10.10 dated note). Covered by Story 10.10.
  - **RESOLVED 2026-11-03 (Story 10.10, spec-10-10-graph-drag-and-drop, commit c5d42306):** measured with `benches/drop_actions_depth.rs` (one target at `-o`/`-A`/`-B`, a 200-change stack): 1.5–4.6 s per query — it bit, since `-A`/`-B` move every descendant of the target. Both remedies taken: the app queries once per target and operation (cached), and `rebase_is_no_op` answers from a read-only port of jj-lib's planned parents (no apply): 26–71 ms per query after; an inline test compares it with the old dry run over linear and merge history.
- **The preview rows stop at the verbs' plan phase, so a template fault the verb hits after it still reads `Available`.** Low.
  - `describe` / `new` / `squash` / `split` render `templates.commit_trailers`, `templates.new_description` or `templates.draft_commit_description` after their plan fn (`mutate.rs::describe`, `new_change`; `squash_split.rs::squashed_description`, `describe_split`), and `duplicate` parses `templates.duplicate_description` after the location (`cmd_duplicate` @ v0.46.0), past `plan_duplicate` — a malformed template refuses the verb `jj.config_error` while its row said `Available` (the verb's refusal stays authoritative, as the story intends).
  - **Follow-up:** if the UI should grey those rows out, the template parse (not the render) can move into the plan fns.
  - **2026-10-21 (follow-on, spec-9-31-matrix-rows-rebase-b-revert-restore-absorb-interdiff):** the `Revert` row joins: `cmd_revert` parses `templates.revert_description` after the location, past `revert::plan_revert` (the spec keeps the parse in Story 9.18's transaction phase).
  - **2026-10-21, Ralph:** stays advisory — the verb's own refusal is authoritative (epics.md 10.10 dated note). Record.
- ~~**`edit` of the current `@` previews `Available`.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-25 (spec-9-38-edit-current-at-is-noop, commit 5b71805): the card's `edit` row takes `no_op` from `plan_edit`; witnesses `change_actions_of_the_working_copy_report_edit_as_a_no_op` and the `@` pick in `change_actions_rows_agree_with_their_verbs`. Low — already Story 9.38's: when `plan_edit` reports "Already editing that commit." the row becomes `NoOp` with no change here.
- ~~**The rebase dry run still writes git commit objects, `refs/jj/keep/*` refs and extra-metadata entries for every commit it rebases, on every hover.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-03 (spec-10-10-graph-drag-and-drop, commit c5d42306): the no-op check writes nothing; a whole query leaves 0 keep refs and 0 loose objects. Low.
  - `crates/zanshin-jj/src/rebase.rs::rebase_is_no_op` → `apply` → `CommitBuilder::write` → `GitBackend::write_commit`, which keeps a no-gc ref per commit (jj-lib 0.46.0 `git_backend.rs` ~:1467); signing is dropped for the dry run (review patch), but the transaction's drop does not remove the objects and refs, which stay until `jj util gc`.
  - **Follow-up:** if they pile up visibly (colocated `git for-each-ref`), compute the no-op from the parents without writing, or move the check off the hover path.
  - **2026-10-21 (follow-on, spec-9-31-matrix-rows-rebase-b-revert-restore-absorb-interdiff):** the new rows add to it — `RebaseBranch` runs the same dry run, `Restore` writes its merged tree and `Absorb` its split's content blobs to the store on every hover (no commit, no ref); kept as the spec defers them. The hover also does more work: a one-target `onto` now plans ten rows instead of five, and `Absorb`'s `split_hunks_to_trees` annotates every changed file of the source through history — measure it with the rebase dry run in Story 10.10.
  - **2026-10-21, Ralph:** measure with the hover cost in Story 10.10 (epics.md 10.10 dated note). Covered by Story 10.10.
  - **RESOLVED 2026-11-03 (Story 10.10, spec-10-10-graph-drag-and-drop, commit c5d42306):** before, +226–664 `refs/jj/keep/*` per query; after the write-free no-op, +0 keep refs and +0 loose git objects per whole query (all rows, restore/absorb included) at every depth of the bench.
- ~~**The bookmark row's "any side of a conflict" fast-forward rule is untested.** Low.~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-21 (closing run, spec-9-31-closing-conflicted-bookmark-fast-forward, commit 6691ae0): `tests/it/drop_actions.rs::a_conflicted_bookmark_moves_forward_when_any_side_is_an_ancestor` conflicts `mark` (adds `B`, `D`) and pins onto `C` / `D` fast-forward, onto `A` backwards; an "all sides" mutation of `is_fast_forward` fails it; `a_conflicted_bookmark_with_a_deleted_side_counts_only_the_present_side` pins a conflict with a deleted side (adds `B` and absent).
  - Was: `crates/zanshin-jj/src/bookmark_ops.rs::is_fast_forward` loops over `present_adds`; every test used a non-conflicted bookmark, so an "all sides" regression would have passed.
  - **Follow-up:** the shared plan rule is now witnessed by the `drop_actions` tests; Story 9.19's move verb still owes its own test of the WRITE — a conflicted bookmark moved to a descendant of one side becomes normal.
  - **RESOLVED 2026-10-23 (Story 9.19, spec-9-19-bookmark-move-advance-rename-list-filters, commit a23ba38):** `tests/it/bookmark_move.rs::a_conflicted_bookmark_moved_to_a_descendant_of_one_side_becomes_normal` conflicts `mark` (adds `B`, `D`), runs `Repo::bookmark_move` onto `C` (a child of `B`) and pins the stored target as `RefTarget::normal(C)` — the write witness. Separately, `the_verb_agrees_with_every_drop_actions_bookmark_row` witnesses row/verb agreement (conflicted `mark` included), not the write.
- ~~**The original matrix rows `rebase -b`, `revert`, `restore`, `absorb` and `interdiff` are not built.** Low.~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-21 (follow-on, spec-9-31-matrix-rows-rebase-b-revert-restore-absorb-interdiff, commit ad64376): `DropVerb` appends `RebaseBranch`, `Revert`, `Restore`, `Absorb`, `Interdiff`, each answered by its plan fn — `rebase::plan_rebase_branch`, `revert::plan_revert` (new), and `mutate::plan_restore` / `plan_absorb` / `commit_views::plan_interdiff`, extracted from the shipped verbs, which now call them (behaviour unchanged, agreement tests).
  - epics.md Story 9.31's 2026-10-14 design update: "(Claude's proposal) The matrix shrinks to these rows, because nothing consumes the others"; the story adopted the proposal (spec Design Notes).
  - **Follow-up:** none unless Ralph wants those rows; their plan fns would then follow the same pattern.
  - **2026-10-21, Ralph:** build them after all — follow-on run after the story loop.

## Surfaced in the Story 9.31 matrix-rows run (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-21)

Spec: spec-9-31-matrix-rows-rebase-b-revert-restore-absorb-interdiff.

- ~~**A location's immutable set is resolved before its ids and whether or not it has children, so `-A`/`-B` plans refuse `jj.config_error` where the CLI would not.**~~ — REJECTED 2026-10-21 (spec-9-31-matrix-rows-closing-location-ids-before-immutable): the premise is false — the CLI's `tx.finish` resolves `immutable_heads()` on every write with changes, so it refuses too; the reorder failed the parity oracle and was reverted; witness `new_insert::insert_after_a_leaf_refuses_an_unresolvable_alias_like_the_cli` (commit 70c98be). Do not reorder. Low.
  - `revert::plan_revert` and `rebase::plan_rebase_branch` (and the shipped `duplicate::plan_duplicate`, `mutate::plan_new`) call `resolve_immutable` before `resolve_commit_location`; the CLI's `compute_commit_location` (`cli_util.rs` @ v0.46.0) resolves the ids first and checks rewritability only for non-empty children — so an unresolvable `immutable_heads()` alias with `-A` on a leaf, or with an unknown location id, reads `jj.config_error` instead of success / the unknown revision.
  - **Follow-up:** resolve the location ids first and the immutable set only when `new_child_ids` is non-empty (one helper in `op_support.rs` shared by all four plans), plus a test with an unresolvable alias.

  - **Follow-up:** audit every unguarded finish caller and resolve the immutable set against the base repo in the shared finish (as `commit.rs:204` / `remotes.rs:568` already do per verb; `squash_split.rs:714` / `workspace_add.rs:218` do not) and in the affected plans, plus an oracle scenario with an unresolvable alias (it needs a ledger entry for the CLI's short-prefixes warnings, which Zanshin does not emit).

## Surfaced in Story 9.56 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-21)

- **The CLI's generic, error-structure-derived hint families stay dropped on errors (revset parse/resolution "Did you mean …" and divergent-id hints, config "Check the config file: <path>", fileset, string-pattern and name-pattern parse hints).** Low.

  - **Follow-up:** a story that derives these hints from the jj-lib error structures and sets them through the existing `hints()` accessors.
- ~~**`convert`'s "import git head" finish does not resolve the immutable set, while the CLI's `jj git init --git-repo` goes through `tx.finish`.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-22 (spec-9-57-convert-no-own-rollback-cli-parity, commit f1c4046): the head-import finish resolves `immutable_heads()` against the base repo before the rebase and refuses `jj.config_error` after `.jj/` exists, as the CLI; both convert verbs dropped their own rollback and report what they left in `left_behind`; oracle `convert_import_unresolvable_immutable` / `convert_colocated_unresolvable_immutable`. Low.
  - (as found) `crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs:655/666` (plain rebase, then `finish_transaction`); under an unresolvable alias the CLI fails after writing `.jj/`, while Zanshin's convert rolls `.jj/` back on any failure (no-data-loss invariant).
  - ~~**Follow-up:** Ralph decides whether convert refuses like the CLI (and keeps or rolls back the half-initialised `.jj/`).~~ Done (f1c4046).
  - **2026-10-21, Ralph:** refuse like the CLI and drop convert's OWN rollback altogether (it could delete a `.jj/` we did not create) → **Story 9.57**. Covered by Story 9.57.
- ~~**The immutable refusal's "Could not modify commit: <summary>" hint (like push's "Rejected commit:" hint) renders Zanshin's approximate summary, not the CLI's `templates.commit_summary`, so a commit with bookmarks loses its "<refs> | " part and a custom summary template is ignored.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-21 (spec-9-56-closing-commit-summary-template-hints, commit 348d70f): `Templates::commit_summary` renders the effective `templates.commit_summary` through the jj-cli engine at all three sites (immutable hint, push "Rejected commit:" hint, the skipped-signing list); `push::commit_summary` is gone; oracle `hints::` witnesses compare the two hints (default template on bookmarked commits, custom template) against the pinned CLI on the same repo; the signing list is pinned by `tests/it/push.rs` only. Low.
  - (as found) `crates/zanshin-jj/src/op_support.rs` `immutable_hints` reused `push::commit_summary` (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/push.rs:476`, a documented deliberate divergence since Story 9.20); CLI v0.46.0 `cli_util.rs:2064-2068` + `cli/src/config/templates.toml:4`, `:350-362`.
  - ~~**Follow-up:** render `templates.commit_summary` through the jj-cli template engine (Story 9.51) for both hint sites, threading the template context into `check_rewritable`'s callers.~~ Done (348d70f).

## Surfaced in the closing run of Story 9.56 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-21)

Spec: spec-9-56-closing-commit-summary-template-hints.

- **The CLI parses `templates.commit_summary`, `templates.op_summary` and the short change-id template at the start of EVERY workspace command, so a broken one refuses every command and their parse diagnostics warn on every command; Zanshin parses the summary template only when a hint renders it (diagnostics dropped) and `templates.op_summary` never.** Low.
  - CLI v0.46.0 `cli/src/cli_util.rs:1272-1290` (`WorkspaceCommandHelper::new`: `get_string` of both keys, then `parse_operation_template` / `parse_commit_template` ×2 "to report error before starting mutable operation"); `crates/zanshin-jj/src/snapshot.rs` `CommandEnv::load` parses neither. Found while porting the summary hint.
  - **Follow-up:** parse the three templates in `CommandEnv::load` (the operation template needs an operation template language port), refusing `jj.config_error` and recording their diagnostics once per call.

Low.

  - **Follow-up:** Ralph 2026-10-22: no `left_behind` there; the FFI and Dart docs say the repo is complete and the UI can reopen it → **Story 9.58** (item 5). Covered by Story 9.58.
- **The rebase and lock error arms of `import_head_into_destination` are reached by no test.** Low.
  - `crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs` `import_head_into_destination` (`rebase_descendants_with_options(..).map_err(convert_failed)?`, `lock_git_import_export(..).map_err(convert_failed)?`); the `.jj/` holding the lock is created in the same call, and a rebase fails only on a broken backend.

## Surfaced in Story 9.9 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-23)

- ~~**Under a Dart host the VM can reap a child process the cdylib started, so its exit status is lost (`ECHILD`) — `jj fix` then keeps the file's old content silently, as the CLI's runner does for any wait failure.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-24 (loop-9-9-9-12-followon-2-dart-spawn-fix-cancel-select-tree-modes, commit af46df3d2eb1d5566dbd845d505dcd4c7d13f978): house rule in project-context.md (Dart starts child processes only detached, or from Rust) plus the guard `app/test/process_spawn_guard_test.dart` over every Dart `lib/` tree; no Rust change. Medium.
  - Seen in `packages/zanshin_core`'s `dart test`: `zanshin_jj::fix`'s `child.wait_with_output()` failed with "No child processes (os error 10)" on roughly half the tool runs (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/fix.rs` `Runner::run_tool`); Dart's process exit-code handler waits on ANY pid while a Dart-started (non-detached) process is alive — under `dart test` the test runner's own subprocess. The Rust suites (no Dart VM) are deterministic; `test/worker/metaedit_duplicate_fix_test.dart` therefore uses an unstartable tool. The same hazard applies to every subprocess jj-lib spawns from the cdylib (`git` for fetch/push/clone, signing) whenever the app has a Dart-started child running.
  - **Follow-up:** decide whether the app may ever hold a non-detached Dart child while a verb runs; if so, a reap-proof wait (e.g. kqueue `EVFILT_PROC`/`NOTE_EXITSTATUS`) or running such children from Rust only.
  - **2026-10-24, Ralph:** no — a house rule in project-context.md (Dart starts child processes only detached, or from Rust; today the only one, `login_shell.dart`'s, is `detachedWithStdio`) plus a guard test over the Dart `lib/` trees → the loop 9.9–9.12 second follow-on run.
- ~~**`jj.fileset_diagnostic` is registered but unreachable at jj-lib 0.46**~~ — RECORD 2026-10-24 (Ralph): the code stays (CLI parity); the re-check is an item of the jj pin-bump checklist. (jj-lib emits no fileset diagnostic; `revset.rs` is its only `add_warning` caller). Low.
  - `crates/zanshin-jj/src/fix.rs` `get_tools_config` records them under "In `fix.tools.<name>`" exactly as the CLI prints them.
  - **Follow-up:** re-check at the next jj-lib pin bump; a test once jj-lib emits one.
- ~~**`jj.config_deprecated` reaches only the snapshotting verbs and the config verbs; `config gc`, the non-snapshotting reads and the creation paths run the `line-range-arg` migration but drop its "Deprecated … config" warning, which the CLI prints on every command.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-23 (closing run spec-9-9-closing-config-deprecated-every-start-up, commit 9629c9e): `config::check_startup_stack(sink)` records the migrations first in init / clone / `convert_colocated` / `convert_import` (kept on a refusal past the start-up check); `config_gc_list` / `config_gc_delete` return `ConfigOutcome` and their start-up records into it (FFI: both envelopes). The graph/revset reads were not affected — `walk_graph` / `graph_topology` / `eval_revset` run `start_call`, which records once per call (witness `fix::a_deprecated_line_range_arg_warns_once_per_graph_read`); `log_defaults` is the config half of the same `jj log` and stays silent.
  - Was: `crates/zanshin-jj/src/config_cmd.rs` called `startup(.., None)` for `config gc`; `check_startup_stack` migrated and dropped the `Deprecations`.
  - **Follow-up:** done (spec-9-9-closing-config-deprecated-every-start-up).
- ~~**A configured fix tool that never exits blocks the repo's worker for good — the CLI's user can Ctrl-C, the app's cannot, and `fix` has no cancel.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-24 (loop-9-9-9-12-followon-2-dart-spawn-fix-cancel-select-tree-modes, commit bb053950f2fa711f2a8f7163949b585d642d9e35, review patch 73134a30cfb4ab99d9cb5daa66c653d12b909cb6): `fix` is a 9.52-style cancellable operation (`FixRequest.cancel_token`, helper isolate, `NetworkOperation<FixResult>`; a cancel kills the tool's process group and refuses `jj.cancelled` with no operation, as the CLI's Ctrl-C) and takes `fix.tools.<name>.timeout` (seconds or `<n>ms`/`<n>s`/`<n>m`; an overrun is a failed run warning `jj.fix_tool_timed_out`). Medium.
  - `crates/zanshin-jj/src/fix.rs` `Runner::run_tool` waits without a deadline, as the CLI's `run_tool` does.
  - **Follow-up:** give `fix` the network verbs' cancel (Story 9.52: helper + process-group kill), or a per-tool timeout if Ralph wants one.
  - **2026-10-24, Ralph:** both — the 9.52 cancel (the GUI's Ctrl-C) and an optional per-tool timeout `fix.tools.<name>.timeout` (Zanshin-only; the stock CLI's `RawToolConfig` has no `deny_unknown_fields`, so it ignores the key; absent = no timeout, as in the CLI) → the loop 9.9–9.12 second follow-on run.
- ~~**A fix tool that exits without reading stdin might raise SIGPIPE in the app process, if the Flutter/Dart host does not ignore SIGPIPE (unverified).**~~ — REJECTED 2026-10-23 (closing run spec-9-9-closing-config-deprecated-every-start-up): probed with `sigaction(SIGPIPE, NULL, &old)` through Dart FFI — the standalone Dart VM (`dart run` / `dart test`), `flutter_tester` (`flutter test`) and the Flutter macOS embedder (a `flutter build macos --debug` app) all report `SIG_IGN` (the Dart VM's `Platform::Initialize`); the cdylib runs only after the host VM is up, so an early-closed stdin yields `EPIPE` (discarded, as in the CLI), never a signal. No code.
  - Was: `crates/zanshin-jj/src/fix.rs` `Runner::run_tool` writes the content with `write_all`; Rust ignores SIGPIPE only in a binary's `main`, a cdylib inherits the host's disposition.
  - **Follow-up:** none.

## Surfaced in Story 9.12 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-24)

- ~~**`op_show_diff::op_diff_of_a_rebasing_merge_operation_fails_like_the_cli` is flaky: the reconciling merge does not always produce the predecessor cycle it pins.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-24 (closing run spec-9-12-closing-status-without-at-and-head-reload, commit 0b70704): the cause is the commit timestamp, not the op-head order — `op diff`'s own `merge_operations` of the two parents re-rebases the second branch's commit, and only when that rebase yields the very commit the head load's merge wrote does the predecessor map cycle; the Git backend keeps the committer timestamp in whole seconds, so the two merges agreed only within one second (the pinned CLI alike: with `JJ_TIMESTAMP` it fails every time, with the wall clock and a second between the merges it prints the diff). The test pins `debug.commit-timestamp` in the fixture's repo config; 40/40 isolated runs pass. `diverge_with` is unchanged (ordering the op heads by time did not help: 4 of 40 still failed).
  - Was: measured 2026-10-24: 3 of 40 isolated runs fail on the 9.12 baseline (`git archive 12c5b60`), 2 of 40 with Story 9.12 applied, and it failed in two full `cargo test -p zanshin-jj` runs; the outcome depends on the unpinned commit/operation timestamps the two `diverge` describes get (`crates/zanshin-jj/tests/it/op_show_diff.rs`). Not caused by 9.12.
  - **Follow-up:** done (spec-9-12-closing-status-without-at-and-head-reload).

## Surfaced in Story 9.25 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-25)

- ~~**Only `revset_counts` / `find_changes` resolve revset symbols with the id-prefix context; `graph_topology` and every mutating verb still resolve without it.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-25 (spec-9-25-closing-id-prefix-context-search-warnings-view-hints, commit 0bad054): every user and config revset (topology, eval/resolve_revset, show/evolog, bookmark list/move/advance, workspace add, absorb, arrange, push) resolves with the effective `revsets.short-prefixes` id-prefix context; the CLI's own no-context sites (immutable set, `trunk()` check, op diff) stay without. Medium (CLI parity).
  - The CLI's `default_symbol_resolver` (`cli/src/revset_util.rs:197-203` @ v0.46.0) resolves every command's revsets with `SymbolResolver::with_id_prefix_context` over `revsets.short-prefixes`, so a short prefix unique within that set but ambiguous in the full index resolves in the CLI. Zanshin's shared `crate::revset::resolve_for_evaluation` (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/revset.rs`) takes the context as an argument; only `crates/zanshin-jj/src/revset_search.rs` passes one. Every other read and verb passes `None`, so such a prefix refuses `jj.revset_resolution_error` (ambiguous) there.
  - **Follow-up:** a story that passes the effective `revsets.short-prefixes` context (the topology call: its `short_prefixes` argument) at every `resolve_for_evaluation` / `resolve_expression` call site, with oracle scenarios; it touches every verb.
- ~~**A call-level failure of `revset_counts` / `find_changes` drops the warnings collected before it**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-25 (spec-9-25-closing-id-prefix-context-search-warnings-view-hints, commit 0bad054): both calls return `Warned<RevsetSearchError>` on failure and the FFI writes those warnings onto the error envelope. (the alias load's, and `find_changes`' `short_prefixes` diagnostics). Low.
  - The two calls take `&self` and return their warnings in the answer (`RevsetCounts.warnings` / `FoundChanges.warnings`, `crates/zanshin-jj/src/revset_search.rs`); on an `Err` they are logged (`warning::log_dropped`) and the error envelope carries none, unlike the Story 9.32 rule for verbs that keep warnings on error envelopes.
  - **Follow-up:** carry the warnings on `RevsetSearchError` (or return `Warned<…>` on both arms) and write them onto the error envelope, or leave it.
- ~~**`RevsetSpan` offsets are UTF-8 bytes, while Dart strings are UTF-16.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-01 (spec-10-21-graph-search, commit 5258103): a shared `zanshin_core` helper `revsetSpanUtf16` converts the spans; the error footer uses it. Low.
  - The story requires byte offsets. `packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/ffi/revset_search.dart` (`RevsetSpan`) exposes them unconverted, so a highlighter would put the mark in the wrong place for a non-ASCII expression such as `author("Jörg")`.
  - **Follow-up:** 10.21 / 10.22 convert byte offsets to UTF-16 when they highlight, or `zanshin_core` gets a small conversion helper next to the model.
- ~~**The CLI's revset hint lines reach only some calls.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-25 (spec-9-25-closing-id-prefix-context-search-warnings-view-hints, commit 0bad054): show/evolog and the bookmark list carry the hints to `FfiError.hints`; op diff rejected — the CLI prints no revset hints there (`operation/diff.rs:203-223`, `:280-310` @ v0.46.0). Low.

  - **Follow-up:** map `detail.hints` onto those calls' error envelopes, the way `crates/zanshin-jj/src/mutate.rs::rev` does.
- ~~**`render_span` reads the span back out of pest's rendered error text.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-26 (console session, Ralph): pin-bump step (6) in project-context.md § zanshin-jj now names it. Low.

  - **Follow-up:** add "re-verify `render_span` (or switch to a span accessor if jj-lib exposes one)" to the jj pin-bump steps in project-context.md § zanshin-jj.

## Surfaced in Story 5.1 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-27)

  - Story 5.1 added the two label lists to `zanshin_jj::ShowEntry`. A merged parent tree's labels sit inside its conflict markers, so `compute_hunks` needs them to print the text `jj diff --git` prints and to cut the keys `select_tree` accepts when both sides carry markers. `crates/zanshin-ffi/src/commit_views.rs` ignores them, and the wire is unchanged.
  - **Follow-up:** Story 5.8 (diff FFI wrapper) adds them to `schemas/envelope.fbs`' `ShowEntry` together with the diff payload, so a Dart caller can name a conflicted parent tree completely.
  - **2026-11-01, Story 5.8:** done — `ShowEntry.parent_tree_labels` / `tree_labels` are on the wire (always written) and on the Dart `ShowEntry` (`parentTreeLabels` / `treeLabels`); witnessed by `crates/zanshin-ffi/tests/it/commit_views_ffi.rs::show_carries_a_conflicted_merges_tree_labels` and `packages/zanshin_core/test/ffi/commit_views_test.dart`.

Done (console session): `LICENSE-APACHE` (jj's `LICENSE` @ v0.46.0, verbatim) at the root, `zanshin-jj`'s `license = "MIT AND Apache-2.0"`, `source_symbol.rs` points to the copy (jj ships no NOTICE file, so §4(d) does not apply); left: a third-party notice for the Rust crates in the app bundle, with the release-phase `[licenses]` table in `deny.toml`.

## Surfaced in Story 5.6 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-31)

- ~~**A renamed or copied file from `Repo::diff` cannot be selected through `select_tree` as one entry: the diff shows it as its target path (no sections), while `select_tree` sees a removed source path plus an added target path.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-03 (spec-10-26-hunk-selection-gui-tree, commit 5937e4f4): renamed/copied files are whole-only; a whole pick also selects the source path on both routes. Medium.
  - `Repo::diff` uses copy records like `jj diff`, folding the source's removal into the target entry; `select_tree` mirrors the builtin diff editor, which uses no copy records (`builtin.rs:567` @ v0.46.0). Choosing the target "whole" leaves the source path unselected (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/hunks.rs` `DiffReader::file` empties a copy's sections).
  - **Follow-up:** Story 10.26 (hunk selection) also selects the source path's removal when a renamed file is chosen whole, or offers renamed files whole-only with that pairing.
  - **Resolved in Story 10.26 (2026-11-03, commit 5937e4f):** a renamed (or copied) file is whole-only; picked whole, the paths arm adds its `fromPath` (`jj commit root:new root:old`) and the tree arm lists `FileSelection.whole` for both (`InspectorViewModel._composer`, `app/lib/src/features/graph/graph_inspector.dart`). A copy's source is untouched, so nothing is added for it.

## Surfaced in the follow-up review of Story 10.19 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-11-01)

- ~~**The gap legend popover hangs 320 px right of the chip's left edge with no clamp and may be clipped at the window's right edge.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-01 (spec-10-19-closing-gap-legend-stack-keys-vertical-index, commit 34ffbba): the popover shifts left to stay 8 px inside the window like View options; checked in the real FloatingShell rig at 1041 px. Medium (unverified).
  - `CompositedTransformFollower(targetAnchor: Alignment.bottomLeft)` with no flip or clamp (`app/lib/src/features/graph/graph_gap_legend.dart:180`), unlike the View options popover; the design anchors it `left:0` too (`design/graph.css:337`).
  - **Follow-up:** check the chip's position in the floating shell at 1041+ px; if it clips, right-align or clamp the popover inside the window like View options.
- ~~**A gap whose child or target is a collapsed stack hands `stack:` keys to "Show in all()", which may miss in the `all()` layout and leave the flash empty.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-01 (spec-10-19-closing-gap-legend-stack-keys-vertical-index, commit 34ffbba): Show in all() passes member keys (oldest member of a stacked child, newest of a stacked target), found via placeOfKey; a test flashes h1 where the old code flashed nothing. Medium (unverified).
  - `_showGap` passes `layout.keyOf(...)` of the folded card (`app/lib/src/features/graph/graph_canvas.dart:725-727`); `_gapLanding` looks targets up by key in the new layout (`graph_view_model.dart:419`).
  - **Follow-up:** a view-model case where a target stack changes its oldest member in `all()`; if the flash is empty, pass member keys (the edge's entry member) instead of the stack key.
- ~~**Marker placement's vertical-strip lookup scans every vertical run of the x strip over the whole plane height, near the forbidden O(D·E) on a long one-lane history.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-01 (spec-10-19-closing-gap-legend-stack-keys-vertical-index, commit 34ffbba): vertical runs indexed per x by top with a running max bottom (GraphVerticalRuns): 885 → 30 ms at 20k one-lane nodes, placement unchanged. Medium (unverified).
  - Router B's `verticalXs` tie in polyline order, so the scan cannot binary-search by y (`app/lib/src/features/graph/graph_gaps.dart:656-664`, `:679-685`, `shortcut:` comment).
  - **Follow-up:** time placement on a filtered revset over a long linear history; if slow, index each x's runs by y.

## Surfaced in the follow-up review of Story 10.20 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-11-01)

- ~~**A same-op reload landing inside the 720 ms transition ends it at once: the glide and edge fade snap to their end and a covered born card is never revealed.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-01 (spec-10-20-closing-same-op-reload-keeps-transition, commit c4579635): a same-op layout carries the running transition on (GraphTransition.continued; canvas keeps it, viewport retargets by key), an early end still reveals the covered born card; widget tests pin it. The running-app timing log stays a manual check but no longer changes the outcome. High (unverified).
  - The watcher's `OpHeadsChangedEvent` for Zanshin's own verb triggers a second reload (`app/lib/src/features/graph/graph_view_model.dart:890-894`, 50 ms debouncer in `crates/zanshin-ffi/src/watcher.rs:184`); its layout lands with the same op id, so `_land` builds no transition and the canvas's "a layout without a transition ends the running one" branch stops it and drops `_bornReveal` (`app/lib/src/features/graph/graph_canvas.dart:334-341`), while the model already removed the born key from `reveal` (`graph_view_model.dart:1067-1070`). Unverified: whether that event usually lands after the verb's own reload (then nearly every own verb snaps) or before it (then the queued request absorbs it).
  - **Follow-up:** log in the running app when the own verb's `OpHeadsChangedEvent` reload lands relative to the transition; if inside it, keep the running transition across a same-op layout (or skip a reload whose head equals the shown op) and reveal the born card on an early end.

- **The inspector's diff view has not been compared against the DS DiffView / DiffHunk / FileRow cards in the running catalogue; only widget tests check it.** Low.
  - Unattended run: no Marionette / Playwright check (`app/widgetbook/folders/graph.dart` demo details carry a diff).

## Surfaced in the closing run of Story 10.24 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-11-01)

Low.
  - `cmd_diff` (`cli/src/commands/diff.rs` @ v0.46.0) reads `-r` through `parse_union_revsets`; `crates/zanshin-jj/src/hunks.rs` (`diff`) refuses a set of several commits ("resolved to more than one revision").
  - **Follow-up:** port the range form (merged parents of the roots against the merged heads, the gaps refusal); `RevisionDiff.commit_id` then needs the heads across the FFI.
- ~~**A revision that resolves to several commits is refused without the CLI's "resolved to these revisions" hint.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-02 (spec-loop-11-13-10-25-leftovers, commit cbdf25762b460b3cfa647e508c04d7697c2b4269): `single_commit` takes the call's `Templates` and adds the CLI's one hint (heading, up to five `templates.commit_summary` lines, `  ...` past five), before `bookmark advance`'s own hint; oracle `diff_revisions.rs` compares it verbatim with `jj diff --from 'all()'`. Low.
  - `crates/zanshin-jj/src/push.rs` (`single_commit`) returns `hints: vec![]`; the CLI's `format_multiple_revisions_error` (`cli/src/revset_util.rs` @ v0.46.0) lists up to five commit summaries. Shared by git push, bookmark advance and now diff.
  - **Follow-up:** add the hint with the commit summaries in `single_commit`.

## Surfaced in Story 10.30 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-11-02)

- ~~**Merge-up exits deviate from the uniform port rules: they never take the centre and sit 4 px outside their fan slot.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-02 (spec-10-30-graph-ports-local-direction, commit cbcd7492): the exception is gone; ports go by local direction and a column tie-break (longer vertical keeps its port) keeps 0 collinear overlaps. Medium.
  - Applied literally, the rules gave 199 collinear overlaps on the 10k synthetic free layout (a lone merge-up exit runs up its parent's column on the centre / fan slots the entries above use); `_mergeUpShift` in `packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/graph_layout/router.dart` brings it to 0 and keeps the invariants (rule 5) green. A lone merge-up exit is therefore not centred and its offset differs from the entry fan.
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph confirms the exception or picks another way to avoid the overlap (e.g. a different column for the climb).
- ~~**The "straight line at a merge's bottom takes the centre even if not the first parent" decision never applies: the router only classifies a first parent as `kindStraight`.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-02 (spec-10-30-graph-ports-local-direction, commit cbcd7492): Ralph decided "straight" = vertical at the port, whatever the kind; a same-column second parent turns below its child and fans. Low.
  - A same-column second parent is routed as a gutter line, so it fans like the others and the centre stays free (pinned by `story_10_30_a_merge_whose_first_parent_bends_leaves_the_entry_centre_free` in `packages/zanshin_core/test/graph_layout/router_test.dart`).
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph decides whether a same-column non-first parent should take the centre geometrically; otherwise none.
- ~~**An alias / parameter-expansion revset error's one-line message names only the expansion, not the failure inside it.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-02 (spec-10-30-closing-cause-chain-copy-id-keyboard, commit 8dcbdfad): the one-line message appends each source() cause, as the CLI's 'Caused by:'. Low.
  - `parse_detail` builds `message` from the top-level pest render (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/revset.rs` `render_message`), e.g. "Error at N: In alias `x`"; the CLI prints the cause chain as "Caused by:" lines.
  - **Follow-up:** append the bottom error's message to the sentence for `InAliasExpansion` / `InParameterExpansion`.
- ~~**The inspector's copyable ids work only with a pointer.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-02 (spec-10-30-closing-cause-chain-copy-id-keyboard, commit 8dcbdfad): Tab stop with focus ring, Enter/Space copies, in the app and the DS kit. Medium.
  - `_CopyId` (`app/lib/src/features/graph/graph_inspector.dart`) is a `GestureDetector` + `Semantics(onTap)`, not focusable, no Enter/Space; the DS `ZKCopyId` (`design_system/ui_kits/desktop/inspector.jsx`) has `tabIndex` but no key handler.
  - **Follow-up:** make the ids focusable with Enter/Space activation (app and DS kit).
- ~~**The DS UI kit's graph layout reads the new port API from a bundle that is not regenerated yet, and counts entry lines by edges, not slot owners.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-02 (spec-10-30-graph-ports-local-direction, commit cbcd7492): layout.js places only slot owners, MERGE_UP_SHIFT is gone, the GraphEdge bundle block matches; DS republished (Version 14), canvas synced (Version 24). Low.
  - `design_system/ui_kits/desktop/layout.js` reads `GP.MERGE_UP_SHIFT` / `GP.exits`, absent from `_ds_bundle.js` until the next republish (merge-up exits compute NaN meanwhile); it centres on `ins.length === 1` and drops lines whose approach equals `cx`, where the router counts distinct slot owners.
  - **Follow-up:** regenerate the bundle at the republish (`design-artifacts`) and align `layout.js` with `router.dart`'s owner count.
- ~~**Copying a parent id is tested only for a parent inside the layout.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-02 (spec-10-30-closing-cause-chain-copy-id-keyboard, commit 8dcbdfad): tests for a parent outside the layout and a stale working copy. Low.

## Surfaced 2026-11-04 in the jj 0.46.0 → 0.47.0 pin bump (console session)

_The bump ported every CLI change that needs no API change (root-merge refusal removed, `reset_head` staging area, push export/`some_pushed`, fetch expansion per remote, fetch description `", "`, `workspace add` order/attribution, capped "Did you mean" hints, builtin diff-editor tree merge, push validator counts remote tags, `workspace remove` / snapshot-config order / `export git refs` / sign-on-push op). These need a public API / FFI decision._

- ~~**`jj config gc` also collects workspace configs since 0.47.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-04 (spec-11-23-jj-0-47-follow-ups, commit b068f068): gc also scans `<cfg>/jj/workspaces`; `MissingRepoConfig.kind` (Repo/Workspace) through schema, FFI and zanshin_core; the CLI's error text. Medium.
  - CLI `cli/src/commands/config/gc.rs:43-112` @ v0.47.0 also scans `<config>/jj/workspaces`; error texts become "No repo configs directory found" / "No workspace configs directory found". Zanshin `crates/zanshin-jj/src/config_cmd.rs` (`gc_root`, `find_missing_repo_configs`) only knows repo configs.
  - **Proposal:** a `kind` (repo / workspace) on `MissingRepoConfig` through FFI + Dart; or leave it (workspace configs stay until the CLI's gc runs).
  - **Follow-up:** Story 11.23 item 1 (Ralph, 2026-11-04: like the CLI, `kind` field).
- ~~**`jj file search` handles binary files since 0.47.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-04 (spec-11-23-jj-0-47-follow-ups, commit b068f068): `binary` = Detect/AsText/Skip, a binary match (or a conflict with a binary side) is one path-only match with `binary: true`, through Rust, FFI and Dart. Medium.
  - CLI `file/search.rs:72-79,138-181` @ v0.47.0: `--binary-as-text` / `--skip-binary`; a binary match (NUL in the first 8000 bytes, or any conflict side) yields only "Binary file matches: <path>". Zanshin `crates/zanshin-jj/src/file.rs` `file_search` returns lines.
  - **Proposal:** a tri-state `binary` mode parameter + a `binary` flag on the match, through FFI; or leave it.
  - **Follow-up:** Story 11.23 item 2 (Ralph, 2026-11-04: tri-state + flag).
- ~~**Dead wire values after 0.47.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-11-04 (spec-11-23-jj-0-47-follow-ups, commit b068f068): the three values removed end to end; the schema keeps their numbers as gaps so the remaining values stay put. Low.
  - `PushRefStatus::Unexported` and `WarningKind::PushBookmarksNotUpdatedLocally` (`jj.push_bookmarks_not_updated_locally`) are never produced (jj-lib's push no longer exports to Git); `RevsetErrorKind::NotPrefixOperator` never (jj dropped `:x`). Kept so FlatBuffers enum values stay put; the push sheet / l10n still handle them.
  - **Proposal:** remove them end to end (schema, Rust, Dart, l10n) in a cleanup, or keep them as deprecated.
  - **Follow-up:** Story 11.23 item 3 (Ralph, 2026-11-04: remove end to end).
- **`zanshin_git_remote_changes` no longer refuses a filter that cannot become a refspec.** Low.
  - jj-lib 0.47 made `expand_fetch_refspecs` private (only `GitFetch::fetch` expands); the poll now just matches, the next fetch refuses. Porting the ~80-line private expansion check was not worth it for a non-CLI poll.
  - **Proposal:** leave it.
  - **Follow-up:** none — Ralph, 2026-11-04: leave it. Record.
- **`resolve`'s "too many sides" hint changed and `:editor` resolves any number of sides (0.47).** Low.
  - `merge_tools/mod.rs`, `command_error.rs:625-629` @ v0.47.0; Zanshin's `ConflictNotResolvable` carries no hint (predates 0.47).
  - **Proposal:** decide with the resolve UI whether `ResolveTool::Files` should lift the two-sides limit like `:editor`.
- **Rust 1.99 lint `assert_is_empty` allowed workspace-wide** (`Cargo.toml` `[workspace.lints.clippy]`) — its fix for ~270 test asserts is `assert_eq!(x, [] as [T; 0])`. Revert if preferred.
- **`spikes/graph-layout-ab/rust-inputs` not rebuilt** (own `Cargo.lock`, path deps on zanshin-jj, still `chrono`); outside the gate.

Unexercised: the native View menu's ⌘+ (`CharacterActivator('+')` in `app/lib/src/features/shell/app_menu.dart`); engine `setConstraints`/`setSize` growing windows on a zoom change (`app/lib/src/features/window/app_windows.dart` `zoom` setter); the removed native `minSize` (`app/macos/Runner/MainWindowChrome.swift`); a restored frame grown to the zoomed minimum (`WindowFrameStore.swift` `.full` restore, which assumes `contentMinSize` is set before `restore` runs). Widget tests cover the Dart side only.
  - **Follow-up:** run the app; switch Density in Settings and compare one window per density with the DS cards; step Zoom 90→150 with ⌘+ ⌘= ⌘− ⌘0; check the windows grow and shrink, the traffic lights and the bar, and menus/drags at 125 %; relaunch at 150 % with a frame saved at 100 %.
